\renewcommand{\accession}{VI}
\title{\textbf{\Huge Wallet Guide}\\[6pt]\large The Zcash wallet layer: keys, transactions, and light-client synchronisation}
\author{m@rek.onl}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
This volume of \emph{The Zcash Arboretum} states the protocols that a Zcash
wallet runs: the derivation of its keys and addresses from one seed; the
discovery of its notes from the chain through an untrusted light-client
server, and what that server learns; and the construction, authorisation and
submission of its payments. Each protocol is stated as a definition,
construction or rule, and its correctness, security or privacy guarantee as
a lemma, proposition or theorem under named assumptions. The subject is the
wallet of a user of the Orchard protocol who holds funds in the Ironwood
pool, the sealed Orchard pool, the Sapling pool and the transparent pool;
the light-client service is an abstract query and response interface, and
no implementation is described. The volume assumes the \emph{Math Guide},
the \emph{Crypto Guide}, the \emph{Halo 2 Guide} and the \emph{Consensus
Guide}, and cites the \emph{Ironwood Guide} for every construction of the
shielded protocol. It is non-normative: the Zcash Protocol Specification and
the Zcash Improvement Proposals are authoritative.
\end{abstract}
\clearpage
\tableofcontents
\clearpage

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}

This section fixes the subject of the volume, its status and the vocabulary
that every later section uses.

\subsection{Scope and status}\label{sec:intro-scope}

This volume states the protocols by which a wallet, in the sense of
``Parties, pools, and classifications'' (\S\ref{sec:intro-model}), performs
three tasks. It derives and holds keys and addresses: hierarchical key
derivation (ZIP 32), and unified addresses and unified viewing keys
(ZIP 316). It discovers and tracks its notes: the compact-block format
(ZIP 307) as an abstract message format, trial decryption, nullifier
tracking, the retention of the note commitment tree information that its
witnesses need, and what an untrusted light-client server learns. It pays:
anchors and confirmation depths (ZIP 315), expiry (ZIP 203), the
conventional fee (ZIP 317), transaction construction (note selection,
change, dummy Actions), partially created transactions (ZIP 374), the
transaction lifecycle from submission to a terminal state, and payment
requests (ZIP 321).

The subject is the wallet of a user of the Orchard protocol who holds funds
in the Ironwood pool, the sealed Orchard pool, the Sapling pool and the
transparent pool. Sapling constructions and transparent scripts are cited to
the protocol specification and to the BIPs where a wallet protocol needs
them, and are never constructed; constructions of the shielded protocol are
cited to the \emph{Ironwood Guide} by section title, and are never
re-derived. The light-client service is an abstract query and response
interface, and tree storage is the information that a wallet retains; no
implementation is described. The volume is non-normative: the protocol
specification and the ZIPs are authoritative.

The volume describes the protocols as specified for NU7, whose deployment
ZIP, ZIP 259, has status Draft.

ZIP 307 specifies the compact format for the Sapling pool only: a compact
block of height, block hash and transactions, and a compact transaction of
index, transaction identifier, and compact Sapling spends and outputs. The
parent hash, the tree sizes, the compact Actions of both Orchard-protocol
pools, the transparent part and pool selection are designed but unspecified
in the sense of the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}, Definition ``Epistemic classes'' (``Scope and status''), and the
volume states them abstractly in ``Compact blocks (ZIP 307)''
(\S\ref{sec:sync-compact}).

Rules of the Ironwood pool and of NU6.3 are cited to the NU6.3 edition of
the protocol specification, labelled ``NU6.3 proposal'', whose section
numbers equal those of the default edition; the text on
$\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ for protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key
Components'', is in no edition and is cited to ZIP 2005, ``Changes to the
Protocol Specification'', directly.

Table~\ref{tab:intro-zips} gives the status, and only the status, of each
ZIP that the volume cites. Every later citation of these ZIPs relies on the
table for status. ZIP 258 activates ZIP 2005 with NU6.3 (ZIP 258, ``ZIP 2005
activation'').

\subsection{Parties, pools, and classifications}\label{sec:intro-model}

A \emph{wallet} is a party that holds a seed and the keys derived from it,
and no copy of the chain. A \emph{light-client server} is a party that
answers the queries of a wallet from its own view of the best chain, the
best valid block chain of the \emph{Consensus Guide}, ``Work, and the best
chain'' (Definition ``Best valid block chain''). The \emph{network} is the
set of the nodes that relay transactions and blocks and of the miners that
produce blocks. The trust placed in the server is not fixed here; it is an
assumption of ``Architecture and authority'' (\S\ref{sec:service-trust}).

A wallet handles funds in four value pools. The first is the
\emph{transparent pool}. The second is the \emph{Sapling pool} (protocol
specification, \S``Chain Value Pool Balances''), whose constructions are
cited, never constructed. The third and fourth are the \emph{Ironwood pool}
and the sealed \emph{Orchard pool}, the two value pools of the Orchard
protocol (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, ``The Orchard protocol and its two pools'',
Definitions ``Orchard protocol'' and ``Orchard pool, Ironwood pool''; ZIP 229,
Terminology); each has its own note commitment tree, its own nullifier set
and its own chain value pool balance. The rules that seal the Orchard pool
are cited there (ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules from NU6.3 activation''), and
are stated for wallets in ``Payments to pools''
(\S\ref{sec:pipeline-routing}).

Heights are counted as in the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, ``Scope and status''
(Definition ``Block chain, transaction, consensus rules''), the genesis block
having height~$0$. The \emph{tip} $T$ is the height of the last block of the
wallet's view of the best chain. Every other height (the target height, the
anchor height, the expiry height) is defined where it is first used.

\begin{definition}[Classes of claim]\label{def:intro-classes}
The classes \emph{specified} and \emph{designed but unspecified} are those
of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Epistemic classes'' (``Scope and
status''), applied here to wallet rules as well as to claims about the
deployed protocol. A wallet rule is \emph{specified} when normative text of
a ZIP or of the protocol specification fixes it, the status of the ZIP being
that of Table~\ref{tab:intro-zips}. A wallet rule is \emph{designed but
unspecified} when wallets are designed to follow it and no normative text
fixes it. This volume adds a third class: a question is an \emph{open
problem} when neither a ZIP nor a stated design settles it.
\end{definition}

Every design-stage statement of the volume carries one class of
Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}, and every deployed rule is cited at its
use to the section of the protocol specification or of the ZIP that fixes
it. A specified rule keeps the normative level of its source (MUST, SHOULD,
MAY).

\section{Hierarchical key derivation (ZIP 32)}\label{sec:keys-zip32}

This section derives every key of a wallet from one seed. It fixes the seed
and its fingerprint, the hardened-only derivation of Orchard spending keys
and the standard account path, and the passage from an account spending key
to the key components that the \emph{Ironwood Guide} constructs from a
spending key, including the alternative branch of ZIP 2005. It then
constructs the internal keys of an account and proves the separations
between the two scopes, bounds the probability that an account key is
invalid, and closes with the domains of key derivation.

\subsection{Wallet seeds}\label{sec:zip32-seeds}

\begin{definition}[Wallet seed]\label{def:zip32-seed}
A \emph{wallet seed} is a byte string $S$ with
$32 \le \mathrm{length}(S) \le 252$, where $\mathrm{length}(S)$ is its
length in bytes (MUST). The method that generates $S$ MUST give it at least
$256$ bits of entropy; the requirement extends to any mnemonic or input
randomness from which $S$ is obtained (ZIP 32, ``Specification: Wallet
seeds''). Every key of every account of the wallet is derived from $S$: the
account spending key in \S\ref{sec:zip32-path}, and what recovery needs
beyond $S$ in \S\ref{sec:zip32-account-keys}.
\end{definition}

Every key of every account is a deterministic function of $S$, so a seed
space of lower entropy is searched once for all accounts, and across many
wallets as a multi-target search that matches the first derived addresses
or viewing keys against a set of observed ones (ZIP 32, ``Rationale for the
256-bit entropy requirement'').

\begin{construction}[Seed fingerprint]\label{def:zip32-seedfp}
For a wallet seed $S$, the \emph{seed fingerprint} is
\[
  \mathsf{SeedFP}(S) := \mathrm{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}\bigl(
    \texttt{Zcash\_HD\_Seed\_FP},\
    [\mathrm{length}(S)] \,\|\, S\bigr),
\]
unkeyed BLAKE2b with a $32$-byte output and the $16$-byte personalisation
shown, over the single length byte of $S$ followed by $S$; the length fits
one byte because $\mathrm{length}(S) \le 252$. It MAY be used to identify
the seed of a hierarchical deterministic wallet. Its string form is
Bech32m (BIP 350) with human-readable part \texttt{zip32seedfp} and the $32$
fingerprint bytes as data; no short tag is defined (ZIP 32, ``Seed
Fingerprints''). A partially created transaction carries the fingerprint
together with a derivation path, so that a signing party locates its key
(\S\ref{sec:pczt-fields}).
\end{construction}

A partially created transaction relies on collision resistance of
BLAKE2b-256 under this personalisation (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Security
notions: preimage, second-preimage, and collision resistance'', Definition
``Collision resistance''); Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} additionally models it
as a random oracle, so that the fingerprint reveals nothing of the keys. The
fingerprint of a full viewing key is constructed with the key
components, in \S\ref{sec:zip32-account-keys}.

\subsection{Hardened-only derivation}\label{sec:zip32-hardened}

ZIP 32 instantiates its hardened-only derivation for Orchard with the
master-key domain $\texttt{ZcashIP32Orchard}$, a $16$-byte BLAKE2b
personalisation that SHOULD be disjoint from every other BLAKE2b domain
separator of Zcash, and the child-key domain $\mathtt{0x81}$, a lead byte
of $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$ (ZIP 32, ``Specification: Hardened-only key
derivation'', ``Instantiation'', and ``Specification: Orchard key
derivation''). An \emph{Orchard extended spending key} is a pair
$(\mathsf{sk}, \mathsf{c})$ of $32$-byte strings, where $\mathsf{sk}$ is a
spending key in the sense of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The spending
key and the spend-side secrets'', and $\mathsf{c}$ is the \emph{chain code}
(ZIP 32, ``Orchard extended keys''). Only hardened derivation is defined for
Orchard: child derivation fails at every index $i < 2^{31}$. ZIP 32 defines
no extended full viewing key for Orchard; a full viewing key has no
child-derivation capability for which a chain code would serve. For
$0 \le i < 2^{31}$ write $i' := i + 2^{31}$.

\begin{construction}[Orchard master key generation]\label{def:zip32-mkg}
For a wallet seed $S$ (Definition~\ref{def:zip32-seed}), let
\[
  I := \mathrm{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(\texttt{ZcashIP32Orchard},\, S),
\]
unkeyed BLAKE2b with a $64$-byte output, and let
$\mathsf{sk}_m := I[0..32)$ and $\mathsf{c}_m := I[32..64)$, the first and
the last $32$ bytes. The \emph{master extended spending key} is
$m_{\mathsf{Orchard}} := (\mathsf{sk}_m, \mathsf{c}_m) =: \mathsf{MKG}(S)$
(ZIP 32, ``Hardened-only master key generation'' and ``Orchard master key
generation'').
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Orchard child key derivation]\label{def:zip32-ckd}
For a parent extended spending key $(\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{par}},
\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{par}})$ and an index $i \in \{0, \dots, 2^{32} - 1\}$:
if $i < 2^{31}$, derivation fails; otherwise
\[
  I := \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{par}}}\bigl(
    [\mathtt{0x81}] \,\|\, \mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{par}} \,\|\,
    \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i)\bigr),
\]
a $64$-byte string, and
$\mathsf{CKD}\bigl((\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{par}}, \mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{par}}),
i\bigr) := (I[0..32), I[32..64))$. Here $\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i)$ is the
$4$-byte little-endian encoding of $i$, and $\mathsf{PRFexpand}_k(t) =
\mathrm{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(\texttt{Zcash\_ExpandSeed}, k \,\|\, t)$ is the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions'',
Construction ``Expansion function'' (ZIP 32, ``Hardened-only child key
derivation'' and ``Orchard child key derivation''). The parent chain code is
the key of the expansion; the parent spending key is message data.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Pseudorandomness of derived spending keys]\label{lem:zip32-prf}
Assume the following.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item BLAKE2b-512 under the personalisation \texttt{ZcashIP32Orchard} and
  BLAKE2b-256 under the personalisation \texttt{Zcash\_HD\_Seed\_FP} are
  modelled as random oracles (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The
  random oracle model'', Definition ``Random oracle''), independent of each
  other and of every other personalised use of BLAKE2b (\emph{Crypto Guide},
  \S``Domain separation and personalisation'', Proposition ``Domain
  separation yields independent oracles''), and of every other derivation
  from $S$ that is modelled as a random oracle.
\item The seed $S$ has min-entropy at least $256$ bits, the reading of the
  entropy requirement of Definition~\ref{def:zip32-seed} that the lemma
  uses.
\item The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Pseudorandomness of PRF
  expansion'' (\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions'') holds.
\end{enumerate}
Let $N$ be a fixed finite set of distinct nodes of the Orchard derivation
tree rooted at $m_{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, each reached by
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-ckd} along hardened indices, and let $T$ be the
subtree spanned by the paths from the root to the nodes of $N$. Then the
spending keys of the nodes of $N$, the chain codes of those nodes of $N$
that are not a proper ancestor of another node of $N$, and
$\mathsf{SeedFP}(S)$ are jointly computationally indistinguishable from
independent uniform $32$-byte strings. For an adversary making $q$
random-oracle queries, the distinguishing advantage is at most
$q \cdot 2^{-256}$ plus one PRF advantage for each node of $T$ whose chain
code keys a derivation in $T$; there are at most as many such nodes as $T$
has edges.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
A hybrid argument (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The hybrid argument'', Lemma
``Hybrid lemma''). Game~$0$ is the real experiment. Game~$1$ answers every
oracle query whose input is determined by $S$ (the master query of
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-mkg}, the input of $\mathsf{SeedFP}$ and the
inputs of the other modelled derivations) by fresh uniform strings,
independent of $S$. The two games differ only if the adversary itself
queries such an input. In Game~$1$ its view is independent of $S$ until
that happens, so each of its $q$ queries does so with probability at most
$2^{-256}$ by hypothesis~(ii), and the games differ by at most
$q \cdot 2^{-256}$. In Game~$1$ the pair $(\mathsf{sk}_m, \mathsf{c}_m)$
and $\mathsf{SeedFP}(S)$ are independent uniform strings.

Then visit the nodes of $T$ that have a child in $T$, each parent before
its children. At such a node $v$ the chain code $\mathsf{c}_v$ is, by the
earlier hops, a uniform string that is used only as a $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$
key and is not released, because $v$ is a proper ancestor of a node of $N$.
The hop replaces $\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{c}_v}$ by a uniformly random
function. The children of $v$ in $T$ have distinct indices, hence distinct
inputs $[\mathtt{0x81}] \,\|\, \mathsf{sk}_v \,\|\,
\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i)$, so their $64$-byte outputs become fresh uniform
strings (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Pseudorandom functions and field
reductions'', Lemma ``Independence of domain-separated expansions'', first
conclusion, applied to a uniform key; its statistical-distance clause is not
used). The hop costs the PRF advantage of a reduction that samples every other
node itself and forwards the queries of $v$ to its oracle. In the last game
every released value is a fresh uniform string; the chain codes of proper
ancestors, which are not fresh relative to their children, are exactly those the
lemma does not release.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The spending key as a leaf capability]\label{rem:zip32-leaf}
In Construction~\ref{def:zip32-ckd} the parent chain code keys the
expansion and the parent spending key is message data, so deriving children
requires the pair $(\mathsf{sk}, \mathsf{c})$. A $32$-byte spending key alone
derives no child: by Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf}, without $\mathsf{c}$ the
children are pseudorandom (ZIP 32, ``Orchard child key derivation''). A
wallet that keeps $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ and not
$\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ keeps no power to derive keys below the
account.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The standard account path}\label{sec:zip32-path}

% compute/zip32.py:
% [sec:zip32-path] purpose 32' = index 0x80000020 = 2^31 + 32; coin_type 133 (Mainnet),
%   1 (Testnet); account a in {0, ..., 2^31 - 1}
\begin{definition}[Account spending key]\label{def:zip32-account}
For a wallet seed $S$, a coin type $t$ and an account index
$a \in \{0, \dots, 2^{31} - 1\}$,
\[
  (\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}, \mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{acct}}) :=
  \mathsf{CKD}\bigl(\mathsf{CKD}\bigl(\mathsf{CKD}\bigl(\mathsf{MKG}(S),
    32'\bigr), t'\bigr), a'\bigr),
\]
the path $m_{\mathsf{Orchard}} / \mathit{purpose}' / \mathit{coin\_type}' /
\mathit{account}'$. The purpose index is $32' = \mathtt{0x80000020}$,
following BIP 43; the coin type is the SLIP 44 value, $133$ on Mainnet and
$1$ on Testnet, every test network sharing the value $1$; accounts are
numbered sequentially from $0$, as in BIP 44. All three levels are
hardened. The \emph{account spending key} is $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$;
no construction of this volume uses $\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{acct}}$
(Remark~\ref{rem:zip32-leaf}). A wallet implementing Orchard ZIP 32
derivation MUST support this path for every account in
$\{0, \dots, 2^{31} - 1\}$, MUST support generating the default payment
address, at diversifier index $0$, of every account it supports, and MAY
generate further diversified addresses of an account (ZIP 32, ``Key path
levels'' and ``Orchard key path'').
\end{definition}

The path ends at the account. Addresses are not path children: an account
has $2^{88}$ diversified addresses per scope, the images of the diversifier
indices (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Diversified addresses'', Construction
``Diversified address''). There is no change level. A shielded output does
not show its address on chain, so returning change to the originating
address is indistinguishable from using a change address; the wallet
separates change from received funds by the internal scope below the
account (\S\ref{sec:zip32-internal}; ZIP 32, ``Key path levels'').
Figure~\ref{fig:keypath} shows the derivation from the seed to the
addresses of both scopes.

The last spending key is $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$, and it is valid in

\subsection{Orchard key components of an account}%
\label{sec:zip32-account-keys}

The \emph{Ironwood Guide} constructs from a spending key $\mathsf{sk}$ the
components $\ask$, $\nksym$ and $\rivk$ (\S``The spending key and the
spend-side secrets''), the spend validating key $\aksym^{\PP}$ and its
coordinate $\aksym$ (same subsection), the full viewing key
$\fvk = (\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$, the incoming viewing key scalar
$\ivk = \CommitIvk_{\rivk}(\aksym, \nksym)$, and $(\dk, \ovk)$ as the two
halves of
\[
  \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathrm{LE}_{256}(\rivk)}\bigl([\mathtt{0x82}] \,\|\,
    \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym) \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym)
  \bigr)
\]
(\S``Viewing keys''), and the diversified addresses (\S``Diversified
addresses''). These constructions are the $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
\mathsf{false}$ branch (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The spending key and the
spend-side secrets'', Remark ``Scope of the key derivations''; protocol
specification, \S``Orchard Key Components''). They are cited, not restated; this
subsection supplies $\mathsf{sk} := \mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ and the
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$ branch.

\begin{lemma}[Account keys as uniform spending keys]\label{lem:zip32-uniform}
Let $R$ be a result of the \emph{Ironwood Guide} about a key generated with
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, stated as a bound on the probability
of a win event $W$ in a game $G$ whose challenger receives the output
$\mathsf{sk}$ of key generation (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The spending key and
the spend-side secrets'', Definition ``Spending key and key generation''), runs
in time polynomial given $\mathsf{sk}$, and decides $W$. Let $G_{\mathsf{acct}}$
be $G$ with $\mathsf{sk} := \mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ at a fixed account
path, the adversary $\Adv$ also receiving $\mathsf{SeedFP}(S)$ and the spending
keys at a fixed finite set of other account paths, and let $V$ be the event that
$\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ passes the discard conditions of that Definition
(made exact in \S\ref{sec:zip32-valid}). Let $\mathcal{B}$ be the adversary of
$G$ that samples those extra inputs as independent uniform $32$-byte strings,
answers the random-oracle queries of $\Adv$ by lazy sampling, and runs $\Adv$.
Then
\[
  \bigl|\Pr[W \text{ in } G_{\mathsf{acct}} \mid V] -
    \Pr[\mathcal{B} \text{ wins } G]\bigr|
  \le \frac{2\delta}{1 - \rho - \delta},
\]
where $\delta$ is the advantage bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} for the
distinguisher of the proof and $\rho$ is the probability that a single draw
from a uniform $\mathsf{sk}$ is discarded, negligible by the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Lemma ``Rejection in key generation'' (\S``Viewing keys''); the bound
assumes
$\rho + \delta < 1$. Every key-side result of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}
therefore holds for $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ up to this loss.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The key $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ is only computationally close to
uniform, so the statistical-distance clause of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Lemma ``Independence of domain-separated expansions'' (\S``Pseudorandom
functions and field reductions'') does not apply; the proof is a reduction. Both
$V$ and $W$ are decided efficiently by the challenger. A distinguisher for
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} receives either the real values
($\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$, the other spending keys and
$\mathsf{SeedFP}(S)$) or independent uniform strings, runs the challenger and
$\Adv$ on them, and outputs $1$ exactly on $W \wedge V$; a second distinguisher
outputs $1$ exactly on $V$. Write $a := \Pr[W \wedge V]$ and $v := \Pr[V]$ for
the real values, and $a_u$, $v_u$ for the uniform ones. Then $|a - a_u| \le
\delta$ and $|v - v_u| \le \delta$, with $v_u = 1 - \rho$. With uniform inputs,
the extra inputs are independent of the key and distributed as $\mathcal{B}$
samples them. Key generation outputs a single uniform draw conditioned on
acceptance, so $\Pr[\mathcal{B} \text{ wins } G] = a_u / v_u$. Hence
\[
  \Bigl|\frac{a}{v} - \frac{a_u}{v_u}\Bigr|
  \le \frac{|a - a_u|}{v} + \frac{a_u}{v_u} \cdot \frac{|v_u - v|}{v}
  \le \frac{2\delta}{v} \le \frac{2\delta}{1 - \rho - \delta},
\]
using $a_u \le v_u$ and $v \ge v_u - \delta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Key components under use\_qsk]\label{def:zip32-useqsk}
The flag $\mathsf{use\_qsk} \in \{\mathsf{false}, \mathsf{true}\}$ is chosen
at key generation. For a spending key $\mathsf{sk}$, in both branches
\[
  \nksym := \mathrm{ToBase}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{sk}}
    ([\mathtt{0x07}])\bigr).
\]
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$: $\ask$, $\aksym^{\PP}$,
  $\aksym$ and $\rivk$ are those of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, and
  $\mathsf{qsk} = \mathsf{qk} = \bot$.
\item If $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$: a spend validating key
  $\aksym^{\PP} \in \Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \setminus \{\mathcal{O}\}$ with
  even $y$-coordinate, that is, with bit $255$ of its star encoding equal to
  $0$, is an input, obtained by a method outside this construction, for
  example a directly generated $\ask$ with
  $\aksym^{\PP} = [\ask]\,G^{\mathsf{SpendAuth}}$, or FROST distributed key
  generation. Then
  \begin{align*}
    \aksym &:= \Extract_{\PP}(\aksym^{\PP}), \\
    \mathsf{qsk} &:= \mathsf{truncate}_{32}\bigl(
      \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{sk}}([\mathtt{0x0C}])\bigr), \\
    \mathsf{qk} &:= \mathsf{BLAKE3.derive\_key}\bigl(
      \texttt{Zcash ZIP 2005 qk-derivation v1},\ \mathsf{qsk},\ 32\bigr), \\
    \rivk &:= \mathrm{ToScalar}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{qk}}\bigl(
      [\mathtt{0x0D}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym) \,\|\,
      \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym)\bigr)\bigr),
  \end{align*}
  where $\mathsf{truncate}_{32}$ keeps the first $32$ bytes and
  \[
    \mathsf{BLAKE3.derive\_key}(\mathit{context}, \mathit{material},
    \mathit{length})
  \]
  is the function that ZIP 2005 specifies, cited to it and
  not constructed; no property of it is assumed.
\end{enumerate}
The components $\ivk$, $\dk$, $\ovk$ and every address are computed from
$(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$ by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s constructions in
both branches, so they depend on $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ (ZIP 2005, ``Changes to
the Protocol Specification'', \S 4.2.3 ``Orchard Key Components'', and
``Usage with FROST''). No edition of the protocol specification carries
this branch.

Wallet rules. A wallet MUST generate every key of one ZIP 32 account with
the same value of $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ (ZIP 326, ``Wallet key-generation
restrictions''). The rule of the same section on Orchard-pool funds and
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$ keys is stated once, with routing
(\S\ref{sec:pipeline-routing}). The value of $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ is not part
of the recovery information, so a wallet restoring from a seed scans with
both values (ZIP 326, ``Scanning when restoring from seed'';
\S\ref{sec:scan-keys}).

Class (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}): how an account whose keys are
to be recoverable from the seed with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$
derives $\aksym^{\PP}$ from $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ is fixed by no
ZIP; it is designed but unspecified.
\end{construction}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the key results]\label{rem:zip32-qsk-scope}
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform} and every later key-side result of this
volume (separations, hiding, validity bounds) is claimed for
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$ only. For $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
\mathsf{true}$ the trapdoor $\rivk$ is an expansion keyed by $\mathsf{qk}$,
and a result would need BLAKE3 in its key-derivation mode modelled as a
PRF, which is not assumed, and a hypothesis on the source of
$\aksym^{\PP}$. Constructive statements, which state what is computed from
what, hold in both branches.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Key recoverability]\label{prop:zip32-recover}
For $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, every spending, full viewing,
incoming viewing and outgoing viewing key and every address of an account
is a deterministic function of the seed, the coin type, the account index,
the scope and the diversifier index; the seed with the account index
recovers all of them. For $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$ the same holds
of $\nksym$, $\mathsf{qsk}$ and $\mathsf{qk}$, and, given $\aksym$, of
$\rivk$, $\ivk$, $\dk$, $\ovk$ and every address; recovery needs $\aksym$ in
addition, and, to spend, the key material that signs under $\aksym$, held
collectively by the signers under FROST. In both cases the wallet must know
the account's value of $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ or try both. The function is
partial: it is undefined at an account index whose key is invalid
(\S\ref{sec:zip32-valid}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Composition of Constructions~\ref{def:zip32-mkg} and~\ref{def:zip32-ckd}
and Definition~\ref{def:zip32-account}, which map the seed to
$\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$; of Construction~\ref{def:zip32-useqsk},
which maps $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ and $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$, together
with $\aksym^{\PP}$ when $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$, to
$(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$; and of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s constructions
from $(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$ (\S``Viewing keys'' and \S``Diversified
addresses''). The keys of the internal scope are a function of $\fvk$
(\S\ref{sec:zip32-internal}), and the composition only adds that function.
ZIP 2005, ``Changes to the Protocol Specification'', \S 4.2.3 ``Orchard Key
Components'', notes, and ``Usage with FROST'' state the same requirements
for recovery.
\end{proof}

% compute/zip32.py:
% [sec:zip32-account-keys] FVK fingerprint = BLAKE2b-256('ZcashOrchardFVFP',
%   I2LEOSP_256(ak) || I2LEOSP_256(nk) || I2LEOSP_256(rivk)); input 96 bytes;
%   tag = first 4 bytes; master key's parent tag = 00000000
\begin{construction}[Full viewing key fingerprint and tag]%
\label{def:zip32-fvfp}
For a full viewing key $(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$ of either branch,
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{FVFP}(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk) := \mathrm{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}\bigl(
    &\texttt{ZcashOrchardFVFP}, \\
    &\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym) \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym)
    \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rivk)\bigr),
\end{align*}
over the $96$-byte string shown, which is the raw encoding of an Orchard
full viewing key (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Raw Full Viewing
Keys''). It MAY be used to identify the key uniquely; its uses rely on collision
resistance, cited as for Construction~\ref{def:zip32-seedfp};
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-domains} models it as a random oracle. The \emph{tag} is
$\mathsf{FVFP}[0..4)$, its first $4$ bytes; it serves key lookup and MUST NOT be
assumed unique, and nothing is claimed of it (ZIP 32, ``Orchard Full Viewing Key
Fingerprints and Tags''). In ZIP 32's encoding of an extended spending key, a
key names its parent by this tag, the master key's parent tag being four zero
bytes (ZIP 32, ``Orchard extended spending keys'').
\end{construction}

\subsection{Internal keys: change and auto-shielding}%
\label{sec:zip32-internal}

With no change level, the separation between externally visible receipt and
wallet-internal operations runs below the account. The internal operations
are change and \emph{auto-shielding}, the automatic transfer of received
transparent funds to the most recent shielded pool (ZIP 315,
``Terminology''; its rules in \S\ref{sec:pipeline-tex}). The derivation
applies to keys at the account level (ZIP 32, ``Orchard internal key
derivation'').

\begin{construction}[Internal full viewing key]\label{def:zip32-internal}
For a full viewing key $\fvk = (\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$ let
$K := \mathrm{LE}_{256}(\rivk)$, written $\mathsf{I2LEBSP}_{256}(\rivk)$ in
ZIP 32, and
\[
  \rivk_{\mathsf{int}} := \mathrm{ToScalar}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_K\bigl(
    [\mathtt{0x83}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym) \,\|\,
    \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym)\bigr)\bigr) \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}.
\]
The \emph{internal full viewing key} is $\fvk_{\mathsf{int}} :=
(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk_{\mathsf{int}})$. The internal keys are
$\ivk_{\mathsf{int}} := \CommitIvk_{\rivk_{\mathsf{int}}}(\aksym, \nksym)$
and $(\dk_{\mathsf{int}}, \ovk_{\mathsf{int}})$, the two halves of the
$\mathtt{0x82}$ expansion keyed by $\mathrm{LE}_{256}(\rivk_{\mathsf{int}})$
over the same message. The input of the $\mathtt{0x83}$ expansion has the
key and message body of the $\mathtt{0x82}$ expansion and another lead
byte. The internal expanded spending key is
$(\ask, \nksym, \rivk_{\mathsf{int}})$; no $32$-byte internal spending key
exists (ZIP 32, ``Orchard internal key derivation''; protocol
specification, \S``Orchard Key Components'').
\end{construction}

Hence $\aksym_{\mathsf{int}} = \aksym$ and $\nksym_{\mathsf{int}} =
\nksym$, as the protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key Components'',
notes: spend authorisation and nullifier derivation serve both scopes, and
$\ivk$, $\dk$ and $\ovk$ are derived again from $\rivk_{\mathsf{int}}$. The
construction does not force the keys of the two scopes to differ; their
equality is an event of negligible probability
(Proposition~\ref{prop:zip32-scopes}), not an excluded case. For Sapling,
ZIP 32 keeps the spend authorising key and derives again the key that
enters the proof and the nullifier, the outgoing viewing key and the
diversifier key, so the two scopes share spend authority and differ in the
key that enters the proof and the nullifier (ZIP 32, ``Sapling internal key
derivation''); this is cited, not constructed. Payments are sent from an
account and a scope, not from an address: no outgoing viewing key per
diversified address exists (ZIP 32, ``Orchard diversifier and OVK
derivation''; ZIP 316, ``Usage of Outgoing Viewing Keys'').

\begin{proposition}[Capability of a full viewing key]\label{prop:zip32-fvk}
From $\fvk$ alone one computes, for both scopes, $\ivk$, $\dk$, $\ovk$ and
the address at every diversifier index $j \in \{0, \dots, 2^{88} - 1\}$. For
a key with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, no efficient algorithm
given $\fvk$ outputs $\ask$, and no efficient algorithm given the keys of
one account outputs the spending key of another account, except with
negligible probability. Recovering the index and scope of a given address
is stated with the index (\S\ref{sec:addr-indices}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Computation: $\fvk_{\mathsf{int}}$ is a function of $\fvk$
(Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}), and each address is a function of
$(\dk, \ivk)$ and $j$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Diversified addresses'',
Construction ``Diversified address''). No $\ask$: the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Key capabilities and scope'', Proposition ``Capability
separation'', part~(a), under its Assumptions ``Pseudorandomness of PRF
expansion'' and ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'', transferred to
$\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}. No other
account: the spending key of another account lies at a distinct path, and
only hardened derivation exists; by Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} the two keys
are jointly indistinguishable from independent uniform strings, so an
algorithm given every key of this account outputs the other key with
probability at most $2^{-256}$ plus the advantage bound of that lemma.
\end{proof}

\begin{assumption}[Joint hiding of the viewing keys of both scopes]%
\label{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}
For keys with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$: for every
$(\aksym, \nksym) \in \{1, \dots, p_{\Pallas} - 1\} \times \F_{p_{\Pallas}}$,
given to the distinguisher, and $\rivk$ uniform on $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$, the six
keys $(\ivk, \dk, \ovk, \ivk_{\mathsf{int}}, \dk_{\mathsf{int}},
\ovk_{\mathsf{int}})$, computed by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s constructions
and Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}, are computationally
indistinguishable from
\[
  \bigl(\CommitIvk_{r}(\aksym, \nksym),\ u_1,\ u_2,\
    \CommitIvk_{r'}(\aksym, \nksym),\ u_3,\ u_4\bigr),
\]
where $r$ and $r'$ are uniform on $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ and $u_1, \dots, u_4$ are
uniform $32$-byte strings, all independent. It extends the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Assumption ``Joint hiding of the viewing keys'' (\S``Viewing keys''),
which covers the
external triple only, to the $\mathtt{0x83}$ row, whose key
$\mathrm{LE}_{256}(\rivk)$ is also the $\CommitIvk$ trapdoor of the external
$\ivk$ and so lies outside the hypothesis of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Lemma ``Independence of domain-separated expansions'' (\S``Pseudorandom
functions and field reductions'') (Remark ``Hiding of $\ivk$ and the double use
of $\rivk$'' (\S``Viewing keys'')); the PRF assumption does not give it
(protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key Components'', note on $\rivk$ as
trapdoor and key). It is applied after Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}, averaging
over the key-generation distribution of $(\aksym, \nksym)$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Separation of scopes under trial decryption]%
\label{prop:zip32-scopes}
Let an account key have $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, with external
and internal incoming viewing keys outside $\{0, \bot\}$. An output honestly
addressed to an internal-scope address of the account is accepted under the
account's external $\ivk$ only with negligible probability, under the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas''
(\S``Sinsemilla: hash, commitment, and short forms'') and ``GroupHash as a
random oracle'' (\S``GroupHash, domain separation, and nothing-up-my-sleeve
generators''), Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes} and
the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}. Consequently an external
incoming viewing key reveals no change or auto-shielding output. Since
$\fvk_{\mathsf{int}}$ is computable from $\fvk$, a full viewing key reveals
both scopes, and the boundary between the scopes lies at the incoming
viewing tier.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Acceptance under a key $\ivk$ derives the transmission key again as
$[\ivk]\,\gd$ from the decrypted diversifier and checks the note commitment
of the derived note against $\cmx$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Trial
decryption and note acceptance'', Construction ``Acceptance of an
Ironwood-pool output'', check~(iv); for an Orchard-pool output the check of
the protocol specification, \S``Decryption using an Incoming Viewing Key
(Sapling and Orchard)''). The honest output commits to a note $n$ with
transmission key $[\ivk_{\mathsf{int}}]\,\gd$. If the note $n'$ derived
under $\ivk_{\mathsf{ext}}$ differs from $n$, acceptance exhibits two
distinct notes with one extracted commitment, which happens with negligible
probability (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Note commitments'', Proposition ``Hiding
and binding of note commitments'', part~(b)). If $n' = n$, then
$[\ivk_{\mathsf{ext}}]\,\gd = [\ivk_{\mathsf{int}}]\,\gd$; since $\gd \neq
\mathcal{O}$ in a group of prime order and both scalars lie in $\{1, \dots,
p_{\Pallas} - 1\}$ with $p_{\Pallas} < p_{\Vesta}$, this forces
$\ivk_{\mathsf{ext}} = \ivk_{\mathsf{int}}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Viewing
keys''). By Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform} and
Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes} the pair is replaced by
$(\CommitIvk_r(\aksym, \nksym), \CommitIvk_{r'}(\aksym, \nksym))$ with
independent uniform $r$ and $r'$, at the cost of the distinguishing advantage,
since equality is efficiently decidable. By the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma
``Distribution of $\ivk$'' (\S``Viewing keys''), the second value equals any
fixed value with
probability at most $2/p_{\Vesta}$, independently of the first, so the two are
equal with probability at most $2/p_{\Vesta}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Transparent outgoing viewing keys]\label{def:zip32-tovk}
A P2PKH (pay-to-public-key-hash) output pays to the hash of one secp256k1
public key (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The transparent layer's schemes: ECDSA
and BIP-340 over secp256k1''). P2PKH keys have no outgoing viewing key, yet
auto-shielding outputs need one. For the BIP 32 account-level extended
public key $(\mathsf{c}, \mathsf{pk})$ of a transparent account, at path
$m / 44' / \mathit{coin\_type}' / \mathit{account}'$ (BIP 44), let
\[
  I_{\ovk} := \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{c}}\bigl([\mathtt{0xD0}] \,\|\,
    \mathsf{ser}_P(\mathsf{pk})\bigr),
\]
with the chain code $\mathsf{c}$ used as a $32$-byte key and
$\mathsf{ser}_P(\mathsf{pk})$ the $33$-byte compressed encoding of BIP 32;
then $\ovk_{\mathsf{external}} := I_{\ovk}[0..32)$ and
$\ovk_{\mathsf{internal}} := I_{\ovk}[32..64)$ (ZIP 316, ``Deriving
Internal Keys''). The external transparent incoming viewing key is the
extended public key of the non-hardened child $0$ of $(\mathsf{c},
\mathsf{pk})$ (ZIP 316, ``Deriving a UIVK from a UFVK'') and the internal one
that of the non-hardened child $1$, at the change level of BIP 44 (ZIP 316,
``Deriving Internal Keys''), each obtained by BIP 32 public-to-public
derivation, which is HMAC-SHA512 keyed by $\mathsf{c}$
(BIP 32, ``Public parent key $\to$ public child key''; \emph{Crypto Guide},
\S``PRFs from hash functions: length extension and keyed BLAKE2'').
\end{construction}

\begin{assumption}[Hiding of the transparent account key]%
\label{ass:zip32-bip32}
For an honestly generated account-level extended public key
$(\mathsf{c}, \mathsf{pk})$, the extended public keys of its non-hardened
children $0$ and $1$ and the two halves of $I_{\ovk}$ are jointly
computationally indistinguishable from two independently generated extended
public keys, each a uniform $32$-byte chain code with the public key of a
uniform secret key, and two independent uniform $32$-byte strings. The chain
code $\mathsf{c}$ keys both HMAC-SHA512 in BIP 32 non-hardened derivation
and $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$ in the $\mathtt{0xD0}$ row, so this is not an
instance of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Independence of
domain-separated expansions'' (\S``Pseudorandom functions and field
reductions''); it is assumed.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Capability separation of internal keys]%
\label{prop:zip32-capsep}
The following hold, where ``derives'' means outputs the key with
non-negligible probability.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item A holder of a full viewing key derives the external and internal
  incoming viewing keys and the external and internal outgoing viewing
  keys.
\item A holder of the external incoming viewing key derives neither the
  internal incoming viewing key nor any outgoing viewing key.
\item A holder of the external outgoing viewing key derives neither the
  internal outgoing viewing key nor any incoming viewing key.
\end{enumerate}
For Orchard the statements hold for keys with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
\mathsf{false}$ under Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform} and
Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}; for Sapling they are the properties
that ZIP 316, ``Deriving Internal Keys'', states for the construction of
ZIP 32, ``Sapling internal key derivation'', and are cited, not proved; for
transparent P2PKH keys under Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-bip32} (ZIP 316,
``Deriving Internal Keys'').
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i)~Constructive: Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal} and the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s constructions; for transparent keys,
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-tovk} from $(\mathsf{c}, \mathsf{pk})$.

(ii) and (iii), Orchard: by Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform} and
Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes} the six keys are replaced by the ideal
distribution, at the cost of the distinguishing advantage, since success is
decided by comparing the output with the key. In the ideal distribution
every key not computed from the held one is independent of it: either a
uniform $32$-byte string, output with probability $2^{-256}$, or a
$\CommitIvk$ value under an independent trapdoor, output with probability
at most $2/p_{\Vesta}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Viewing keys'', Lemma
``Distribution of $\ivk$''). Transparent: the same argument in the ideal
distribution of Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-bip32}, where an independently
generated extended public key has a uniform chain code, output with probability
$2^{-256}$. Sapling: cited to ZIP 316, ``Deriving Internal Keys'', which asserts
them from the one-wayness of $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$ and
$\mathsf{CRH}^{\mathsf{ivk}}$; not proved here.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Valid account keys}\label{sec:zip32-valid}

\begin{definition}[Valid account key]\label{def:zip32-valid}
For $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, an account spending key
$\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ is \emph{valid} if and only if $\ask \neq 0$,
$\ivk \notin \{0, \bot\}$ and $\ivk_{\mathsf{int}} \notin \{0, \bot\}$. The
first two conditions are discard conditions of the \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``The spending key and the spend-side secrets'', Definition ``Spending key and
key generation''; the third is the specification's condition on the internal key
of Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}, which that Definition names without
constructing (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key Components''; ZIP 32,
``Orchard internal key derivation''). ZIP 2005 amends the derivation for
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$ (``Changes to the Protocol Specification'',
\S 4.2.3), for which this volume states no validity condition. Child
derivation reads only the spending key and chain code of an intermediate
path node (ZIP 32, ``Orchard child key derivation''), so derivation needs
valid keys only where a wallet uses them. The encoding of an extended
spending key does expand its parent node, whose tag it carries
(Construction~\ref{def:zip32-fvfp}), and the \textsf{orchard} crate rejects
a path through any node whose key fails these conditions.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Derivation failure]\label{rem:zip32-failure}
ZIP 32 notes that a child spending key yields an invalid external or
internal full viewing key ``with small probability'' and prescribes no
wallet behaviour (``Orchard child key derivation''). The specification's
instruction to discard the key and repeat with a new $\mathsf{sk}$ applies
to a freshly random key, not to a fixed path, where nothing is drawn again:
an account index whose key is invalid has no key. Class
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}): the wallet's handling of such an
index is an open problem; Proposition~\ref{prop:zip32-invalid} bounds its
probability.
\end{remark}

% compute/zip32.py:
% [sec:zip32-valid] Commit^ivk message I2LEBSP_255(ak) || I2LEBSP_255(nk): 510 bits,
%   51 chunks of 10 bits, 102 incomplete additions (2 per chunk); the message is the same in
%   both scopes (only rivk differs), so the additions are one event set
% [sec:zip32-valid] 5 is a non-square mod p_Pallas: no point has x = 0, so ivk = 0 iff the
%   commitment point is O; the complete addition of [rivk] R never returns bottom
% [sec:zip32-valid] events per account key: ask = 0 (prob ceil(2^512/r_P)/2^512 < 1/r_P +
%   2^-512); ivk = 0 in the external scope and in the internal scope (each with the same
%   probability, rivk and rivk_internal being ToScalar outputs); bottom in one of the 102
%   additions (each a union of 4 linear relations A = O, B = O, A = B, A = -B among the
%   independent uniform generators Q(D), S(0..1023); each is nontrivial, since B = O names
%   one generator and every other relation gives Q(D) the coefficient 2^t or 2^(t+1),
%   nonzero mod r_P; each has probability 1/r_P)
% [sec:zip32-valid] Pr[sk_acct invalid] <= 3 ceil(2^512/r_P)/2^512 + 408/r_P < 412/r_P
%   = 2^-245.31, plus the PRF advantage; constant 411 = 1 + 2 + 4 x 102 < 2^9
% [sec:zip32-valid] union over the keys a wallet uses, 1 account: <= 1 x 412/r_P
%   = 2^-245.31 (+ n x PRF advantage)
% (r_P, the order of the Pallas group, is p_Vesta in this volume's notation.)
\begin{proposition}[Probability of an invalid account key]%
\label{prop:zip32-invalid}
Model the Sinsemilla generators, the points $S(j)$ for $0 \le j < 2^{10}$
and the initial point $Q := Q(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-CommitIvk-M})$, as
independent uniform points of the Pallas group,
independent of every other quantity (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``GroupHash,
domain separation, and nothing-up-my-sleeve generators'', Assumption ``GroupHash
as a random oracle''). Let
\[
  \varepsilon := \frac{3 \lceil 2^{512} / p_{\Vesta} \rceil}{2^{512}}
    + \frac{408}{p_{\Vesta}} < \frac{412}{p_{\Vesta}} = 2^{-245.31}.
\]
For $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$ and a fixed account path,
$\Pr[\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}} \text{ is invalid}] \le \varepsilon +
\delta$, where $\delta$ is the sum of the advantages of efficient
reductions, each deciding validity, against Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf}, the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion''
(\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions'') (rows $\mathtt{0x06}$,
$\mathtt{0x07}$ and $\mathtt{0x08}$) and Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}
(row $\mathtt{0x83}$), plus the statistical distance of $\mathrm{ToScalar}$ from
uniform, below $2^{-257}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Pseudorandom functions and
field reductions''). For the $n$ accounts a wallet uses, $\Pr[\text{some key is
invalid}] \le n\varepsilon + \delta_n$, where $\delta_n$ is the corresponding
sum for the $n$ paths jointly; the union runs over the keys used, not over path
nodes. The bound averages over generators modelled as random and is not a
frequency for the fixed deployed generators.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Replace $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$ by a uniform string
(Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf}); the $512$-bit outputs of rows $\mathtt{0x06}$,
$\mathtt{0x07}$ and $\mathtt{0x08}$ by independent uniform strings (the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Independence of domain-separated
expansions'' (\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions''), first
conclusion); $\rivk$ by a uniform element of $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ (the reduction
distance); and, on the event $\ask \neq 0$, the pair $(\ivk,
\ivk_{\mathsf{int}})$ by $(\CommitIvk_r(\aksym, \nksym), \CommitIvk_{r'}(\aksym,
\nksym))$ with independent uniform $r$ and $r'$
(Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}). Each replacement changes the probability
of invalidity by at most one term of $\delta$, since validity is efficiently
decidable. Then, by the union bound (\emph{Math Guide}, \S``The union bound and
a birthday calculation'', Theorem ``Union bound / Boole's inequality''), the key
is invalid only on one of the following events.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $\ask = 0$. The sign normalisation preserves $0$, so this happens
  exactly when a uniform $512$-bit integer is divisible by $p_{\Vesta}$,
  with probability $\lceil 2^{512}/p_{\Vesta} \rceil / 2^{512}$ (\emph{Math
  Guide}, \S``Uniform sampling and the bias of modular reduction'').
\item The Sinsemilla hash point $\hat{M}$ of the message
  $\mathrm{LE}_{255}(\aksym) \,\|\allowbreak\, \mathrm{LE}_{255}(\nksym)$ is
  $\bot$.
  The message has $510$ bits, $51$ chunks of $10$ bits, so the hash
  performs $102$ incomplete additions, two per round,
  $\Acc_i := (\Acc_{i-1} \dotplus S(m_i)) \dotplus \Acc_{i-1}$ (protocol
  specification, \S``Sinsemilla Hash Function''; \emph{Ironwood Guide},
  \S``Sinsemilla: hash, commitment, and short forms'', Construction
  ``Sinsemilla hash on Pallas''). An incomplete addition $A \dotplus B$ of
  points is exceptional exactly when $A = \mathcal{O}$, $B = \mathcal{O}$,
  $A = B$ or $A = -B$. At the first exceptional addition every accumulator
  so far equals its formal value, an integer combination of $Q$ and the
  $S(j)$, so one of four linear relations among the generators holds. Each
  relation is nontrivial: it either states that the single generator
  $S(m_i)$ is $\mathcal{O}$, or gives $Q$ a coefficient $2^t$ or $2^{t+1}$,
  non-zero modulo the odd prime $p_{\Vesta}$. The message depends only on
  $(\ask, \nksym)$ and $G^{\mathsf{SpendAuth}}$, which are independent of
  $Q$ and the $S(j)$, so each relation holds with probability
  $1/p_{\Vesta}$, and the $102$ additions cost at most $408/p_{\Vesta}$.
  Both scopes commit to the same message, so this event is counted once.
\item $\ivk = 0$ with $\hat{M} \neq \bot$. The addition of
  $[r]\,H_D$ is complete, and $\ivk = 0$ exactly when
  $\hat{M} + [r]\,H_D = \mathcal{O}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Fields, groups,
  and encodings'', Lemma ``Extract vanishes only at the identity''), which,
  since $H_D \neq \mathcal{O}$, holds for a single value of $r$: probability
  $1/p_{\Vesta} \le \lceil 2^{512}/p_{\Vesta} \rceil / 2^{512}$.
\item $\ivk_{\mathsf{int}} = 0$ with $\hat{M} \neq \bot$: likewise, under
  the independent $r'$, with the same bound.
\end{enumerate}
The sum is $\varepsilon$. Negligibility alone is the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Lemma ``Rejection in key generation'' (\S``Viewing keys''). For $n$ accounts,
apply the
replacements to the $n$ paths jointly and the union bound over the $n$
keys; path nodes that are not account keys do not enter the union, since
an account key's validity does not depend on theirs.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\mathsf{PRFexpand}$}{PRFexpand}
  lead-byte registry}%
\label{sec:zip32-registry}

Domain separation of $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$ is per key: under one key,
distinct lead bytes give jointly pseudorandom outputs (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions'', Lemma ``Independence of
domain-separated expansions''), while
expansions under different keys may reuse a byte. Byte conventions: the key
that ZIP 32 writes $\mathsf{I2LEBSP}_{256}(\rivk)$ is
$\mathrm{LE}_{256}(\rivk)$; the bytes of $\aksym$ are
$\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym)$ of the field element
$\aksym = \Extract_{\PP}(\aksym^{\PP})$, which for the sign-normalised
$\aksym^{\PP}$ equals the byte form $\mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{256}$ of its star
encoding, bit $255$ being $0$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The spending key
and the spend-side secrets''); the key $\nksym$ enters as
$\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym)$ (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key
Components'').

Table~\ref{tab:zip32-leadbytes} lists the expansions and personalisations
that this volume constructs. The expansions of the key components and of
notes are the rows of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Domain
bytes of PRF expansion'' (\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions'') and
are not restated. The list of expansion bytes that the protocol
specification maintains, as amended by ZIP 2005, is
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf#abstractprfs}%
{\S``Pseudo Random Functions''} (ZIP 2005, ``Changes to the Protocol
Specification'', \S 4.1.2); bytes this volume does not use are not listed.

\begin{table}[tp]
\centering\small

\begin{tabular}{@{}l p{2.3cm} p{4.4cm} p{3.2cm} l@{}}
\toprule
Byte & Key & Message & Output & Constructed in \\
\midrule

  & $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{par}} \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i)$

  & Constr.~\ref{def:zip32-ckd} \\

\begin{lemma}[Independence of the key-derivation domains]%
\label{lem:zip32-domains}
Under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Pseudorandomness of PRF
expansion'' (\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions''), with BLAKE2b
under distinct personalisations modelled as independent random oracles, the
expansions of rows $\mathtt{0x81}$ and $\mathtt{0x0C}$, whose keys (a chain code
of the Orchard tree, and $\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{acct}}$) are pseudorandom by
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} and enter no function other than $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$,
and the three personalised BLAKE2b functions of Table~\ref{tab:zip32-leadbytes}
are jointly indistinguishable from independent random functions. The lemma
excludes the other rows, each outside the hypothesis of the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Lemma ``Independence of domain-separated expansions'' (\S``Pseudorandom
functions and field reductions''): the row $\mathtt{0x83}$, whose key is also
the $\CommitIvk$ trapdoor, is covered by Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes};
the row $\mathtt{0xD0}$, whose key also keys HMAC-SHA512, by
Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-bip32}; the row $\mathtt{0x0D}$, keyed by the BLAKE3
output $\mathsf{qk}$ of which nothing is assumed
(Remark~\ref{rem:zip32-qsk-scope}), carries no claim.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For each key, the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Independence of
domain-separated expansions'' (\S``Pseudorandom functions and field
reductions''), applied after the hybrid of Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} has made
the keys uniform; this is a reduction, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}, not
the statistical-distance clause of that lemma. For the three personalisations,
the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s Proposition ``Domain separation yields independent
oracles'' (\S``Domain separation and personalisation''): they are pairwise
distinct $16$-byte strings, and the two BLAKE2b-256 uses and the BLAKE2b-512 use
differ also in the output length, a parameter of BLAKE2b. The proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-prf} uses the same two citations directly.
\end{proof}

\section{Addresses and viewing keys (ZIP 316)}\label{sec:addresses}

This section passes from the keys of an account to the addresses and viewing
keys that a wallet hands out. It defines the diversifier index and the
recovery of an index from an address, the raw encodings of receivers and
keys, the unified container with its jumbled string encoding and its
viewing-key form, the unified address at an index of a viewing key, the
extensions of Revision~$2$ of ZIP 316, and the recipient addresses a sender
handles.

\subsection{Diversifier indices}\label{sec:addr-indices}

The address layer meets two requirements.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(R\arabic*)]
\item Each payee receives an address unlinkable to every other address of
  the same holder, while all of them are spendable by one key and
  recoverable from one seed (protocol specification,
  \S``$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ and
  $\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ Hash Functions'', security
  requirements; ZIP 32; ZIP 316, ``Rationale for address derivation''). Each
  shielded protocol meets~(R1) with diversified addresses.
\item Transparent, Sapling and Orchard receivers coexist, each with its own
  format. A recipient publishes one string, from which the sender selects
  the most preferred receiver type it supports, and whose characters a
  sender can check (ZIP 316, ``Motivation'' and ``Requirements''). ZIP 316
  meets~(R2) above the protocols, with unified
  containers (\S\ref{sec:addr-ua}) and their jumbled string encoding
  (\S\ref{sec:addr-string}).
\end{enumerate}
The \emph{Ironwood Guide} constructs the Orchard key tiers, the diversifier
map and the diversified base (\S``Viewing keys'' and \S``Diversified
addresses''). This subsection adds the diversifier index, shared by Sapling
and Orchard and used by transparent derivation.

The Orchard address at index $j$ of an incoming viewing key $(\dk, \ivk)$
is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Diversified addresses'',
Construction ``Diversified address'', cited and not re-derived:
$d := \mathsf{FF1\text{-}AES256}_{\dk}(\mathrm{LE}_{88}(j))$,
$\gd := \mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}(d)$, which is
$\GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-gd}, \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{88}(d))$ unless
that is the identity and the fallback point
$\GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-gd}, \varepsilon)$ otherwise, and
$\pkd := [\ivk]\,\gd$; the address is $(d, \pkd)$. For each scope
$s \in \{\mathsf{ext}, \mathsf{int}\}$ of an account
(Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}) the pair $(\dk_s, \ovk_s)$ is the
first and the second $32$ bytes of
\[
  \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathrm{LE}_{256}(\rivk_s)}([\mathtt{0x82}] \,\|\,
  \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym) \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym))
\]
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Viewing keys''), and $\ivk_s$ is the incoming
viewing key of the scope. The address at index $0$ is the \emph{default
address} of the scope (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key
Components''; ZIP 32, ``Orchard diversifier and OVK derivation'').

\begin{definition}[Diversifier index]\label{def:addr-index}
For a $32$-byte diversifier key $\dk$ and an index
$j \in \{0, \dots, 2^{88} - 1\}$, the \emph{diversifier at index $j$} is
\[
  d_j := \mathsf{FF1\text{-}AES256.Encrypt}(\dk, \texttt{""},
    \mathrm{LE}_{88}(j)) \in \{0,1\}^{88},
\]
the FF1 mode of NIST SP~800-38G over AES-256 with key $\dk$, radix $2$,
length $88$ and the empty tweak (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Format-preserving
encryption and FF1'', Construction ``The FF1 mode of NIST SP~800-38G'';
protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#concreteprps}%
{\S``Pseudo Random Permutations''}). The map is the same in both shielded
protocols; only the key differs.
\begin{enumerate}
\item For Orchard, $\dk = \dk_s$ of the scope $s$, as above.
\item For Sapling, $\dk$ is the diversifier-key component of the account's
  Sapling extended spending key: $\dk_m = \mathsf{truncate}_{32}(
  \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{sk}_m}([\mathtt{0x10}]))$ at the master key
  and $\dk_i = \mathsf{truncate}_{32}(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{I_L}(
  [\mathtt{0x16}] \,\|\, \dk_{\mathsf{par}}))$ for a hardened child, with
  $I_L$ the left half of the child's derivation output (ZIP 32, ``Sapling
  master key generation'' and ``Sapling child key derivation''); Sapling
  key derivation is cited and not constructed.
\end{enumerate}
The diversifier $d_j$, and the index $j$, are \emph{valid} for a protocol if
and only if that protocol's $\mathsf{DiversifyHash}$ does not return $\bot$
at $d_j$.
\begin{enumerate}[resume]
\item Orchard: $\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is total, so every
  index is valid, a scope has exactly $2^{88}$ addresses, and the default
  diversifier is $d_0$ (ZIP 32, ``Orchard diversifier and OVK
  derivation'').
\item Sapling:
  \[
    \mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}(d) :=
    \mathsf{GroupHash}^{\mathbb{J}(r)*}_{\mathsf{URS}}(\texttt{Zcash\_gd},
    \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{88}(d)),
  \]
  a hash into the points of order $r_{\mathbb{J}}$
  of the Jubjub curve that returns $\bot$ on some inputs (protocol
  specification, \S``$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ and
  $\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ Hash Functions'',
  \S``Jubjub'' and \S``Group Hash into Jubjub'', cited and not
  constructed). Approximately half of all indices are valid (ZIP 32,
  ``Sapling diversifier derivation''); the probability under the
  specification's random-oracle model is derived in
  \S\ref{sec:addr-derive}.
  The default Sapling diversifier is $d_j$ for the least valid $j$ (same
  section).
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

Totality of $\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ removes the search
for a valid index. The Sapling failure rate matters when one index must
serve several protocols at once (\S\ref{sec:addr-derive}).

Index-choice rules, specified (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Key
Components'' and \S``Sapling Key Components'', notes). Orchard diversifier
indices SHOULD NOT be chosen at random; ZIP 32 specifies their use in
hierarchical deterministic wallets. Address generators MAY encode
information in the diversifier index, recoverable by a holder of $\dk$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:addr-ownership}). For Sapling, generators MAY encode
information in the diversifier itself, and it is RECOMMENDED to encode it in
the ZIP 32 diversifier index instead, so that only a holder of $\dk$ reads
it.

% compute/ua.py:
% [sec:addr-indices] k random 88-bit diversifiers collide with probability in
%   [1 - e^(-k(k-1)/2^89), k(k-1)/2^89]; at k = 2^44: [0.393, 0.500]
\begin{proposition}[Distinct diversifiers]\label{prop:addr-distinct}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item For every diversifier key $\dk$ the map $j \mapsto d_j$ is a
  bijection from $\{0, \dots, 2^{88} - 1\}$ onto $\{0,1\}^{88}$. In
  particular distinct indices give distinct diversifiers, and a wallet
  issues the indices $0, 1, 2, \dots$ over the whole index space without a
  repeated diversifier.
\item If instead $k$ diversifiers are drawn independently and uniformly
  from $\{0,1\}^{88}$, the probability $P$ that two coincide satisfies
  \[
    1 - e^{-k(k-1)/2^{89}} \le P \le \frac{k(k-1)}{2^{89}},
  \]
  so a collision becomes likely once $k$ is of order
  $2^{44} = \sqrt{2^{88}}$; at $k = 2^{44}$ the bounds are $0.393$ and
  $0.500$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~The map $\mathrm{LE}_{88}$ is a bijection from
$\{0, \dots, 2^{88} - 1\}$ onto $\{0,1\}^{88}$, and
$\mathsf{FF1\text{-}AES256}$ under a fixed key and tweak is a bijection of
$\{0,1\}^{88}$ (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Format-preserving encryption and
FF1'', Proposition ``FF1 is a keyed bijection on $\mathcal{X}$''); the
composition is a bijection.
(b)~The \emph{Math Guide}, \S``The union bound and a birthday
calculation'', Proposition ``Birthday bound'', with $N = 2^{88}$.
\end{proof}

The specification notes that an $88$-bit diversifier is not unguessable at
the $128$-bit security level and that randomly chosen diversifiers collide
near $2^{44}$ choices; the keyed permutation removes the collisions, provided
indices do not repeat (protocol specification,
\S``$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ and
$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ Hash Functions'', notes). One
further reason for a keyed permutation is ZIP 32's: it makes the diversifier
sequence of each account pseudorandom and uncorrelated with that of any other
account, so issued diversifiers do not reveal how many addresses have been
generated (ZIP 32, ``Sapling diversifier derivation'' and ``Orchard diversifier
and OVK derivation''). The \emph{Ironwood Guide} supplies Assumption
``FF1-AES256 as a pseudorandom permutation'' (\S``Diversified addresses'') and
the unlinkability proposition only.

In Orchard, $\dk_s$ is derived from the full viewing key of scope $s$, so a
holder of the account's Orchard full viewing key computes $\dk_s$ for both
scopes, hence the index of any diversifier of the account
(Lemma~\ref{lem:addr-ownership}; ZIP 32, ``Orchard diversifier and OVK
derivation''). In Sapling the same capability belongs to the extended full
viewing key, of which $\dk$ is a component. A holder of the incoming viewing
key $(\dk, \ivk)$ of one scope has this capability for that scope only.

\begin{lemma}[Index recovery and ownership]\label{lem:addr-ownership}
Let $(d, \pkd)$ be an Orchard address and $(\dk, \ivk)$ the incoming viewing
key of some scope.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The value $j := \mathrm{LE}_{88}^{-1}(\mathsf{FF1\text{-}AES256.
  Decrypt}(\dk, \texttt{""}, d))$ is defined for every $d$ and every $\dk$,
  and $d_j = d$. Decryption alone therefore proves nothing about ownership.
\item The address $(d, \pkd)$ is the address of $(\dk, \ivk)$ at some index
  if and only if $\pkd = [\ivk]\,\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}(d)$;
  the index is then unique and equals $j$.
\item A holder of an account's full viewing key decides whether $(d, \pkd)$
  belongs to the account, and at which scope and index, by applying~(ii)
  with $(\dk_{\mathsf{ext}}, \ivk_{\mathsf{ext}})$ and then with
  $(\dk_{\mathsf{int}}, \ivk_{\mathsf{int}})$. Both tests succeed only if
  $\ivk_{\mathsf{ext}} = \ivk_{\mathsf{int}}$, which has negligible
  probability for keys with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$ under
  Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes} and the hypotheses of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}.
\end{enumerate}
The Sapling case of~(i) and~(ii) is the same with
$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$, the test of~(ii) failing when it
returns $\bot$. For~(iii), both tests succeed only if the two Sapling incoming
viewing keys are equal; this volume does not bound that probability for
Sapling.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i)~FF1 decryption is the two-sided inverse of encryption under every key
and tweak (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Format-preserving encryption and FF1'',
Proposition ``FF1 is a keyed bijection on $\mathcal{X}$''), and
$\mathrm{LE}_{88}$ is a bijection.
(ii)~If $(d, \pkd)$ is the address at index $j'$, then $d = d_{j'}$, so
$j' = j$ by~(i) and Proposition~\ref{prop:addr-distinct}~(a), and
$\pkd = [\ivk]\,\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}(d)$ by the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Diversified address'' (\S``Diversified
addresses''). Conversely, if $\pkd =
[\ivk]\,\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}(d)$, the address at index $j$
is $(d_j, [\ivk]\,\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}(d_j)) = (d, \pkd)$.
(iii)~If both tests succeed, then $[\ivk_{\mathsf{ext}}]\,\gd =
[\ivk_{\mathsf{int}}]\,\gd$ with $\gd \neq \mathcal{O}$ in a group of prime
order $p_{\Vesta}$, and both scalars lie in $\{1, \dots, p_{\Pallas} - 1\}$ with
$p_{\Pallas} < p_{\Vesta}$, so $\ivk_{\mathsf{ext}} = \ivk_{\mathsf{int}}$. Its
probability is bounded as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:zip32-scopes}:
after the replacement of Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}, at most
$2/p_{\Vesta}$ by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Distribution of $\ivk$''
(\S``Viewing keys''), plus the distinguishing advantage, equality being
efficiently decidable.
\end{proof}

Unlinkability of the addresses of one key is cited with its assumptions.
For Orchard it is the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Diversified addresses'',
Proposition ``Unlinkability of diversified addresses'': for two keys
generated independently with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, an
adversary that holds $\aksym$, $\nksym$ and $\ovk$ of both keys and chooses
indices $j_0$, $j_1$ and $j' \notin \{j_0, j_1\}$ distinguishes which key
produced the third address with negligible advantage, conditioned on the
three diversifiers being pairwise distinct. Its assumptions are the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion'', ``Joint
hiding of the viewing keys'', ``FF1-AES256 as a pseudorandom permutation'',
``GroupHash as a random oracle'', ``DDH on Pallas'' and ``Discrete
logarithms on Pallas''. Account keys are admitted as such keys by
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}, the win event being decidable by the
challenger; the addresses of one key at distinct indices have distinct
diversifiers by Proposition~\ref{prop:addr-distinct}~(a). For Sapling the
protocol specification argues the same property under DDH on the
prime-order subgroup of Jubjub, with $\mathsf{GroupHash}^{\mathbb{J}(r)*}$
modelled as a random oracle, through IK-CPA key privacy of ElGamal
(\S``$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ and
$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ Hash Functions'', notes;
\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Key privacy''). This volume does not prove the
Sapling case.

\begin{remark}[Chosen-diversifier oracle]\label{rem:addr-oracle}
Suppose that the holder of an incoming viewing key gives an adversary the
address of that key at any diversifier the adversary chooses. The adversary
then breaks unlinkability outright: given an address
$(d', \pkd')$, it requests the address at $d'$ and compares the returned
transmission key with $\pkd'$. The two are equal if and only if the key is
the same, the base $g_{d'}$ being a point other than the identity in a group
of prime order. No other privacy property is affected. Implementations
SHOULD avoid providing such a chosen-diversifier oracle (protocol
specification, \S``$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ and
$\mathsf{DiversifyHash}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ Hash Functions'', notes). An
interface returning the address at a requested \emph{index} gives no chosen
diversifier (Lemma~\ref{lem:addr-index}), but it returns the issued addresses
themselves: it links every address whose index the requester queries, and
under sequential issuance (Proposition~\ref{prop:addr-distinct}) small
indices reach every issued address.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Index queries give no chosen diversifier]\label{lem:addr-index}
Let $\dk$ be either diversifier key of an Orchard key with
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$. Under
Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}, the hypotheses of
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}, and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption
``FF1-AES256 as a pseudorandom permutation'' (\S``Diversified addresses''),
an adversary without $\dk$ that obtains the diversifiers at $q$ indices of
its choice obtains a given $d$, fixed independently of $\dk$, with
probability at most $q/2^{88}$ plus its
distinguishing advantages against the two assumptions and, for an account
key, the loss of Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}. This volume does not prove
the Sapling case.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Replace $\dk$ by a uniform $32$-byte string independent of the other keys;
by Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}, applied after
Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}, the probability changes by at most that
distinguishing advantage and the loss of that Lemma, the win event being
decidable. Then replace
FF1-AES256 keyed by $\dk$ with a uniform permutation of
$\{0,1\}^{88}$; the probability changes by at most the distinguishing
advantage. Repeated indices add nothing, so let the adversary query $k \le q$
distinct indices. Each image is uniform on the values not yet returned, so
the $i$-th image is the first to equal $d$ with probability
\[
  \prod_{j=1}^{i-1} \frac{2^{88} - j}{2^{88} - j + 1} \cdot
  \frac{1}{2^{88} - i + 1} = 2^{-88};
\]
summing over $i \le k$, $d$ is returned with probability
$k \cdot 2^{-88} \le q \cdot 2^{-88}$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Raw encodings}\label{sec:addr-raw}

The items of unified containers carry the byte encodings below. Each is
stated with the validity condition that a decoder applies.

\begin{definition}[Raw receiver and key encodings]\label{def:addr-raw}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item An \emph{Orchard raw payment address} has $43$ bytes,
  $\mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{88}(d) \,\|\, \pkd^{\star}$, where
  $\pkd^{\star} = \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{256}(\mathsf{repr}_{\PP}(\pkd))$ is the
  $32$-byte compressed encoding of the Pallas point $\pkd$ (\emph{Ironwood
  Guide}, \S``Fields, groups, and encodings''). A decoder MUST treat the
  address as invalid if $\mathsf{abst}_{\PP}$ returns $\bot$ or the
  identity (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Raw Payment Addresses'').
\item A \emph{Sapling raw payment address} has $43$ bytes,
  \[
    \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{88}(d) \,\|\,
    \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{256}(\mathsf{repr}_{\mathbb{J}}(\pkd)),
  \]
  with the compressed Jubjub encoding. It MUST be considered invalid if
  $\mathsf{abst}_{\mathbb{J}}$ returns $\bot$, if the encoding of $\pkd$ is
  non-canonical, or if $\pkd$ is not a point of order $r_{\mathbb{J}}$, the
  identity included (protocol specification, \S``Sapling Payment
  Addresses'').
\item An \emph{Orchard raw full viewing key} has $96$ bytes,
  \[
    \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\aksym) \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nksym)
    \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rivk).
  \]
  It MUST be considered invalid if
  $\aksym$, $\nksym$ or $\rivk$ is not the canonical encoding of an element
  of its field ($\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$, $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$, $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$),
  if $\aksym$ is not the $x$-coordinate of a point of
  $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$ other than the identity, or if the external or
  the internal incoming viewing key derived from it is $0$ or $\bot$
  (protocol specification, \S``Orchard Raw Full Viewing Keys'').
\item An \emph{Orchard raw incoming viewing key} has $64$ bytes,
  $\dk \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\ivk)$. It MUST be considered invalid
  unless $\ivk \in \{1, \dots, p_{\Pallas} - 1\}$ (protocol specification,
  \S``Orchard Raw Incoming Viewing Keys'').
\item String forms. No Bech32 or Bech32m encoding exists for an individual
  Orchard payment address, incoming viewing key or full viewing key; the
  unified encodings of \S\ref{sec:addr-string} are their only strings. A
  Sapling payment address keeps a standalone Bech32 string, with
  human-readable part \texttt{zs} on Mainnet and \texttt{ztestsapling} on
  Testnet (same sections).
\item Transparent receivers. A \emph{P2PKH} (pay-to-public-key-hash)
  address pays to the $20$-byte $\mathsf{hash160}$ of a $33$-byte compressed
  ECDSA validating key; a \emph{P2SH} (pay-to-script-hash) address pays to
  the $20$-byte $\mathsf{hash160}$ of a redeem script that the spender
  reveals and satisfies (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The transparent layer's
  hashes''). Their raw encoding prefixes two version bytes to the hash and
  is written in Base58Check (protocol specification, \S``Transparent
  Addresses''). Inside a unified container a transparent receiver is the
  bare $20$-byte hash, without the two version bytes (ZIP 316,
  ``Requirements for both Unified Addresses and Unified Viewing Keys'').
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

One Orchard raw address serves both pools of the Orchard protocol
(\S\ref{sec:addr-ua}).

\subsection{Unified addresses}\label{sec:addr-ua}

ZIP 316 defines Revision~$0$, the format of this subsection and of
\S\ref{sec:addr-string}, and Revision~$2$, which adds metadata items,
splits the address prefix into a shielded-only and a transparent-enabled
form, and renames the viewing-key prefixes (\S\ref{sec:addr-rev2});
Revision~$1$ is withdrawn (ZIP 316, ``Revisions''). The revision of a
container is read from its human-readable part. The status of ZIP 316 is
that of Table~\ref{tab:intro-zips}; everything Revision~$2$ adds is
specified in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}.

% compute/ua.py:
% [sec:addr-ua] typecode and length each <= 0x2000000: compactSize(0x2000000)
%   = fe || 4 bytes = 5 bytes, so an item header takes at most 10 bytes
\begin{definition}[Unified container]\label{def:addr-container}
For $n \in \{0, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\}$, $\mathrm{compactSize}(n)$ is the
variable-length integer of Bitcoin that ZIP 316 cites:
\[
  \mathrm{compactSize}(n) :=
  \begin{cases}
    [n] & \text{if } n < 253, \\
    [\mathtt{0xFD}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{16}(n) & \text{if }
      253 \le n < 2^{16}, \\
    [\mathtt{0xFE}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(n) & \text{if }
      2^{16} \le n < 2^{32}, \\
    [\mathtt{0xFF}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{64}(n) & \text{otherwise},
  \end{cases}
\]
the range of $n$ fixing the form. An \emph{item} is a pair $(\tau, v)$ of a
\emph{typecode} $\tau \in \N$ and a byte string $v$. The \emph{raw encoding}
of a container with items $(\tau_1, v_1), \dots, (\tau_k, v_k)$, where
$\tau_1 < \dots < \tau_k$, is
\[
  \mathsf{raw} := \mathop{\big\|}_{i=1}^{k} \mathrm{compactSize}(\tau_i)
    \,\|\, \mathrm{compactSize}(\mathrm{length}(v_i)) \,\|\, v_i .
\]
Both $\tau_i$ and $\mathrm{length}(v_i)$ MUST be at most
$\mathtt{0x2000000}$; since $\mathtt{0x2000000} \in [2^{16}, 2^{32})$, each
takes at most $5$ bytes and an item header at most $10$.

Typecodes $\mathtt{0x00}$ and $\mathtt{0x01}$ are \emph{transparent},
$\mathtt{0x02}$ and $\mathtt{0x03}$ \emph{shielded}, and $\mathtt{0xC0}$ to
$\mathtt{0xFC}$ designate \emph{metadata items}, items that are neither
receivers nor viewing keys (\S\ref{sec:addr-rev2}). A \emph{unified address}
(UA) is a container whose non-metadata items are receivers, in the raw
encodings of Definition~\ref{def:addr-raw} under the typecodes of
Table~\ref{tab:addr-typecodes}. A \emph{unified full viewing key} (UFVK) and
a \emph{unified incoming viewing key} (UIVK) are containers whose
non-metadata items are full, respectively incoming, viewing keys
(\S\ref{sec:addr-uvk}); UVK denotes either. No typecode exists for Sprout.

Validity. Every container holds at least one non-metadata item. A
Revision~$0$ UA, and a Revision~$0$ UVK, holds at least one shielded item. A
Revision~$2$ shielded-only UA holds no transparent item; a Revision~$2$
transparent-enabled UA may consist of a single P2PKH receiver, and a
Revision~$2$ UVK need hold no shielded item. A Consumer MUST reject a
container
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item that violates the containment rule of its revision;
\item in which a typecode repeats, or which holds both $\mathtt{0x00}$ and
  $\mathtt{0x01}$;
\item which holds only metadata items;
\item whose items are not in ascending typecode order;
\item which has bytes after the last item;
\item any of whose items fails the validation of its encoding
  (Definition~\ref{def:addr-raw}; \S\ref{sec:addr-uvk}).
\end{enumerate}
A Consumer MUST ignore items with typecodes it does not recognise, with the
exception stated in \S\ref{sec:addr-rev2}; a Consumer therefore parses a
container that holds unrecognised items.

Same-account rule. Within one UA or UVK, all receivers, full viewing keys
and incoming viewing keys derived hierarchically SHOULD belong to the same
account at the ZIP 32 or BIP 44 account level, or, for ZIP 48 and FROST
UVKs, to one set of signers able to generate independent addresses for that
spending authority. FROST key material MUST be derived according to ZIP 312
and ZIP 2005, in particular ZIP 2005, ``Usage with FROST''.
