  a note to it, compute its commitment and note ciphertext, choose the
  remaining compact fields ($\nfsym$ of an Action, which is $\rho$ of the
  note), and serve the result as a compact output or Action, with tree
  sizes consistent with it; the result passes
  Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept}. Unless a transaction of the chain
  appends the same commitment, no authentication path from it to an anchor
  of the chain can be found except with negligible probability, so the
  wallet displays a note whose every spend is rejected.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
(a)~By Proposition~\ref{prop:compact-sound}(a) an accepted candidate opens
the commitment of its compact record. By clause~(a) that record is the
corresponding field of a transaction of the best chain, whose commitments
are appended to the pool's tree in the order that fixes the position
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Merkle hash and the tree'', Definition ``Note
commitment tree''; Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes}(a)). The omitted fields are
validated by the consensus rules for every transaction of the chain
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'' and
\S``Anchors'').

(b)~Acceptance checks only the lead byte, the field ranges, the commitment
recomputed from the plaintext and $\mathsf{epk} = [\mathsf{esk}]\,\gd$
(Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept}), and an honestly constructed note to
an address of the wallet satisfies all of them; the tree-size checks of
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes} pass on sizes the server chooses consistently,
by part~(d) of that lemma. A spend proves a valid path from the commitment
to an anchor of the chain (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authentication
paths''), and a valid path from a value that is not the leaf at its position
yields a collision of the Merkle hash, found with at most negligible
probability (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Anchors'', Proposition ``Membership
soundness''; protocol specification, \S``Merkle Path Validity'', for Sapling).
ZIP 307, ``Output Compression'', grounds the commitment check on the same trust,
in the entity that assembles transactions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Omission is undetected]\label{rem:service-omission}
Completeness of the served data is trusted, not verified. The size check of
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes} detects the omission of a commitment only
while the served sizes are correct. A server that omits compact outputs or
Actions and serves tree sizes, frontiers, subtree roots and roots
consistent with the omission, or omits or alters
revealed nullifiers, of which a compact block carries no count, passes
every check of Proposition~\ref{prop:service-verify}. Omitted outputs hide
receipts. Omitted nullifiers hide spends: the wallet shows spent notes as
spendable, and a re-spend of such a note is rejected for a repeated
nullifier (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Nullifier sets'') while it discloses
to every party that receives it that it spends what an earlier transaction
spent, the leak of repeated spends of ZIP 315, ``Obtaining user consent for
information leakage''. Only clause~(a) of Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}
excludes omission (ZIP 307, ``Security Model'').
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Malformed responses]
A response with a field outside its type (a nullifier not of $32$ bytes, a
non-canonical encoding of a field element, a malformed compact output or
Action, a block outside the requested range) is an error of the response:
the results of the query are not applied, and the query may be repeated. It
is not a continuity error and triggers no rollback (``Reorganisation
detection and recovery'', \S\ref{sec:tree-reorg}). This rule is designed but
unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Checks of a fetched transaction]
A full transaction fetched by its identifier SHOULD be checked against the
corresponding compact outputs, in addition to verification of its
signatures (ZIP 307, ``Block header validation''). The checks are: the
identifier computed from the fetched transaction equals the queried one
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'', Definition
``Transaction identifier''; protocol specification, \S``Transaction
Identifiers''); its outputs and Actions agree with the compact records; and its
signatures verify over its signature digest (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Transaction digests and signatures'', Definition ``Signature digest''). They
bind the full transaction to the compact data and its authorisation to its
effects. They bind neither to the chain: compact blocks carry too little to
check an identifier against the block's transaction tree, and that binding rests
on clause~(a) of Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The query interface}\label{sec:service-queries}

A wallet holds keys and no chain state (Definition~\ref{def:service-arch}),
so the interface delivers what the protocols below consume: every compact
output and Action of every shielded pool, for trial decryption
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Trial decryption and note acceptance''); every
revealed nullifier, for spend detection (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Nullifier sets''); for each pool, tree sizes, frontiers and the roots of
complete subtrees, for positions and authentication paths (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Authentication paths''); the full transactions that concern the
wallet; submission of its transactions and their status; and, for
transparent receivers, the transparent outputs paid to its addresses.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, tree.py:
% [sec:tree-shards] depth 32, split at height 16: at most 2^32 / 2^16 =
%   2^16 = 65536 subtrees, each covering 2^16 = 65536 positions
% [sec:tree-ready] subtree of position p: floor(p / 2^16); extent
%   [i 2^16, (i + 1) 2^16); e.g. p = 65535 -> 0, p = 65536 -> 1,
%   p = 2^32 - 1 -> 65535
\begin{definition}[Subtree root and completing height]%
\label{def:service-subtree}
Let $\mathsf{pool} \in \{\mathsf{Sapling}, \mathsf{Orchard},
\mathsf{Ironwood}\}$, whose note commitment tree has depth $32$ and layers
numbered from layer~$0$, the root, to layer~$32$, the leaves
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Merkle hash and the tree'', Definition ``Note
commitment tree''; protocol specification, \S``Note Commitment Trees'', for
Sapling). For $0 \le i < 2^{16}$, \emph{subtree}~$i$ of the tree is the subtree
whose leaves are the positions $[\,i \cdot 2^{16},\ (i+1) \cdot 2^{16})$; its
\emph{root} is the node $M^{16}_i$, sixteen layers above the leaves. When the
tree holds $n$ leaves, subtree~$i$ is \emph{complete} if $n \ge (i+1) \cdot
2^{16}$, after which its root no longer changes; the complete subtrees are those
with $i < \lfloor n / 2^{16} \rfloor$. The \emph{completing height} of a
complete subtree~$i$ is the height of the block whose transactions append
position $(i+1) \cdot 2^{16} - 1$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Transaction status]\label{def:service-status}
The \emph{status} of a transaction identifier $\mathsf{txid}$ on the
server's best chain, with tip height $T$, is one of:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\mathsf{unknown}$: the server knows no transaction with identifier
  $\mathsf{txid}$;
\item $\mathsf{unmined}$: the server knows the transaction, in its mempool or
  in a block off the best chain, and no block of the best chain contains it;
\item $\mathsf{mined}(h)$: the block of the best chain at height $h \le T$
  contains it.
\end{enumerate}
The encoding of the status lies outside the abstract interface. The status
is designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Light-client query interface]\label{def:service-queries}
The \emph{light-client service} is the set of typed queries of
Table~\ref{tab:service-queries}, each answered from the server's best chain
under clause~(a) of Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}. A \emph{block
identifier} is a height or a $32$-byte block hash. A \emph{pool set} is a
set $\mathcal{P}$ of the pool selection of ``The compact block and the
compact transaction'' (\S\ref{sec:compact-format}): shielded pools, with
transparent data selectable alongside. The service serves every pool set
and transparent data. A query whose argument names no block of the best
chain, or a transaction that the server does not know, is answered
with~$\bot$. Queries of the mempool and of server metadata are not part of
the interface.
\end{definition}

\begin{table}[tp]
\centering\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{2.9cm} p{3.0cm} p{7.4cm}@{}}
\toprule
Query & Arguments & Response \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{Chain}} \\
$\mathsf{Tip}()$ & none
  & $(h, \mathsf{hash})$ of the tip of the best chain; the tip input of
    every confirmation count (\S\ref{sec:spendability}) \\
$\mathsf{Block}(b)$ & block identifier $b$
  & the compact block of $b$, with all shielded pools and transparent
    data \\
$\mathsf{Blocks}(s, e, \mathcal{P})$
  & \raggedright heights $s \le e$;\newline pool set $\mathcal{P}$
  & the compact blocks at heights $s, s+1, \dots, e$ in ascending order,
    restricted to $\mathcal{P}$; every height of $[s, e]$ present, a block
    left with no transaction carrying an empty list \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{Trees}} \\
$\mathsf{TreeState}(b)$ & block identifier $b$
  & height and hash of $b$, and for each shielded pool the size $n$ and the
    frontier of its tree in the final treestate of $b$; an empty frontier
    denotes the empty tree \\
$\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}(\mathsf{pool}, i)$
  & \raggedright shielded pool $\mathsf{pool}$;\newline start
    index~$i$
  & in index order, the pair (root, completing height) of every complete
    subtree of the tree of $\mathsf{pool}$ with index at least $i$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{Transactions}} \\
$\mathsf{Tx}(\mathsf{txid})$ & $32$-byte identifier
  & the full serialised transaction \\
$\mathsf{Submit}(\mathit{bytes})$ & serialised transaction
  & $\mathsf{accepted}(\mathsf{txid})$ if the server's node admits it to its
    mempool, else $\mathsf{rejected}$ \\
$\mathsf{Status}(\mathsf{txid})$ & $32$-byte identifier
  & the status of Definition~\ref{def:service-status} \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{Transparent}} \\
$\mathsf{Utxos}(A)$ & \raggedright set $A$ of transparent addresses
  & the unspent outputs of the best chain paying to $A$, each with outpoint,
    value, script and height \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Queries, arguments, and responses of the light-client service
(Definition~\ref{def:service-queries}). Each response is the value on the
server's best chain; $\bot$ answers an argument that names nothing on it.}
\label{tab:service-queries}
\end{table}

The frontier in a response of $\mathsf{TreeState}$ is that of the
\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Append-only and incrementally updatable trees'',
Definition ``Frontier'': for a tree of $n$ leaves, the left-sibling nodes
along the path of the next leaf, one for each set bit of $n$, from which the
root and every later append are computed. The empty frontier is the state
before the pool's first commitment. The final treestate of a block is that
of the protocol specification, \S``Transactions and Treestates''
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Anchors'', Definition ``Treestate and anchor''). The
serialisation of the frontier is fixed by no ZIP and is not restated. ZIP
307, ``Proxy operation'', also identifies a transaction by its block and its
index in the block; the interface uses the identifier alone.

\begin{remark}[Classification of the queries]
ZIP 307, ``Proxy operation'', specifies the queries $\mathsf{Tip}$,
$\mathsf{Block}$, $\mathsf{Blocks}$ and $\mathsf{Tx}$. No ZIP specifies
$\mathsf{TreeState}$, $\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}$, $\mathsf{Submit}$,
$\mathsf{Status}$ or the transparent query $\mathsf{Utxos}$, and ZIP 307,
``Client-server interaction'', leaves the acquisition of a starting tree
state open: ``Or, it has to set X to the block height at which Sapling
activated, so as to be sent the entire commitment tree. [TODO: Decide which
to specify.]'' These queries, and pool selection in $\mathsf{Blocks}$, are
designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Minimal query set]
A shielded synchronisation uses $\mathsf{Tip}$, $\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}$ for
each shielded pool, $\mathsf{Blocks}$ and $\mathsf{TreeState}$; the full
transactions that concern the wallet add $\mathsf{Tx}$. A wallet that
watches transparent receivers adds $\mathsf{Utxos}$, whose argument names
its addresses in the clear (``Key independence of queries'',
\S\ref{sec:priv-keyindep}). This set is the requirement of the
synchronisation protocols of ``Note discovery''
(\S\ref{sec:sync-scanning}) and ``Commitment-tree synchronisation''
(\S\ref{sec:sync-tree}), not a deployed fact.
\end{remark}

\section{Note discovery}\label{sec:sync-scanning}

This section states the protocol by which a wallet reconstructs its notes
from the chain. It fixes the chain state and the account birthday that bound
the scan, the keys an account is scanned with under the rules of ZIP 326,
the note state of a wallet and the scan of one compact block that updates
it, the continuity predicate and the conditions under which a scan in any
order is correct, the theorems of sound and complete discovery and of
recovery from the seed, and the work that scanning costs. The tree
information a wallet retains for its witnesses is the subject of
``Commitment-tree synchronisation'' (\S\ref{sec:sync-tree}).

Throughout, $P$ ranges over the shielded pools $\mathsf{Sapling}$,
$\mathsf{Orchard}$ and $\mathsf{Ironwood}$; $H_{\mathsf{S}}$ is the Sapling
activation height, and $H_{\mathsf{NU6.3}}$ the NU6.3 activation height of
ZIP 258. For a compact block $b$, $n_P(b)$ and $S^{P}_{\mathsf{end}}(b)$ are
the commitment count and the end size of
Definition~\ref{def:compact-position}.

\subsection{The account birthday}\label{sec:scan-birthday}

\begin{definition}[Chain state]\label{def:scan-chainstate}
For a height $h$, the \emph{chain state} at $h$ is
\[
  C(h) := \bigl(h,\ \mathsf{hash}_h,\
    (m_P(h), F_P(h))_{P \in \{\mathsf{Sapling}, \mathsf{Orchard},
    \mathsf{Ironwood}\}}\bigr),
\]
where $\mathsf{hash}_h$ is the $32$-byte hash of block $h$;
$m_P(h) \in [0, 2^{32}]$ is the number of leaves of the note commitment
tree of pool $P$ in the final treestate of block $h$; and $F_P(h)$ is the
frontier of that tree (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Append-only and
incrementally updatable trees'', Definition ``Frontier''): for each set
bit~$t$ of $m_P(h)$, the root of the completed left subtree of height $t$
on the rightmost filled path. The empty tree has $m_P(h) = 0$ and the empty
frontier, and the full tree, $m_P(h) = 2^{32}$, has its root as frontier.
The three trees are distinct trees of depth $32$
($\mathsf{MerkleDepth}^{\mathsf{Sapling}} =
\mathsf{MerkleDepth}^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = 32$; protocol specification,
\S``Constants'' and \S``Note Commitment Trees''): the Sapling tree of the
specification, and the Orchard-pool and Ironwood-pool trees, each built with
the Orchard Merkle hash (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Merkle hash and the
tree'', Definition ``Note commitment tree''). The chain state $C(h)$ is the
response of $\mathsf{TreeState}(h)$ (Table~\ref{tab:service-queries}).
\end{definition}

By the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s Definition ``Frontier'' (\S``Append-only and
incrementally updatable trees''), the pair $(m_P(h), F_P(h))$ determines the
root of the tree of $P$ after block $h$ and suffices to append every later leaf
without any leaf appended before block $h + 1$. That it also yields every later
authentication path is shown in ``Frontiers as scan seeds''
(\S\ref{sec:tree-seeds}).

\begin{definition}[Account birthday]\label{def:scan-birthday}
The \emph{birthday} of an account is a height $B$, known to the wallet, that
is a lower bound on the height of the first block in which the account
could have received funds (ZIP 326, ``Terminology''). With the birthday the
wallet stores the chain state $C(B - 1)$, which summarises every note
commitment of every pool before $B$. The scan of the account starts at
block $B$, and no block below $B$ is scanned for it. The birthday of a
wallet is the minimum of the birthdays of its accounts.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{Added accounts.} Let an account with birthday $B'$ be added
  while the greatest height of a block the wallet has scanned exceeds $B'$.
  Every block of $[B', \text{that height}]$ is then scanned again with the
  scanning keys of the new account (\S\ref{sec:scan-keys}), since earlier
  scans did not try them; the results of the other accounts are unchanged.
\item \emph{No recorded birthday.} A wallet that restores a seed without a
  recorded birthday takes $B := H_{\mathsf{S}}$: no shielded address of a
  light client can have received a relevant transaction before Sapling
  activation (ZIP 307, ``Client-server interaction'', ``Importing a
  pre-existing seed''). The bound concerns shielded receipts only; the
  transparent history of the account's addresses may lie below it.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Choosing a birthday]\label{rem:scan-birthday-depth}
Let the tip be at height $T$ when an account is created, and let $d$ be at
least the wallet's assumed maximum reorganisation depth. The birthday of
the new account is taken as $B \le T - d + 1$, with stored chain state
$C(B - 1)$ at height $T - d$ or below. A reorganisation that replaces at
most $d$ blocks re-mines no transaction below height $T - d + 1$; with $B$
closer to the tip, such a reorganisation could re-mine a transaction paying
the account at a height below $B$, which the wallet never scans, and the
payment would be missed. Consensus imposes no maximum reorganisation depth
(\emph{Consensus Guide}, \S``Rollback limits, coinbase maturity, and finality''), so $d$ is a wallet parameter, not a guarantee. The rollback
window of $600$ blocks that ZIP 218, ``Block-count-based constants'',
recommends concerns nodes and bounds no wallet's choice of $d$. An earlier
birthday is always safe for discovery and costs scanning work
(\S\ref{sec:scan-cost}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Scanning keys}\label{sec:scan-keys}

\begin{definition}[Scanning keys of an account]\label{def:scan-keys}
Let an account $a$ have Sapling keys and Orchard-protocol keys derived as in
``Hierarchical key derivation (ZIP 32)'' (\S\ref{sec:keys-zip32}), the
latter with the flag $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ of
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-useqsk}. Scopes are
$s \in \{\mathsf{ext}, \mathsf{int}\}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{Sapling.} For each scope $s$, the pair
  $(\ivk^{\mathsf{S}}_s, \nksym^{\mathsf{S}}_s)$ of incoming viewing key and
  nullifier deriving key (ZIP 32, ``Sapling internal key derivation'').
  Sapling outputs are scanned from $B$.
\item \emph{Orchard protocol.} For each scope $s$ and each
  $u \in \{\mathsf{false}, \mathsf{true}\}$, the incoming viewing key
  $\ivk_{s,u}$ of scope $s$ computed with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = u$, and the
  nullifier deriving key $\nksym$, the same in both branches of
  Construction~\ref{def:zip32-useqsk} and in both scopes
  (Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}).
\end{enumerate}
A receiver and its $\ivk$ are scoped to the Orchard protocol, not to a
pool: the same $\ivk$ trial-decrypts Orchard-pool and Ironwood-pool note
ciphertexts (ZIP 326, ``Scanning for Ironwood-pool and Orchard-pool
notes''); the pool of an Action is that of the bundle that carries it, and
Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept} admits lead byte $\mathtt{0x02}$ in the
Orchard pool and $\mathtt{0x03}$ in the Ironwood pool. The \emph{use\_qsk set}
$U_a$ is $\{u\}$ when the wallet knows the account's value $u$ and
$\{\mathsf{false}, \mathsf{true}\}$ when it does not.

The following rules of ZIP 326, with their normative levels, fix which
$\ivk$ is tried on which output.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(R\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Restoration.} A wallet restoring from a seed MUST take
  $U_a = \{\mathsf{false}, \mathsf{true}\}$ and scan with both values until
  rule~(R2) narrows it, because $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ is not part of the
  recovery information (``Scanning when restoring from seed'').
\item \emph{Narrowing.} Once the wallet sees funds received in either
  Orchard-protocol pool under a value $u$, it SHOULD stop scanning with the
  other value, $U_a := \{u\}$. The evidence stands if the transaction that
  provided it is later reorganised away, since it was valid on some chain
  (``Scan ranges'').
\item \emph{Ironwood pool.} The Ironwood pool is scanned from
  $\max(B, H_{\mathsf{NU6.3}})$ to the tip with $\ivk_{s,u}$ for
  $u \in U_a$, hence with up to four keys
  ($\{\mathsf{ext}, \mathsf{int}\} \times \{\mathsf{false},
  \mathsf{true}\}$) while $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ is unknown, subject to
  rule~(R4). The value $u = \mathsf{true}$ is kept for an account whose
  birthday precedes NU6.3 activation, since a recorded birthday may be
  conservatively early (``Scan ranges'').
\item \emph{First funds.} ZIP 326 describes this rule as an optimisation and
  does not require it (``First-funds scanning in the Ironwood pool''). While
  the wallet knows that the account has received no Ironwood-pool funds in
  any block below the one scanned, only the external keys $\ivk_{\mathsf{ext},
  u}$ are needed for an output, except in a transaction whose
  $\mathsf{valueBalanceIronwood}$ is negative, where both scopes are used.
  The knowledge is kept only for the contiguous prefix of heights from
  $\max(B, H_{\mathsf{NU6.3}})$ scanned without a receipt, and a
  reorganisation whose last unchanged block is at height $h_f$ cuts the
  prefix back to $\max(h_f, H_{\mathsf{NU6.3}})$. A compact transaction
  carries no value balance (Definition~\ref{def:compact-tx}), so a scan of
  compact blocks applies the exception only where it obtains
  $\mathsf{valueBalanceIronwood}$ otherwise; without it, both scopes are
  used.
\item \emph{Orchard pool} (``Scan ranges''). Orchard-pool note ciphertexts
  SHOULD NOT be scanned with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$ keys. For
  an account with birthday after NU6.3 activation they SHOULD NOT be scanned
  at all. For an account with birthday before NU6.3 activation they SHOULD
  NOT be scanned with the external $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$ key
  after NU6.3 activation. If the account's Orchard-pool balance is known to
  be zero at a block at or after NU6.3 activation, they SHOULD NOT be
  scanned in any block that descends from it.
\item \emph{Order.} A wallet MAY scan blocks in any order (``First-funds
  scanning in the Ironwood pool'').
\item \emph{Outgoing notes.} Outgoing notes are detected with the $\ovk$ of
  the same $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ value, which adds no key dimension
  (``Outgoing-note detection'').
\end{enumerate}
Under~(R5) the Orchard pool is scanned with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
\mathsf{false}$ only: with $\ivk_{\mathsf{int},\mathsf{false}}$ from $B$
until the Orchard-pool balance is known to be zero after NU6.3 activation,
and with $\ivk_{\mathsf{ext},\mathsf{false}}$ from $B$ to
$H_{\mathsf{NU6.3}} - 1$. The \emph{scanning keys} $K_a(P, b, t)$ of
account $a$ for pool $P$, block $b$ and transaction $t$ are the triples
$(\ivk, \nksym, s)$ that items~(i) and~(ii) and rules~(R1) to~(R5) select.
Every $\ivk$ is labelled $(a, s)$, which attributes each accepted note to
its account and scope.

Transparent receipts are detected at the transparent addresses the wallet
watches, which MUST include index~$0$ of each account (``Deriving unified
addresses'', \S\ref{sec:addr-derive}; ZIP 316, ``Deriving a Unified Address
from a UIVK''). The Ironwood pool is scanned as a pool of its own: its
outputs are accepted under its lead byte, its revealed nullifiers are
matched only against nullifiers of Ironwood-pool notes, and its tree is its
own (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Nullifier sets'', Remark ``Pool
locality'').
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Omitted keys and conformant senders]
Rules~(R2), (R4) and~(R5) omit a key only where the restrictions of ZIP 326,
``Wallet key-generation restrictions'', exclude an output of the account
under it. Every key of an account has one $\mathsf{use\_qsk}$ value (MUST).
No wallet sends Orchard-pool funds to $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$
keys, or to any external receiver after NU6.3 activation (MUST NOT).
Internal receivers are exposed to no party outside the wallet (ZIP 326,
``Scanning for Ironwood-pool and Orchard-pool notes''), so before the
account's first Ironwood-pool funds a note at its internal Ironwood receiver
arises only from the account's own transaction, funded by a negative
$\mathsf{valueBalanceIronwood}$ (``First-funds scanning in the Ironwood
pool''). No value enters the Orchard pool after NU6.3 activation, so a zero
Orchard-pool balance stays zero (``Scan ranges''). An output created in
violation of these restrictions by a non-conformant sender may be missed;
this is the cost of the SHOULD NOT rules, and it is part of the statement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:scan-discovery}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Spend detection requires the nullifier key]%
\label{prop:scan-ivk-spends}
The nullifier of an Orchard-protocol note is
$\mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(\rho, \psi, \cmsym)$ (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``The nullifier'', Definition ``Nullifier''), and that of a
Sapling note is $\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfSapling}}_{\nksym^{\star}}
(\rho^{\star})$ with
\[
  \rho^{\star} = \mathsf{repr}_{\mathbb{J}}\bigl(
  \mathsf{MixingPedersenHash}(\cmsym, \mathsf{pos})\bigr),
\]
a function of the
nullifier deriving key and of the note's position (protocol specification,
\S``Computing $\rho$ values and Nullifiers'').
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Let an Orchard-protocol account key have $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
  \mathsf{false}$. No efficient algorithm that receives the incoming viewing
  key $(\dk, \ivk)$ of one scope and $\aksym^{\PP}$, but not $\nksym$, and
  is then given notes of its choice with pairwise distinct $\rho$ outputs
  the nullifier of one of them, except with negligible probability.
\item Assume that $\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfSapling}}$, keyed by
  $\nksym^{\star}$ of a Sapling key generated as the specification
  prescribes, is pseudorandom to an efficient algorithm that holds the
  incoming viewing key and the spend validating key of that key: the
  specification's requirement that $\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfSapling}}$ be a
  pseudorandom function
  (\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#abstractprfs}%
  {\S``Pseudo Random Functions''}), applied to this key distribution.
  Then the statement of~(a) holds for the Sapling key.
\end{enumerate}
A holder of $(\dk, \ivk)$ therefore detects receipts, since trial
decryption needs only $\ivk$ (Construction~\ref{def:compact-trial}), and
cannot recognise a revealed nullifier as that of its note. A wallet that
holds only incoming viewing keys has an empty tracked-nullifier set, and the
set of notes it considers unspent is a superset of the true set. Links
through other public data are outside the statement (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Nullifier uniqueness and unlinkability'', Remark ``Limits''). For
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$ no claim is made
(Remark~\ref{rem:zip32-qsk-scope}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~Let $\mathcal{C}$ be an algorithm that outputs the nullifier of a
submitted note with probability $\varepsilon$. It yields a distinguisher
for the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Nullifier unlinkability''
(\S``Nullifier uniqueness and unlinkability''): the distinguisher runs
$\mathcal{C}$ on its inputs $(\dk, \ivk)$ and $\aksym^{\PP}$, submits the
notes $\mathcal{C}$ is given, and outputs $1$ exactly when an answer equals
the value $\mathcal{C}$ outputs for that note. In the real game it outputs
$1$ with probability at least $\varepsilon$. In the ideal game each answer
is $\Extract_{\PP}$ of a point uniform on $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$,
independent of the notes, except with probability $1/p_{\Vesta}$; it equals
a given value with probability at most $2 / \#\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$, since
$\Extract_{\PP}$ is at most two-to-one. Hence $\varepsilon$ is bounded by
the advantage bound of that proposition plus a negligible term, under the
assumptions it names. For the internal scope the proof of that
proposition applies with its hybrid~$H_2$ changed: the pair
$(\ivk_{\mathsf{int}}, \dk_{\mathsf{int}})$ is replaced by
$(\CommitIvk_{r'}(\aksym, \nksym), u_3)$ by
Assumption~\ref{ass:zip32-vk-scopes}, whose reduction, like that of~$H_2$,
draws $\aksym$ and $\nksym$ itself and passes on the fourth and fifth
components of its tuple. The cost is the advantage against that assumption
in place of the term for the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Joint
hiding of the viewing keys'' (\S``Viewing keys''), and the later hybrids are
unchanged, since the game after the hop is that of~$H_2$. That proposition
concerns a key generated from a uniform spending key; for an account key the
bound holds up to the loss of Lemma~\ref{lem:zip32-uniform}. (b)~An algorithm
that outputs $\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfSapling}}_ {\nksym^{\star}}(\rho^{\star})$
for a submitted $\rho^{\star}$ not queried before distinguishes the function
from a random function, which outputs that value with probability $2^{-256}$,
contradicting the assumption.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Receipt and spend detection}\label{sec:scan-detect}

\begin{definition}[Note state of a wallet]\label{def:scan-state}
The \emph{note state} of a wallet consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item per account $a$ and pool $P$, the set of \emph{detected notes}, each
  with its value, scope, position $\mathsf{pos}$
  (Definition~\ref{def:compact-position}), creating transaction, its
  nullifier when derivable, its memo once fetched, and a \emph{spent mark}:
  either $\mathsf{unspent}$ or the triple (height, index in block,
  $\mathsf{txid}$) of the transaction that revealed its nullifier;
\item per pool $P$, the \emph{tracked set} $W_P$ of the nullifiers of
  detected notes of $P$ not marked spent, and the set $R_P$ of
  \emph{retained unlinked nullifiers}, each with its revealing transaction;
\item for each wallet-relevant transaction, its mined height, or $\bot$ if
  it is unmined;
\item the chain state (Definition~\ref{def:scan-chainstate}) of the last
  block of each maximal interval of scanned heights; for a scan in
  increasing height, that of the last scanned block.
\end{enumerate}
A transaction is \emph{wallet-relevant} if and only if it creates a
detected note or reveals the nullifier of one. A light wallet stores no copy
of the chain; its note state is reconstructed from compact blocks
(Definition~\ref{def:compact-block}) and fetched full transactions alone,
supplied by a server that the wallet does not trust for privacy
(Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}, clause~(b)). The information retained
for witnesses is adjoined in ``The information a wallet retains''
(\S\ref{sec:tree-retained}).
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Scanning a block]\label{def:scan-block}
Let $b$ be a compact block that continues the wallet's chain, in the sense
fixed in ``Scan order'' (\S\ref{sec:scan-order}), scanned for a set $A$ of
accounts. For each pool $P$, in block, transaction and description order,
the start size of $P$ in $b$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes}) and
Definition~\ref{def:compact-position} give each compact output or Action
its position.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Receipts.} For each compact Sapling output (for
  $P = \mathsf{Sapling}$) and each compact Action of $P$ (for an
  Orchard-protocol pool), in transaction $t$, and each account $a \in A$,
  run Construction~\ref{def:compact-trial} and
  Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept} under every $(\ivk, \nksym, s) \in
  K_a(P, b, t)$ (Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys}). Record an accepted note
  with its value, scope $s$, position and transaction. When $\nksym$ is
  held, compute its nullifier $\nfsym$ (for Sapling from its position), and:
  if $\nfsym \in R_P$, mark the note spent at the retained transaction and
  remove $\nfsym$ from $R_P$; otherwise add $\nfsym$ to $W_P$.
\item \emph{Spends.} For each nullifier revealed in $P$ by $b$ (each compact
  Sapling spend, and the $\nfsym$ of each compact Action of $P$): if it lies
  in $W_P$, mark the matching note spent at $(\mathrm{height}(b),
  \text{index}, \mathsf{txid})$ and remove the nullifier from $W_P$;
  otherwise retain it in $R_P$ with its revealing transaction.
\item \emph{Chain state.} Compute $C(\mathrm{height}(b))$ from the stored
  $C(\mathrm{height}(b) - 1)$ and $b$: the hash of $b$, and for each pool
  the size $S^{P}_{\mathsf{end}}(b)$ and the frontier obtained by appending
  the commitments of $P$ in $b$, in order, to $F_P(\mathrm{height}(b) - 1)$.
  It replaces $C(\mathrm{height}(b) - 1)$ in the note state.
\end{enumerate}
Step~1 precedes step~2, so a receipt and its spend in one block, or in one
range scanned in increasing height, are both found in one pass. A
nullifier is compared only with nullifiers of its own pool (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Nullifier sets''). The retention period of $R_P$ is fixed by
Lemma~\ref{lem:scan-spends} (\S\ref{sec:scan-order}).

\emph{Retrieval.} For each wallet-relevant transaction the wallet may
fetch the full transaction by $\mathsf{Tx}(\mathsf{txid})$ (phase~C of ZIP
307, ``Client-server interaction''), which yields the memo, by decryption of
the full $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$, and the outgoing plaintexts recoverable under
the account's $\ovk$, at the privacy cost stated in ``Leakage of transaction
queries'' (\S\ref{sec:priv-txq}).

\emph{Class} (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). Trial decryption,
nullifier matching in increasing height and retrieval are specified (ZIP
307, ``Local processing'', ``Detecting spends'' and ``Client-server
interaction''); the scanning keys are those of ZIP 326. The retained set
$R_P$ and the chain state of step~3 are designed but unspecified.
\end{construction}

\subsection{Scan order}\label{sec:scan-order}

\begin{remark}[The interaction of ZIP 307]
ZIP 307, ``Client-server interaction'', divides the interaction between
client and server into four phases.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\Alph*)]
\item \emph{First start.} The client obtains the tip height $X$ and the
  commitment tree state at $X$, or sets $X := H_{\mathsf{S}}$; the ZIP
  leaves the choice open. For an imported seed $X := H_{\mathsf{S}}$
  (``Importing a pre-existing seed'').
\item \emph{First update.} The client obtains the tip $Y$ and the compact
  blocks $X, \dots, Y$. It checks that the hash of block $X$ equals its
  stored copy, a mismatch meaning that $X$ was orphaned by a
  reorganisation, and discards block $X$ without further processing. For
  each later block it validates the header if one is present,
  trial-decrypts for its notes, creates and updates witnesses of detected
  notes, and scans for their nullifiers.
\item \emph{Transactions.} At user direction, the client fetches full
  transactions.
\item \emph{Later updates.} The client obtains the tip $Z$ and the blocks
  $Y, \dots, Z$, rechecks block $Y$ as in~(B), updates witnesses, marks the
  notes whose nullifiers appear as spent at that height, and scans for new
  notes as in~(B).
\end{enumerate}
In the queries of Table~\ref{tab:service-queries} the phases use
$\mathsf{Tip}$, $\mathsf{TreeState}$ or $\mathsf{Block}$, $\mathsf{Blocks}$
and $\mathsf{Tx}$.
\end{remark}

Scanning a range $[h, h']$ requires the chain state $C(h - 1)$: its hash
for the continuity check of block $h$, and each pool's size and frontier
for positions and authentication paths. The wallet holds it after scanning
block $h - 1$, or obtains it by $\mathsf{TreeState}(h - 1)$; a
server-supplied frontier is accepted under clause~(a) of
Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc} and is checked against no header
(Proposition~\ref{prop:service-verify}).

\begin{definition}[Continuity predicate]\label{def:scan-continuity}
Let the wallet hold the chain state
$C(h) = (h, \mathsf{hash}_h, (m_P(h), F_P(h))_P)$. A compact block $b$
\emph{continues the wallet's chain at $h$} if and only if
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\mathrm{height}(b) = h + 1$;
\item $\mathsf{prevHash}(b) = \mathsf{hash}_h$; and
\item for every pool $P$, the start size of $P$ in $b$,
  $S^{P}_{\mathsf{end}}(b) - n_P(b)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes}),
  equals $m_P(h)$.
\end{enumerate}
Within a range each block is checked against the chain state computed from
its predecessor, and the first block against the stored chain state. When
a range is scanned directly below an already scanned block $h' + 1$, that
block is checked again against the chain state $C(h')$ that the new range
computes. This extends ZIP 307's single hash comparison at the start of
each phase to every block and to both sides of every range boundary.

\emph{Rule (verify first).} The scan results of a block are applied to the
note state only after the block is checked to continue the wallet's chain,
and the results of a range only after each of its blocks is. Failure of the
predicate is a \emph{continuity error}, handled by rollback
(``Reorganisation detection and recovery'', \S\ref{sec:tree-reorg}). A
malformed or insufficient field is an error of the response, not a
continuity error (``Architecture and authority'',
\S\ref{sec:service-trust}). The predicate beyond ZIP 307's comparison is
designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Scanned heights]\label{def:scan-heights}
For a wallet with birthday $B$, the \emph{maximum scanned height} is the
greatest height of a scanned block, and the \emph{fully scanned height} $f$
is the greatest $h \ge B - 1$ such that every block of $[B, h]$ has been
scanned. Under out-of-order scanning $f$ may lie below the maximum scanned
height.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Out-of-order spend detection]\label{lem:scan-spends}
Fix a chain. Let a note of an account in pool $P$ be created at height
$h_n$ and its nullifier revealed at height $h_s \ge h_n$, and let the wallet
hold the note's nullifier deriving key. If each nullifier retained in $R_P$
(Construction~\ref{def:scan-block}) that was revealed at height $h$ is kept
until the fully scanned height is at least $h$, the note is marked spent at
the revealing transaction whichever of the blocks $h_n$ and $h_s$ is scanned
first. The retention bound is the least that guarantees this: if a
nullifier revealed at $h$ is discarded while $f < h$, some block below $h$
is unscanned, and a note of the account created there and spent at $h$ is
never marked spent.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If block $h_n$ is scanned no later than block $h_s$, the note's nullifier
is in $W_P$ when block $h_s$ is scanned (steps~1 and~2 of
Construction~\ref{def:scan-block}, in that order when $h_n = h_s$), and it
is matched on arrival. Otherwise block $h_s$ is scanned first; the
nullifier matches no tracked note and is retained in $R_P$. Since
$B \le h_n \le h_s$ (Definition~\ref{def:scan-birthday}), $f$ reaches
$h_s$ only after block $h_n$ is scanned, so the nullifier is still in $R_P$
when step~1 finds the note, and the note is marked spent. Minimality: block
$h$ has been scanned, since it revealed the nullifier, so $f < h$ means that
some $h' \in [B, h - 1]$ is unscanned; a note created at $h'$ and spent at
$h$ has then lost the only record of its spend.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Order independence of scanning]\label{lem:scan-order}
Fix a chain and a range $[B, T]$. Let a schedule scan every block of
$[B, T]$ once, in any order and in ranges of any lengths, each block $b$
with key sets $K_a(P, b, t)$, and let
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item the results of each range be applied only after continuity is
  verified (Definition~\ref{def:scan-continuity}), and
\item unlinked nullifiers be retained as Lemma~\ref{lem:scan-spends}
  requires.
\end{enumerate}
Then the resulting notes, positions, nullifiers and spent marks equal those
of a scan of $[B, T]$ in increasing height that applies to each block the
same key sets, and the chain state held for height $T$ is $C(T)$. The key
sets of Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys} depend on the order only through
rules~(R2), (R4) and the last clause of~(R5), whose omitted keys accept no
output of the account under the restrictions of ZIP 326; under those
restrictions every order of Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys} yields the note state
of the scan in increasing height. The witness half of order independence is
stated in ``Witness readiness'' (\S\ref{sec:tree-ready}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Receipt detection of an output is local to its block: it depends only on
the compact output and the key set (Construction~\ref{def:compact-trial},
Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept}). Positions are local: by
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes} the start size of each pool in $b$ follows
from the compact block $b$ alone, and~(a) makes it agree with the end size
of the predecessor. Nullifiers are functions of the note, the key and, for
Sapling, the position. Spent marks follow from Lemma~\ref{lem:scan-spends}
in either order. The chain state at $T$ is computed by step~3 of
Construction~\ref{def:scan-block} from the chain state below the last range
that ends at $T$, which~(a) makes the true one, by induction over the
ranges. For the last clause, a key omitted by~(R2), (R4) or the last clause
of~(R5) in one order and tried in another accepts no output of the account
(the Remark on omitted keys above; for~(R5), no value enters the Orchard
pool after NU6.3 activation, so a zero Orchard-pool balance stays zero), so
both orders accept the same outputs.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Soundness and completeness of note discovery]%
\label{thm:scan-discovery}
Assume clause~(a) of Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}, and let the server's
best chain be unchanged while an account $a$ with birthday $B$ is scanned
by Construction~\ref{def:scan-block} with the scanning keys of
Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys}, under conditions~(a) and~(b) of
Lemma~\ref{lem:scan-order}. Let $f$ be the fully scanned height. For every
$h \le f$, restricted to notes created at heights at most $h$ and to spent
marks set by nullifiers revealed at heights at most $h$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item the note state holds a note of $a$ in pool $P$ at position $p$ if and
  only if the best chain has, at or below $h$, an output of $P$ at position
  $p$ that Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept} accepts under a key of
  $K_a(P, b, t)$ for its block $b$ and transaction $t$;
\item when the wallet holds the note's nullifier deriving key, the note is
  marked spent if and only if its nullifier is revealed in pool $P$ of the
  best chain at or below $h$, and, except with negligible probability, a
  revealed nullifier equal to it marks a spend of that note and of no
  other.
\end{enumerate}
Completeness is over accepted outputs under the keys that
Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys} applies, not over all outputs addressed to
the account: an output that its sender encrypted malformed, one that
acceptance rejects, and one that a non-conformant sender created under a
key omitted by the rules of ZIP 326 are excluded. Ranges scanned out of
order above $f$ add notes and spent marks above $h$, which the statement
does not cover. A holder of incoming viewing keys alone obtains~(i) only
(Proposition~\ref{prop:scan-ivk-spends}). The assumptions of~(ii) are those
named in the results cited in the proof.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By clause~(a) the scanned compact blocks are those of the best chain, and
every output of each is trial-decrypted under the applicable keys
(Construction~\ref{def:scan-block}). Soundness of acceptance is
Proposition~\ref{prop:compact-sound}, and membership of the accepted note
in the best chain is Corollary~\ref{cor:service-fabrication}(a). Positions
are exact by Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes}(a) and
Definition~\ref{def:scan-continuity}. The result does not depend on the
order, by Lemma~\ref{lem:scan-order}; this gives~(i).

For~(ii), spent status is nullifier matching within $P$, with spends found
in either order by Lemma~\ref{lem:scan-spends}. For Orchard-protocol notes,
one nullifier per note and distinct nullifiers for distinct notes are the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``One nullifier per note'' (\S``Double-spend
resistance'') and Proposition ``Nullifier uniqueness'' (\S``Nullifier
uniqueness and unlinkability''), under the assumptions they name. For
Sapling notes they are the Spend statement, which fixes the nullifier of
the consumed note (protocol specification, \S``Spend Statement
(Sapling)''), and the requirement that $\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfSapling}}$
be collision-resistant across all keys
(\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#abstractprfs}{\S``Pseudo
Random Functions''}), with $\rho^{\star}$ distinct for distinct positions
(\S``Computing $\rho$ values and Nullifiers''). Without clause~(a),
Remark~\ref{rem:service-omission} defeats completeness and
Corollary~\ref{cor:service-fabrication}(b) defeats soundness.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Recovery from the seed]\label{thm:scan-recovery}
Let an account have $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$ and a valid
account key (``Valid account keys'', \S\ref{sec:zip32-valid}), so that its
keys are determined by the seed $S$ and the account index
(Proposition~\ref{prop:zip32-recover}). Under clause~(a) of
Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}, scanning the best chain from a lower
bound on the account's birthday, or from $H_{\mathsf{S}}$ when none is
recorded (Definition~\ref{def:scan-birthday}), with the scanning keys of
Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys}, in particular with
$U_a = \{\mathsf{false}, \mathsf{true}\}$ by~(R1) until~(R2) narrows it,
reconstructs the note state of the account up to the fully scanned height,
as characterised by Theorem~\ref{thm:scan-discovery}. Memos, and the
outgoing plaintexts of outputs that the account encrypted under its $\ovk$,
are recovered once the wallet-relevant transactions are fetched.
Transparent funds are recovered at the addresses the wallet watches:
index~$0$ of each account by obligation (\S\ref{sec:addr-derive}); how far
beyond index~$0$ a wallet looks is fixed by no ZIP and is designed but
unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). Data that never reached
the chain, such as the memos of transactions never mined, is not
recoverable. An account with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{true}$, whose keys
$S$ does not determine, is outside the theorem, although
Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys} scans with both values when restoring.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:zip32-recover} gives the keys, hence the scanning
keys. Definition~\ref{def:scan-birthday} makes the scan start at or below
the first receipt. Theorem~\ref{thm:scan-discovery} gives the note state.
Memos and outgoing plaintexts are functions of the fetched full
transactions and the keys, and under clause~(a) $\mathsf{Tx}$ returns the
transactions of the best chain.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[A reference interaction]
The following interaction of a wallet that synchronises by ranges out of
order is designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). Per
synchronisation the wallet runs:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}(P, 0)$ for each pool, storing the roots of the
  complete subtrees;
\item $\mathsf{Tip}()$;
\item selection of ranges: those near the tip, and those completing the
  subtrees of found notes (\S\ref{sec:tree-ready}), before historic ranges;
\item for each range $[s, e]$: $\mathsf{TreeState}(s - 1)$ unless
  $C(s - 1)$ is held, then $\mathsf{Blocks}(s, e, \mathcal{P})$, the
  continuity check and the scan;
\item a return to step~2 when a scan changes the selection: a newly found
  note, or a rollback after a continuity error.
\end{enumerate}
The order is a wallet policy fixed by no ZIP, and it is observable by the
server (``Leakage of range queries'', \S\ref{sec:priv-range}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The cost of scanning}\label{sec:scan-cost}

Trial decryption costs one key agreement per compact output or Action per
candidate $\ivk$, for every block from the birthday to the tip,
independently of the wallet's own activity. Compaction
(Definition~\ref{def:compact-elements}) reduces the bandwidth, not the
number of trials. Restoring an account repeats this work from its birthday.
Retrieval of full transactions and the maintenance of authentication paths
scale with the wallet's own notes, the latter linearly in its unspent notes.

% compute/compact.py; .wip/repin/scripts/wallet_zip218.py (zips 660b3f80):
% [sec:scan-cost] 3456 target blocks per day at 25 s
% [sec:scan-cost] Orchard-pool + Ironwood-pool Actions per block <= 330; per
%   target day <= 1,140,480; compact payload <= 168,791,040 bytes; per block
%   <= 48,840
% [sec:scan-cost] Actions of both pools + Sapling spends and outputs per
%   block <= 330
\begin{proposition}[Scanning work per target day]\label{prop:scan-cost}
At NU7's target spacing of $25$ seconds (ZIP 218, ``Block target spacing'';
\emph{Consensus Guide}, \S``Confirmation times at 25-second spacing''), a target
day of $86{,}400$ seconds holds $3456$ blocks. A block holds at most $330$
Actions of the Orchard pool and the Ironwood pool together (ZIP 218, ``Shielded
pool action limits''), so a target day holds at most $1{,}140{,}480$ compact
Actions of the two pools, with at most $168{,}791{,}040$ bytes of their compact
payload at $148$ bytes each (Definition~\ref{def:compact-elements}), and
requires at most $1{,}140{,}480$ trial decryptions of Actions per candidate
$\ivk$; the number of candidate keys of an account is fixed by
Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys}. The same bounds hold for Actions, Sapling spends
and Sapling outputs together, since the shared budget of the same section limits
their sum to $330$ per block, and a compact Sapling output ($116$ bytes) or
spend ($32$ bytes) is smaller than a compact Action. The figures count target
blocks: the number of blocks mined in a day varies, and the target spacing is
not a rate limit.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The counts are $86{,}400 / 25 = 3456$, $330 \cdot 3456 = 1{,}140{,}480$ and
$148 \cdot 1{,}140{,}480 = 168{,}791{,}040$. The per-block limits are those
of ZIP 218, ``Shielded pool action limits'': each pool of the Orchard
protocol is limited separately by
\[
  \mathsf{OrchardProtocolBlockActionLimit} = 330,
\]
and every Orchard-pool Action, Ironwood-pool Action, Sapling spend and
Sapling output consumes one unit of a single shared budget
$\mathsf{GlobalShieldedBudget} = 330$, so the two pools of the Orchard
protocol together hold at most $330$ Actions. Each compact Action, and each
compact Sapling output, costs one trial decryption per candidate key
(Construction~\ref{def:compact-trial}); a compact Sapling spend costs none.
\end{proof}

\section{Commitment-tree synchronisation}\label{sec:sync-tree}

This section states the tree information that a wallet retains in order to
spend its notes. It shows that the witness maintenance of ZIP 307 forces a
scan from each note to its anchor; decomposes each note commitment tree at
level~$16$ into subtrees and a cap built from downloaded subtree roots;
shows that a frontier seeds every later witness; defines the information a
wallet retains and proves it sufficient; states exactly when a witness is
computable; and states the detection of reorganisations and the recovery
from them, with the bound on the rollback height, the safety theorem and
the correctness theorem of synchronisation.

Throughout, $P$ ranges over the shielded pools $\mathsf{Sapling}$,
$\mathsf{Orchard}$ and $\mathsf{Ironwood}$, with the chain state $C(h)$ of
Definition~\ref{def:scan-chainstate}, the birthday $B$ of
Definition~\ref{def:scan-birthday}, and the commitment count $n_P(b)$ and
end size $S^{P}_{\mathsf{end}}(b)$ of a compact block $b$
(Definition~\ref{def:compact-position}). Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}
is the server assumption; only its clause~(a) is used here.

\subsection{Witness maintenance by linear scanning}\label{sec:tree-linear}

\begin{remark}[What a spend needs]
A detected note is not yet spendable. A spend needs, besides the note, its
position $p$ and the authentication path of $p$ to the anchor of a block at
or after the note's block (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authentication
paths'', Construction ``Authentication path''; anchors as in \S``Anchors'',
Definition ``Treestate and anchor''). By the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma
``Authentication paths from public data'' (\S``Authentication paths''), that
path is a function of $p$ and of the first $n$ leaves of the pool's tree, $n$
being the tree size at the anchor. Path construction and anchor selection are
cited, not re-derived; the anchor height is fixed in ``Anchors, confirmations,
and expiry'' (\S\ref{sec:spendability}). The lemma is stated for the trees of
the Orchard protocol; its proof uses only the recurrence of the tree and applies
unchanged to the Sapling tree, which has the same depth $32$ and the same layer
convention (protocol specification, \S``Note Commitment Trees'' and \S``Merkle
Path Validity''). Everything below applies to each pool $P$ separately.
\end{remark}

An \emph{incremental witness} of a note at position $p$, after $m > p$
leaves have been appended to the pool's tree, is the information from which
the authentication path of $p$ in the tree of size $m$ is computed: the
position $p$, the siblings of the path whose ranges lie below $m$, and the
frontier of the leaves appended after $p$ (ZIP 307, ``Local processing'',
``Creating and updating note witnesses''; \emph{Crypto Guide},
\S``Append-only and incrementally updatable trees'', Definition
``Frontier'').

\begin{construction}[Linear witness maintenance]
ZIP 307, ``Local processing'', ``Creating and updating note witnesses'',
states the protocol for the Sapling pool and its commitments
$\mathsf{cmu}$; it applies to each pool with that pool's commitments,
$\cmx$ for Actions. Compact blocks are processed in increasing height, with
the order of transactions and outputs preserved. The wallet holds its copy
of the pool's tree as a frontier and one incremental witness per tracked
note. For each note commitment $c$ of the block, in order:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item append $c$ to the tree;
\item append $c$ to every held witness;
\item if the output of $c$ is accepted by
  Definition~\ref{def:compact-accept}, create a witness of it from the
  current tree.
\end{enumerate}
A witness updated through block $X$ yields the authentication path of its
note to the anchor of block $X$. No ZIP specifies any other
tree-maintenance protocol.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Linear maintenance consumes every commitment]%
\label{lem:tree-linear}
Under linear witness maintenance, let a note be created in block $h_n$ and
let $a \ge h_n$. The wallet holds the authentication path of the note to
the anchor of block $a$ only after it has processed, in order, every note
commitment of the pool in every block from $h_n$ through $a$. It creates
the note's witness only after it holds the tree state at the end of block
$h_n - 1$, obtained either by processing every earlier block or from a chain
state (Definition~\ref{def:scan-chainstate}). In particular, a wallet
restored from a seed spends no note created at $h_n$ before it has scanned
forward from $h_n$ to the anchor.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By induction on the processed commitments. Steps~(i) and~(ii) are the only
operations on the tree and the witnesses, and each consumes the next
commitment in chain order; step~(iii) creates a witness from the current
tree, which after the commitment of the note is the tree state at the end
of block $h_n - 1$ followed by the commitments of $h_n$ up to that of the
note. A witness therefore reflects block $X$ only after every commitment
through $X$ has passed through step~(ii), and it yields no path to the
anchor of $a$ before it reflects block $a$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The retained-information method]
The method of ``Subtree roots and the cap'' (\S\ref{sec:tree-shards}) to
``Witness readiness'' (\S\ref{sec:tree-ready}) removes the constraint of
Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-linear}. It replaces per-note witnesses by subtree
roots, obtained through the query $\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}$
(Definition~\ref{def:service-queries}), and a frontier, so that the path of
a note needs the commitments of its own subtree only. The method is
designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}): no ZIP
specifies it or the subtree-root query.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Subtree roots and the cap}\label{sec:tree-shards}

% compute/tree.py:
% [sec:tree-shards] depth 32, split at height 16: at most 2^32 / 2^16 =
%   2^16 = 65536 subtrees, each covering 2^16 = 65536 positions
% [sec:tree-shards] known answers: Empty_1 and Empty_32 (the empty
%   Orchard-protocol tree root) match zcash-test-vectors orchard_empty_roots
\begin{definition}[Subtrees and the cap]\label{def:tree-sharded}
Fix a pool $P$ and its note commitment tree of depth $32$, with nodes
$M^h_i$ in layer $h$ counted from the root (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``The Merkle hash and the tree'', Definition ``Note commitment tree'';
protocol specification, \S``Merkle Path Validity''). Let
$\mathsf{MerkleCRH}_P$ be its Merkle hash, $\mathsf{MerkleCRH}^{%
\mathsf{Sapling}}$ for Sapling and $\mathsf{MerkleCRH}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$
for either Orchard-protocol pool, and $\mathsf{Uncommitted}_P$ its
unused-leaf value, $\mathsf{Uncommitted}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ or
$\mathsf{Uncommitted}^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = 2$ (protocol specification,
\S``Constants''). For $0 \le l \le 32$ and $0 \le i < 2^{32-l}$, the node
at \emph{level}~$l$ and index~$i$ is
\[
  \mathsf{node}(l, i) := M^{32-l}_i,
\]
the root over the positions $[\,i\,2^l,\ (i+1)\,2^l)$; its \emph{range} is
that interval. The nodes satisfy the first line below, and the second
defines the values $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,l}$:
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{node}(l + 1, i) &= \mathsf{MerkleCRH}_P(31 - l,\,
    \mathsf{node}(l, 2i),\, \mathsf{node}(l, 2i + 1)), \\
  \mathsf{Empty}_{P,0} &:= \mathsf{Uncommitted}_P, \qquad
  \mathsf{Empty}_{P,l+1} := \mathsf{MerkleCRH}_P(31 - l,\,
    \mathsf{Empty}_{P,l},\, \mathsf{Empty}_{P,l})
\end{align*}
for $0 \le l < 32$. Each $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,l}$ is the root over a range
of unused leaves (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authentication paths'', Lemma
``Authentication paths from public data'', for Orchard-protocol trees; the
same recurrence for Sapling). \emph{Subtree}~$i$ is the node
$\mathsf{node}(16, i)$ with its range, and its completeness, root and
completing height are those of Definition~\ref{def:service-subtree}. There
are $2^{32}/2^{16} = 2^{16} = 65536$ subtrees, with indices $0$ to
$2^{16} - 1$, each over $65536$ positions. The subtree of a position $p$ is
$\sigma(p) := \lfloor p / 2^{16} \rfloor$. The \emph{cap} is the tree
formed by the nodes $\mathsf{node}(l, i)$ with $16 \le l \le 32$; its leaves
are the $2^{16}$ level-$16$ nodes, and its root is the root
$\mathsf{node}(32, 0)$ of the tree. The same geometry holds for the
Sapling, Orchard-pool and Ironwood-pool trees.
\end{definition}

% compute/tree.py:
% [sec:tree-shards] cap combining function at cap height u (1..16):
%   MerkleCRH(16 - u, ., .), i.e. the tree's hash at height u + 16,
%   tag LE_10(u + 15)
\begin{construction}[The cap over level-16 values]\label{def:tree-cap}
For a pool $P$ and a list $r_0, \dots, r_{2^{16}-1}$ of level-$16$ values,
the \emph{cap over} $(r_i)$ is the append-only Merkle tree of depth $16$
(\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Append-only and incrementally updatable trees'',
Definition ``Append-only Merkle tree'') whose cap-level-$0$ labels are the
$r_i$, whose empty-leaf value is $\bot_0 := \mathsf{Empty}_{P,16}$, and whose
combination of two cap-level-$u$ labels into a cap-level-$(u+1)$ label is
\[
  \mathsf{NodeHash}_{u+1} := \mathsf{MerkleCRH}_P(15 - u,\, \cdot,\, \cdot)
  \qquad (0 \le u < 16),
\]
the tree's Merkle hash at level $u + 16$, that is, in layer
$31 - (u + 16)$. Complete subtree roots obtained from
$\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}(P, i)$ enter at cap positions $i, i + 1, \dots$ in
index order.
\end{construction}

% compute/tree.py:
% [sec:tree-shards] lem:tree-cap on the real depth-32 tree (leaves at
%   positions 0-4, 65536-65538, 7 x 65536 + 12345): root of the cap over
%   the subtree roots of subtrees 0, 1, 7 (Empty_16 elsewhere) = tree root
%   = 0x3c6aa7540976df1e21a63fc2ed48bdbaf25c10d18c8ab7f94125bea3218de83a
\begin{lemma}[Cap root equals the tree root]\label{lem:tree-cap}
Let the tree of $P$ hold $n$ leaves, and for each $i$ let
$r_i := \mathsf{node}(16, i)$ of that tree: the complete root when
$(i + 1)\,2^{16} \le n$; the root over the appended leaves of subtree~$i$
padded with $\mathsf{Uncommitted}_P$ when $i\,2^{16} < n < (i + 1)\,2^{16}$;
and $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,16}$ when $i\,2^{16} \ge n$. Then the cap-level-$u$
node of index~$i$ of the cap over $(r_i)$ equals $\mathsf{node}(16 + u, i)$
for $0 \le u \le 16$. In particular the root of the cap equals
$\mathsf{node}(32, 0)$, the anchor of the pool for that tree
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Anchors'', Definition ``Treestate and anchor'').
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Induction on $u$. For $u = 0$ the claim is the definition of $r_i$. For the
step, the cap combines the cap-level-$u$ nodes of indices $2i$ and $2i + 1$
with $\mathsf{MerkleCRH}_P(15 - u, \cdot, \cdot)$; by the induction
hypothesis they are $\mathsf{node}(16 + u, 2i)$ and
$\mathsf{node}(16 + u, 2i + 1)$, and the recurrence of the tree gives
$\mathsf{node}(17 + u, i) = \mathsf{MerkleCRH}_P(31 - (16 + u), \dots)$, in
the same layer $15 - u$. The value $r_i = \mathsf{Empty}_{P,16}$ for an
unused subtree follows from the leaf rule, by which unused positions hold
$\mathsf{Uncommitted}_P$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Merkle hash and the
tree'', Definition ``Note commitment tree'', item ``Leaves''), and from the
induction in the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Authentication
paths from public data'' (\S``Authentication paths''). The empty labels of the
cap, $\bot_{u+1} := \mathsf{NodeHash}_{u+1}(\bot_u, \bot_u)$, are then
$\mathsf{Empty}_{P,16+u}$ by the same induction, so the cap's labels of empty
ranges agree with the tree's.
\end{proof}

% compute/tree.py:
% [sec:tree-shards] lem:tree-split: for positions 3, 65537 and
%   7 x 65536 + 12345 the 32 siblings are the 16 computed from the note's
%   own subtree alone and the 16 cap nodes over the subtree roots; each
%   path hashes to the root
\begin{lemma}[Path decomposition at level 16]\label{lem:tree-split}
Let the tree hold $n$ leaves, let $p < n$ and $\sigma := \sigma(p)$, and let
$(s_0, \dots, s_{31})$ be the authentication path of $p$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Authentication paths'', Construction ``Authentication path''), $s_t =
\mathsf{node}(t, j_t \oplus 1)$ with $j_t := \lfloor p / 2^t \rfloor$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item For $t < 16$, the range of $s_t$ lies inside subtree~$\sigma$, and
  $s_t$ is a function of the leaves of subtree~$\sigma$ below $n$; it is
  $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,t}$ if its range starts at or beyond $n$.
\item For $t \ge 16$, $s_t$ is the cap node of cap level $t - 16$ and index
  $j_t \oplus 1$, a function of the level-$16$ nodes $r_i$ with $i$ in its
  range and $i \neq \sigma$. These are complete subtree roots; the root over
  the incomplete subtree $\lfloor (n - 1)/2^{16} \rfloor$ when $n$ is not a
  multiple of $2^{16}$, a function of the frontier of the tree after its
  $n$-th leaf; and $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,16}$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence the leaves of subtree~$\sigma$, the roots of the complete subtrees
other than~$\sigma$ and the frontier of the tree of size $n$ suffice for the
path, and the wallet needs the commitments of no subtree other
than~$\sigma$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The range of $s_t$ is $[\,(j_t \oplus 1)\,2^t,\ ((j_t \oplus 1) + 1)\,2^t)$,
and $s_t$ is a function of the leaves in its range (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Authentication paths'', Lemma ``Authentication paths from public data'',
whose proof shows this for every node). For $t < 16$ the range lies inside
$[\,\sigma\,2^{16},\ (\sigma + 1)\,2^{16})$, since $j_t$ and $j_t \oplus 1$ have
the same quotient by $2^{16 - t}$, which is $\sigma$. For $t \ge 16$ the range
is a union of whole subtree ranges, and it excludes $\sigma$ because $j_t \oplus
1 \neq j_t$ and $\lfloor \sigma / 2^{t-16} \rfloor = j_t$;
Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-cap} computes $s_t$ from the level-$16$ nodes of its range.
The root of the incomplete subtree is the fold, with the
$\mathsf{Empty}_{P,\cdot}$ values as right siblings, of the carries below
level~$16$ of the frontier of the tree of size $n$, one for each set bit of $n
\bmod 2^{16}$ (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Append-only and incrementally updatable
trees'', Proposition ``Incremental append in logarithmic time'', proof).
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Acquisition of subtree roots]
At each synchronisation the wallet queries $\mathsf{SubtreeRoots}(P, 0)$ for
each pool $P \in \{\mathsf{Sapling}, \mathsf{Orchard}, \mathsf{Ironwood}\}$
(Table~\ref{tab:service-queries}) and records, for each complete
subtree~$i$, the pair (root, completing height) of
Definition~\ref{def:service-subtree}; the roots enter the cap at index~$i$
(Construction~\ref{def:tree-cap}). The roots are trusted under clause~(a)
of Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}: no root is checked against a header,
and a root is verified only if the wallet also computes it from scanned
commitments (Proposition~\ref{prop:service-verify}, item~(iv)). The
acquisition is designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{construction}

\subsection{Frontiers as scan seeds}\label{sec:tree-seeds}

\begin{lemma}[A frontier suffices to continue the tree]\label{lem:tree-seed}
Let $F$ be the frontier of the tree of pool $P$ after block $h - 1$
(\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Append-only and incrementally updatable trees'',
Definition ``Frontier''), holding $m$ leaves, with carries $f_t$ for the set
bits $t$ of $m$, and let $c_m, c_{m+1}, \dots$ be the note commitments of $P$ in
blocks $h, h + 1, \dots$ in chain order. From $F$ and these commitments alone
the wallet computes
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item the root of the tree after every block $b \ge h - 1$, and
\item for every position $p \ge m$ appended by a block at most $b$, the
  authentication path of $p$ to that root.
\end{enumerate}
The commitments at positions below $m$ are not needed. Consequently the
chain state $C(B - 1)$ stored with the birthday $B$ of an account
(Definitions~\ref{def:scan-chainstate} and~\ref{def:scan-birthday}) seeds
every later witness of the account.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i)~Repeated application of the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s Proposition
``Incremental append in logarithmic time'' (\S``Append-only and incrementally
updatable trees''), each append producing the next frontier and root.

(ii)~By the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Authentication paths from
public data'' (\S``Authentication paths''), a right sibling of $p$ ($b_t = 0$)
has a range above $p \ge m$, hence over later leaves or unused positions, and is
computed from the $c_q$ with $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,\cdot}$ values. A left sibling
($b_t = 1$) has the range $[\,(j_t - 1)\,2^t,\ j_t\,2^t)$. If the range starts
at or above $m$, it is computed from later leaves. If it lies below $m$, then
$j_t\,2^t \le m$ and $j_t = \lfloor p/2^t \rfloor \ge \lfloor m/2^t \rfloor$
give $j_t = \lfloor m/2^t \rfloor$, which is odd since $b_t = 1$; so bit~$t$ of
$m$ is set and the range is the block of the frontier at level~$t$, whose root
is the carry $f_t$. If the range contains $m$, its part below $m$ is the union
of the frontier's blocks at the set bits of $m$ below $t$, since the range is
aligned at level~$t$; its root is the fold of those carries with the later
leaves of the range.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Seeded batch]\label{def:tree-seeded}
A batch of consecutive blocks $b_0, \dots, b_k$, of heights increasing by
one, is \emph{seeded} with the chain state $C(h_s)$ of the block preceding
$b_0$ (Definition~\ref{def:scan-chainstate}), with frontiers $F_P(h_s)$ of
sizes $m_P(h_s)$. Before any retained information is modified, the wallet
accepts the batch only if $b_0$ continues the seed in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:scan-continuity}: $\mathrm{height}(b_0) = h_s + 1$,
$\mathsf{prevHash}(b_0) = \mathsf{hash}_{h_s}$, and
$m_P(h_s) + n_P(b_0) = S^{P}_{\mathsf{end}}(b_0)$ for each pool $P$
(Definition~\ref{def:compact-block}, Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes}); each
later block is checked against its predecessor in the same way. A
violation is a continuity error (Definition~\ref{def:scan-continuity}),
handled in ``Reorganisation detection and recovery''
(\S\ref{sec:tree-reorg}). Under clause~(a) of
Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}, the root of each seed frontier $F_P(h_s)$
equals the anchor of block $h_s$ in $P$; nothing else checks it, since the
abstract format does not guarantee a header
(Proposition~\ref{prop:service-verify}). The per-block sizes are designed
but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:compact-block},
Remark~\ref{rem:compact-status}).
\end{definition}

\subsection{The information a wallet retains}\label{sec:tree-retained}

\begin{definition}[Retained tree information]\label{def:tree-retained}
The \emph{retained tree information} of a wallet is adjoined to its note
state (Definition~\ref{def:scan-state}). It comprises a finite set
$\mathcal{H}$ of heights, the \emph{retained block boundaries}, chosen by
the wallet and the same for every pool, which contains $B - 1$, and for
each pool $P$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item for each unspent note of the wallet in $P$, its position $p$ and its
  leaf, and each sibling $s_t$ with $t < 16$ of the path of $p$
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-split}(i)) from the moment its range lies wholly
  below the tree size; such a node is a function of appended leaves and
  does not change thereafter (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authentication paths'',
  Lemma ``Authentication paths from public data'', items~(i) and~(ii) and
  proof);
\item the complete subtree roots with their completing heights
  (Definition~\ref{def:service-subtree}), and the frontier of the tree
  after the last scanned block;
\item for each $r \in \mathcal{H}$, the chain state $C(r)$, whose
  frontiers are the tree states after block $r$: the anchors of the blocks
  in $\mathcal{H}$ are the wallet's candidate anchors, and the heights in
  $\mathcal{H}$ are its rollback points.
\end{enumerate}
The \emph{rollback window} is the span of $\mathcal{H} \setminus
\{B - 1\}$. Because $\mathcal{H}$ is common to the pools, a rollback
restores all trees to one block. Any other node may be discarded once no
retained path needs it. An anchor older than the window is retained only by
explicit choice: ZIP 318, ``Anchor-height bucketing and cohorts'', requires
the anchor of each migration transaction to be the tree state at a shared
network-wide boundary height, so a wallet that follows it retains those
boundaries; any other retention of old anchors is wallet policy. The
definition fixes information, not a representation, and is designed but
unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Sufficiency of the retained information]%
\label{prop:tree-sufficient}
Fix a pool $P$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Let $p$ be the position of a retained note, $\sigma := \sigma(p)$, and
  $A \in \mathcal{H}$ a retained block boundary with tree size $n > p$. If
  the blocks that hold the commitments of subtree~$\sigma$ below $n$ and at
  or above the birthday seed are scanned, the authentication path of $p$
  to the anchor of $A$ is computable from the retained information.
\item For every height $r$ at which the wallet holds a chain state $C(r)$
  for all pools, with $n_r(P) := m_P(r)$ the size of the
  tree of $P$ after $r$, discard every retained node over a range that
  meets $[n_r(P), 2^{32})$, every note position at or above $n_r(P)$, every
  subtree root with completing height above $r$ and every $C(r')$ with
  $r' > r$, and take the frontier of $C(r)$ as the frontier after the last
  scanned block. The result is a function of the blocks at heights at most
  $r$, and it is information that a wallet which scanned only those blocks
  retains.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i)~By Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-split}, each sibling $s_t$ is of one of the
following sorts. A sibling whose range lies below $n$ is the same node at
every later tree state: for $t < 16$ it is in item~(a), or, where its range
lies below the birthday seed, it is a carry of the seed frontier
(Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-seed}); for $t \ge 16$ it is a cap node over complete
subtree roots of item~(b) (Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-cap}). The sibling ranges
are pairwise disjoint, so at most one contains $n - 1$ without lying below
$n$. That sibling is the root over its range, whose part below $n$ is the
union of the blocks of the frontier of $C(A)$ at the set bits of $n$ below
its level; it is the fold of those carries with $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,\cdot}$
values, and the frontier of $C(A)$ is in item~(c). A sibling whose range
starts at or beyond $n$ is $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,t}$.

(ii)~After the discards, every retained node lies over a range below
$n_r(P)$ and is a function of the leaves appended through block $r$
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authentication paths'', Lemma ``Authentication paths
from public data''); every retained subtree root has completing height at most
$r$, and is likewise such a function; the retained chain states are those at
heights at most $r$; and the frontier after $r$ is that of $C(r)$, which the
wallet holds for every pool at the same $r$. A wallet that scanned only the
blocks at most $r$ can hold each of these items, and item~(a) is satisfied,
since a sibling whose range is not below $n_r(P)$ is not yet due.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Witness readiness}\label{sec:tree-ready}

\begin{theorem}[Witness from retained information]\label{thm:tree-witness}
Let $A$ be a retained block boundary with tree size $n$ in pool $P$, let
$p$ be a position, $\sigma := \sigma(p)$, and let $(s_0, \dots, s_{31})$ be
the authentication path of $p$ to the anchor of $A$. If $p \ge n$, the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Authentication path'' (\S``Authentication
paths'') defines no path, and by its Proposition ``Membership soundness''
(\S``Anchors''), item~(iii), no valid path to that anchor with position $p$
exists, except with negligible probability, for the Orchard-protocol
pools; for Sapling, by the same argument under the collision resistance of
$\mathsf{PedersenHash}$ that the protocol specification requires of
$\mathsf{MerkleCRH}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$
(\S``$\mathsf{MerkleCRH}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ Hash Function''), since
$\mathsf{Uncommitted}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ is not in the range of
$\mathsf{NoteCommit}^{\mathsf{Sapling}}$ (\S``Windowed Pedersen
commitments'').
Otherwise the path is computable from the wallet's information if every $s_t$
is of one of the following kinds:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $t < 16$, and $s_t$ is computable from the commitments of
  subtree~$\sigma$ below $n$ that the wallet has scanned or holds summarised
  in a carry, whose range lies below $n$, of a seed frontier
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-seed}) or in the frontier of $C(A)$;
\item $t = 16$, and $s_t$ is a complete subtree root that the wallet holds;
\item $t \ge 16$, and $s_t$ is a cap node computed from held complete
  roots, carries at levels at least $16$ of the frontier of $C(A)$ or, with
  range below $n$, of a seed frontier, $\mathsf{Empty}_{P,16}$ and, where
  its range meets the incomplete subtree, the root over that subtree's
  leaves below $n$, computed from the frontier of $C(A)$;
\item the range of $s_t$ starts at or beyond $n$, and $s_t =
  \mathsf{Empty}_{P,t}$;
\item $t \ge 16$, and $s_t$ is a carry of the frontier of $C(A)$ or a carry,
  whose range lies below $n$, of a seed frontier.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Kinds~(a) to~(d) are the sorts of sibling in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-sufficient}(i), extended in kind~(c) by frontier
carries, and a sibling of kind~(e) is a retained node.
\end{proof}

% compute/tree.py:
% [sec:tree-ready] thm:tree-witness, p = 65537, n = 471098: sibling kinds
%   by height 0..31 = aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaabccddddddddddddd (a own subtree,
%   b complete subtree root, c cap node, d empty root)
\begin{example}[Kinds of the siblings of one path]
Let $p = 65537$, so $\sigma = 1$, and let the anchor's tree hold
$n = 471098 = 7 \cdot 65536 + 12346$ leaves, so that subtrees $0$ to $6$
are complete and subtree~$7$ is incomplete. The siblings $s_0, \dots,
s_{15}$ are of kind~(a); $s_{16}$, the root of subtree~$0$, is of
kind~(b); $s_{17}$, over subtrees $2$ and~$3$, and $s_{18}$, over
subtrees $4$ to~$7$, which meets the incomplete subtree, are of kind~(c);
and $s_{19}, \dots, s_{31}$, whose ranges start at $8 \cdot 65536$ or
beyond, are of kind~(d).
\end{example}

\begin{definition}[Block extent of a subtree]\label{def:tree-extent}
For pool $P$ and subtree~$s$, let $c_P(s)$ be the completing height of
subtree~$s$ (Definition~\ref{def:service-subtree}), and let $c_P(-1)$ be the
activation height of $P$. The \emph{block extent} of subtree~$s$ is the
interval of heights $[\max(c_P(s - 1), B),\ c_P(s)]$, both ends included,
the part below $B$ being summarised by the chain state $C(B - 1)$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:tree-seed}); for the incomplete last subtree the interval
ends at the anchor height. After detecting a note at position $p$, the
wallet scans the extent of subtree~$\sigma(p)$ beyond the ranges it has
already scanned. The incomplete subtree of every pool is covered by the
range from $\max(B, h_{\mathsf{c}})$ to the tip, where $h_{\mathsf{c}}$ is
the least, over the pools, of the completing height of the pool's last
complete subtree, or of the pool's activation height if it has none.
\end{definition}

The start of an extent is inclusive because the completing block of
subtree $s - 1$ may also hold the first commitments of subtree~$s$; the
positions within that block are fixed by Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-sizes}.

\begin{proposition}[Readiness of a witness]\label{prop:tree-ready}
Let a note of pool $P$ lie at position $p$, $\sigma := \sigma(p)$, and let
the anchor block $A$, of height $a$, have tree size $n > p$ and $\kappa :=
\lfloor n / 2^{16} \rfloor$ complete subtrees. The witness of $p$ at $A$ is
computable if
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item the blocks of subtree~$\sigma$ are scanned from the start of its
  extent (Definition~\ref{def:tree-extent}) up to $a$, or through its
  completing height if $\sigma < \kappa$;
\item the wallet holds the root of every complete subtree $i < \kappa$ with
  $i \neq \sigma$, including those after $\sigma$; and
\item the frontier of $C(A)$ is retained, which scanning the extent of
  subtree~$\kappa$ through $a$ yields when $n$ is not a multiple of
  $2^{16}$.
\end{enumerate}
Condition~(2) can be weakened through frontier carries: every complete
subtree $i \neq \sigma$ lies under a sibling of the path of $p$, and the
root of subtree~$i$ is not needed when its range lies inside a carry, at a
level at least $16$ and at most that of the sibling, of the frontier of
$C(A)$ or, provided the carry's range lies below $n$, of a seed frontier.
Note
eligibility, anchor choice and confirmation depth are separate conditions
(``Spendable notes'', \S\ref{sec:spend-categories}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Each condition supplies one kind of Theorem~\ref{thm:tree-witness}:
condition~(1) kind~(a); condition~(2) kind~(b) and the complete part of
kind~(c); condition~(3) the incomplete part of kind~(c). Kind~(d) needs
nothing. For condition~(3), the carries of the frontier at levels
