confirmed-spendability. The spendable set is the input state of
construction (``Transaction construction'', \S\ref{sec:pipeline}): it is
reconstructed by synchronisation (\S\ref{sec:sync-scanning},
Theorem~\ref{thm:scan-discovery}) and taken as given by fee computation and
note selection.

\begin{remark}[Spending before the nullifier set is complete]
\label{rem:spend-before-sync}
A policy that admits a note once the block extent of its own subtree is
scanned up to $A$ (Definition~\ref{def:tree-extent}), before every range up
to $T$ is scanned, departs from clause~(a) of
Definition~\ref{def:spend-spendable} and from ZIP 315, whose clause on an
up-to-date view carries an open TODO in the ZIP. The departure fails as
follows. A note whose spend was mined in a range not yet scanned passes the
policy; a transaction that spends it again is rejected by the consensus rule
that a nullifier must not repeat (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Nullifier
sets''), and its repeated nullifier links it to the earlier spend for every
party that sees it. The departure is designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{remark}

\section{Fees (ZIP 317)}\label{sec:fees}

This section states the fee a wallet pays. It fixes the fee as a consensus
quantity, defines the conventional fee of ZIP 317 over logical actions and
bounds it before signing; it states the padded Action counts on which the fee
is computed; and it states the relay, mempool and block-template rules that
apply to a transaction paying less.

\subsection{The conventional fee}\label{sec:fees-conventional}

For a transaction other than a coinbase transaction, the \emph{transaction
fee} is the value remaining in its transparent transaction value pool: the
sum of its transparent input values and of the balancing values of its
shielded pools, minus the sum of its transparent output values (protocol
specification, \S``Transactions and Treestates''; \emph{Ironwood Guide},
Definition ``Transparent transaction value pool'' and Definition ``Balancing
value'', in ``The balancing value''). Consensus requires it to be
non-negative. No transaction field encodes it, and every term of the sum is
public, so the fee is public.

A fee rule for a shielded protocol meets the requirements of ZIP 317,
``Requirements'': the fee is computable from the public data of the
transaction alone, since a fee that depended on private construction data,
such as which notes were spent or what change was returned, would disclose
that data; it scales linearly with the numbers of inputs and outputs, which
makes transaction-rate denial of service costly (ZIP 317, ``Motivation'' and
``Denial of Service''); it neither penalises padding for privacy nor
favours any pool; and it lets a small number of inputs of value below the
marginal fee be spent. A fee other than the one every wallet pays distinguishes
the wallet that chose it: non-standard fees may reveal specific users,
wallets or wallet versions (ZIP 317, ``Security and Privacy
considerations'').

The conventional fee (Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}) is not a
consensus rule. ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'', states that it is not a
consensus requirement that fees follow the formula, and that wallets SHOULD
create transactions that pay it, to reduce information leakage, unless
overridden by the user. It binds through relay and block-template policy
only (\S\ref{sec:fees-relay}). ZIP 259, ``NU7 consensus changes'', changes
neither the formula nor its parameters.

In what follows, $\lceil x \rceil$ is the least integer $\ge x$ and
$\lfloor x \rfloor$ the greatest integer $\le x$, for real $x$.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:fees-conventional] p2pkh_standard_input_size = 36 (outpoint) + 110
%   (script: 1 length + 1 + 72 signature + 1 sighash + 1 + 33 compressed key
%   + 1 margin) + 4 (sequence) = 150
% [sec:fees-conventional] p2pkh_standard_output_size = 8 (value) + 26
%   (1 length + 25 script) = 34
% Cited numbers: 5000, 2, 150, 34: ZIP 317, 'Fee calculation';
%   150 = 36 + 110 + 4 and 34 = 8 + 26: ZIP 317, 'Rationale for the chosen
%   parameters'
\begin{definition}[Logical actions]\label{def:fees-logical}
Let a transaction of version~$5$ or version~$6$ have the following public
fields of its final encoding (protocol specification, \S``Transaction
Encoding and Consensus''; ZIP 229, ``Transaction Format''), each a
non-negative integer:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\mathit{tx\_in\_total\_size}$ and $\mathit{tx\_out\_total\_size}$,
  the byte lengths of the encoded fields $\mathtt{tx\_in}$ and
  $\mathtt{tx\_out}$, the concatenations of the encoded transparent inputs
  and outputs;
\item $\mathit{nJoinSplit}$, the number of Sprout JoinSplit descriptions;
\item $\mathit{nSpendsSapling}$ and $\mathit{nOutputsSapling}$, the numbers
  of Sapling spend and output descriptions;
\item $\mathit{nActionsOrchard}$, the number of Orchard-pool Actions;
\item $\mathit{nActionsIronwood}$, the number of Ironwood-pool Actions,
  $0$ in a version~$5$ transaction.
\end{itemize}
With the parameters $\mathit{p2pkh\_standard\_input\_size} := 150$ and
$\mathit{p2pkh\_standard\_output\_size} := 34$, in bytes, the number of
\emph{logical actions} of the transaction is
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{logical\_actions} :=\;
  &\max\bigl(\lceil \mathit{tx\_in\_total\_size} / 150 \rceil,\,
    \lceil \mathit{tx\_out\_total\_size} / 34 \rceil\bigr) \\
  &+ 2\,\mathit{nJoinSplit}
    + \max(\mathit{nSpendsSapling},\, \mathit{nOutputsSapling}) \\
  &+ \mathit{nActionsOrchard} + \mathit{nActionsIronwood},
\end{align*}
the sum of the transparent, Sprout, Sapling, Orchard and Ironwood
contributions, in that order (ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'', the
contributions of Revision~$0$ and Revision~$1$).
\end{definition}

The Sprout contribution $2\,\mathit{nJoinSplit}$ is $0$ in every valid
transaction: transactions of version~$5$ and version~$6$ have no JoinSplit
field, and transactions of version~$4$ are invalid under NU7 (ZIP 2003; ZIP
259, ``NU7 consensus changes''). The term is kept so that the definition is
that of ZIP 317. The two divisors are the maximal encoded sizes of a P2PKH
input and output (ZIP 317, ``Rationale for the chosen parameters''):
\begin{align*}
  150 &= 36\ (\text{outpoint: } 32\text{-byte txid},\ 4\text{-byte index})
    + 110\ (\text{script}) + 4\ (\text{sequence}), \\
  110 &= 1\ (\text{length}) + 1\ (\text{signature length})
    + 72\ (\text{signature}) + 1\ (\text{sighash type}) \\
  &\quad + 1\ (\text{key length}) + 33\ (\text{compressed key})
    + 1\ (\text{margin}), \\
  34 &= 8\ (\text{value}) + 1\ (\text{script length})
    + 25\ (\text{P2PKH script}).
\end{align*}
Charging transparent parts by size rather than by count charges a large
script in proportion to its size, and a P2SH multisig input is counted so
(same section).
% .wip/leftovers/scripts/wallet_multisig_input.py: P2SH multisig input,
%   compressed keys, 72-byte signatures: 2-of-3 = 299 bytes (+99.3% over
%   150), 2-of-2 = 262 bytes (+74.7%)
A 2-of-3 input with compressed keys has at most $36 + 3 + 256 + 4 = 299$
bytes, its script of $256$ bytes, with a $3$-byte length, being the opcode
$\mathsf{OP\_0}$, two signature pushes of $74$ bytes and a $107$-byte push
of the $105$-byte redeem script. It is thus nearly twice the $150$ bytes of
a P2PKH input, not, as ZIP 317 states, about $70\%$ larger.

\begin{definition}[Conventional fee]\label{def:fees-conventional}
Let $\mathit{marginal\_fee} := 5000$ zatoshi per logical action and
$\mathit{grace\_actions} := 2$. The \emph{conventional fee} of a transaction
with $\mathit{logical\_actions}$ as in Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} is
\[
  \mathit{conventional\_fee} :=
  \mathit{marginal\_fee} \cdot
  \max(\mathit{grace\_actions},\, \mathit{logical\_actions})
  \text{ zatoshi}
\]
(ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'').
\end{definition}

The parameter values and their rationale are ZIP 317's (``Rationale for the
chosen parameters'').

\begin{remark}[Revisions of the fee rule]\label{rem:fees-revisions}
ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'', defines $\mathit{logical\_actions}$ as the sum
of the contributions of the applicable revision, and a contribution defined
by a revision is included in every later one (ZIP 317, ``Revisions'').
Revision~$0$ defines the transparent, Sprout, Sapling and Orchard
contributions. Revision~$1$ adds the Ironwood contribution
$\mathit{nActionsIronwood}$: Ironwood-pool Actions have the Action structure
of Orchard-pool Actions and are counted identically. Revision~$2$ adds a
contribution for memo bundles (ZIP 231), defined on the transaction format
of ZIP 248, which NU7 does not introduce (ZIP 259, ``Rationale for no new
transaction format''). Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} is therefore
Revision~$0$ with the Revision~$1$ term. The statuses of these ZIPs are those
of Table~\ref{tab:intro-zips}.
\end{remark}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:fees-conventional] conventional_fee = 5000 x max(2, logical_actions);
%   every transaction of at most 2 logical actions pays 5000 x 2 = 10,000
%   zatoshi
\begin{corollary}[The minimum fee]\label{cor:fees-minimum}
Every transaction with $\mathit{logical\_actions} \le 2$ has conventional
fee $\mathit{marginal\_fee} \cdot \mathit{grace\_actions} = 5000 \cdot 2 =
10{,}000$ zatoshi, and no transaction has a smaller conventional fee.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}, since
$\max(2, \mathit{logical\_actions}) = 2$ when $\mathit{logical\_actions} \le
2$, and $\max(2, \mathit{logical\_actions}) \ge 2$ always.
\end{proof}

A wallet computes the fee before it signs, and the encoded size of a
transparent input depends on its signature. The following proposition bounds
the fee from the unsigned transaction.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:fees-conventional] prop:fees-estimate hypothesis: a compressed-key
%   P2PKH input is at most 149 bytes <= 150; an uncompressed-key P2PKH input
%   is up to 181 bytes > 150, so the proposition holds for compressed-key
%   P2PKH inputs only
\begin{proposition}[Fee estimate before signing]\label{prop:fees-estimate}
Let a wallet fix, before signing, every field of a transaction except the
scripts of its transparent inputs $1, \dots, k$, and assign to each input
$i$ a bound $b_i \in \N$ on its encoded size after signing, as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $b_i = 150$ for a P2PKH input spending a public key with a compressed
  $33$-byte encoding, such as each of the wallet's own transparent keys;
\item otherwise any $b_i$ known to bound the encoded size of the signed
  input.
\end{enumerate}
Let $\mathit{fee}_{\mathsf{est}}$ be the conventional fee computed with
$\mathit{tx\_in\_total\_size}$ replaced by $\sum_{i=1}^{k} b_i$. Then
$\mathit{fee}_{\mathsf{est}}$ is at least the conventional fee of the signed
transaction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Signatures enter only the scripts of the transparent inputs, so the field
$\mathtt{tx\_out}$ and every shielded count of
Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} are the same in the unsigned and the
signed transaction. A DER-encoded signature has at most $72$ bytes, so the
script of a P2PKH input with a compressed key has at most
$1 + 1 + 72 + 1 + 1 + 33 = 109$ bytes and the input at most
$36 + 109 + 4 = 149 \le 150$ bytes; under clause~(ii) the bound holds by
hypothesis. The signed $\mathit{tx\_in\_total\_size}$ is therefore at most
$\sum_i b_i$. The maps $x \mapsto \lceil x / 150 \rceil$,
$(a, b) \mapsto \max(a, b)$, the addition of the unchanged remaining
contributions, and $n \mapsto 5000 \cdot \max(2, n)$ are each
non-decreasing in every argument, so their composition is, and
$\mathit{fee}_{\mathsf{est}}$ is at least the conventional fee of the signed
transaction.
\end{proof}

Hypothesis~(i) does not extend to a P2PKH input with an uncompressed
$65$-byte key encoding, which has up to $181$ bytes and needs
$b_i \ge 181$ under~(ii). For an input with no known bound on its signed
size, the fee is unknown before signing.

\subsection{The fee on padded counts}\label{sec:fees-padded}

The fields of Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} are those of the final
encoded transaction: every bundle padded and every change output included
(ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation''). A wallet therefore prices a transaction by
predicting its padded shape (Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}) and its
change before it selects inputs; a fee computed on unpadded counts
underpays whenever padding adds a logical action. This rule is ZIP 317's
and is not a consensus rule.

\begin{definition}[Padded Action count]\label{def:fees-actions}
Let a bundle of the Orchard protocol, in the Orchard pool or the Ironwood
pool, have $s \in \N$ real spends and $o \in \N$ real outputs, and let
$\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} \in \{0, 1\}$ be its flag value
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Bundle flags'', Definition ``Flag values and
expanded receiver''). Its \emph{requested Action count} is
\[
  r(s, o) :=
  \begin{cases}
    \max(s, o) & \text{if } \mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 1, \\
    s + o & \text{if } \mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 0,
  \end{cases}
\]
and its \emph{padded Action count} is $n(s, o) := 0$ if $s = o = 0$ and
$n(s, o) := \max(r(s, o), 2)$ otherwise. The counts
$\mathit{nActionsOrchard}$ and $\mathit{nActionsIronwood}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} are the padded Action counts of the two
bundles. A non-empty Sapling bundle with $s$ real spends and $o$ real
outputs has $\mathit{nSpendsSapling} = s$ and
$\mathit{nOutputsSapling} = \max(o, 2)$.
\end{definition}

The count $s + o$ is that of the pairing a wallet performs when cross-address
transfers are disabled: the note an Action creates has the expanded receiver of
the note it consumes, so each real spend occupies an Action with a
fabricated zero-valued output at its own receiver, and each real output an
Action with a fabricated zero-valued spend at its receiver (ZIP 326,
``Fabricated same-address outputs and randomized note ciphertexts''; ZIP
374, ``Fabricated same-address outputs (NU6.3)''), respectively. Every
Orchard-pool bundle has $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 0$
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, ``Chain state and pool rules'', rule~(i); ZIP 258,
``Consensus rules from NU6.3 activation''); an Ironwood-pool bundle may set
it to $1$.

The floor $2$ and the Sapling padding are a padding policy, designed but
unspecified in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}. Their only
general ZIP anchor is non-normative: ZIP 317, ``Requirements'', notes that
standard builders pad to two inputs and two outputs for each shielded pool,
and ``Rationale for the chosen parameters'' calls padding to two actions the
standard approach; ZIP 318 fixes the padding of its migration and
note-preparation transactions only (below). The Sapling rule departs from
ZIP 317's remark by adding no spend beyond those needed.
Dummy Sapling spends, which this policy does not use, are constructed as in
protocol specification, \S``Dummy Notes (Sapling)''; the dummy Actions of
the Orchard protocol are those of \S\ref{sec:pipeline-dummies}. Logical
actions count an Action once: a charge per input plus per output would
penalise the padding that the Action structure forces, since each Action
consumes one note and creates one, and the maximum in each contribution
favours no pool (ZIP 317, ``Rationale for logical actions'').

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:fees-padded] canonical migration transaction: 2 Orchard-pool Actions
%   + 1 Ironwood-pool Action = 3 logical actions, fee 15,000 zatoshi; at
%   10,000 it has 1 unpaid action
ZIP 318 specifies two exceptions. Under ``Canonical migration transaction
structure'', a canonical migration transaction has one Orchard-pool spend in
an Orchard-pool bundle of exactly two Actions, one spend Action and one
output Action that carries either change or a zero-valued dummy output (ZIP
318, ``Rationale for the canonical transaction shape''), and one
Ironwood-pool output in an unpadded Ironwood-pool bundle of one Action. Its
$\mathit{logical\_actions}$ is $3$ and its conventional fee $15{,}000$
zatoshi. The ``canonical fee (provisionally the ZIP 317 minimum fee)'' of
ZIP 318 is read here as this conventional fee: read as $10{,}000$ zatoshi,
it leaves one unpaid action (Definition~\ref{def:fees-unpaid}). A
note-preparation transaction SHOULD have an Orchard-pool bundle of exactly
$16$ Actions, padded as needed (ZIP 318, ``Note preparation
transactions''), so its count is $16$, not $n(s, o)$.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:fees-padded] one input, one payment output, with or without change:
%   Ironwood pool max(2, max(1, o)) = 2 Actions, Sapling max(1, max(o, 2))
%   = 2: fee 10,000 zatoshi
% [sec:fees-padded] payment funded by one Orchard-pool note: Orchard-pool
%   bundle max(2, 1 + o) = 2 Actions, Ironwood-pool bundle with the payment
%   max(2, o') = 2 Actions: 4 logical actions, fee 20,000 zatoshi (change,
%   if any, in the Orchard or Ironwood pool)
\begin{proposition}[Fee of a single-pool payment]\label{prop:fees-single-pool}
Consider a transaction with no transparent inputs or outputs, one input note
and one payment output, with or without one change output, whose bundles
are padded as in Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If the input note and every output lie in the Ironwood pool, in a
  bundle with $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 1$, or all lie in the Sapling
  pool, the conventional fee is $10{,}000$ zatoshi.
\item If the input is a single Orchard-pool note, the payment output lies in
  the Ironwood pool, and the change output, if any, lies in the Orchard pool
  or the Ironwood pool, then the conventional fee is $20{,}000$ zatoshi.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~In the Ironwood pool $s = 1$ and $o \in \{1, 2\}$, so
$r = \max(1, o) \le 2$ and $n = 2$. In the Sapling pool the contribution is
$\max(1, \max(o, 2)) = 2$. In either case $\mathit{logical\_actions} = 2$,
and Corollary~\ref{cor:fees-minimum} gives $10{,}000$ zatoshi.

(b)~No value enters the Orchard pool, since $\mathsf{valueBalanceOrchard}
\ge 0$ for every transaction (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, ``Chain state and pool
rules'', rule~(ii); ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules from NU6.3 activation''),
and an Orchard-pool output lies at the receiver of the note consumed in its
Action; so a payment to another party cannot lie in the Orchard pool, and
the full routing rule is that of \S\ref{sec:pipeline-routing}. The
payment lies in the Ironwood pool by hypothesis. The Orchard-pool
bundle has $s = 1$ and $o \in \{0, 1\}$, so $r = s + o \le 2$ and $n = 2$.
The Ironwood-pool bundle has $s = 0$ and $o \in \{1, 2\}$, so $n = 2$. Hence
$\mathit{logical\_actions} = 4$ and the conventional fee is
$5000 \cdot 4 = 20{,}000$ zatoshi.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Relay, mempool, and block template}\label{sec:fees-relay}

The conventional fee binds through three mechanisms, none of them a
consensus rule. Nodes that normally relay transactions are expected to relay
those paying at least the conventional fee, unless other reasons of
robustness or denial-of-service mitigation apply (ZIP 317, ``Transaction
relaying''). Mempool size limiting adds a penalty $\mathit{low\_fee\_penalty}$
to the eviction weight of a transaction paying less than the conventional
fee (ZIP 401; ZIP 317, ``Mempool size limiting''). Block-template
construction limits the unpaid actions of a block
(Definition~\ref{def:fees-unpaid}).

% Cited numbers: block_unpaid_action_limit = 50: ZIP 317, 'Block production'
%   ('Recommended algorithm for block template construction')
\begin{definition}[Unpaid actions]\label{def:fees-unpaid}
For a transaction $\mathit{tx}$ with fee $\mathit{tx.fee}$ as in
\S\ref{sec:fees-conventional} and $\mathit{tx.logical\_actions}$ as in
Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical}, the number of \emph{unpaid actions} is
$\mathit{unpaid\_actions}(\mathit{tx}) := 0$ if $\mathit{tx}$ is a coinbase
transaction, and otherwise
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{unpaid\_actions}(\mathit{tx})
  &:= \max\bigl(0,\, \max(\mathit{grace\_actions},\,
    \mathit{tx.logical\_actions}) \\
  &\qquad - \lfloor \mathit{tx.fee} / \mathit{marginal\_fee} \rfloor\bigr).
\end{align*}
The unpaid actions of a block are the sum over its transactions. The
recommended block-template algorithm adds a transaction that pays less than
the conventional fee only while that sum stays at most
$\mathit{block\_unpaid\_action\_limit} = 50$ (ZIP 317, ``Recommended
algorithm for block template construction'').
\end{definition}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:fees-relay] unpaid_actions = max(0, max(2, L) - floor(fee / 5000));
%   zero iff fee >= conventional fee (checked L < 40, fee < 250,000);
%   instances: L = 3 at 10,000 -> 1, L = 5 at 10,000 -> 3, L = 52 at 5000
%   -> 51 (> 50, the block_unpaid_action_limit)
\begin{lemma}[Zero unpaid actions]\label{lem:fees-unpaid}
A transaction other than a coinbase transaction has
$\mathit{unpaid\_actions} = 0$ if and only if its fee is at least its
conventional fee.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $m := \max(\mathit{grace\_actions}, \mathit{logical\_actions})$, an
integer. By Definition~\ref{def:fees-unpaid},
$\mathit{unpaid\_actions} = 0$ if and only if
$\lfloor \mathit{fee} / \mathit{marginal\_fee} \rfloor \ge m$. For an
integer $m$ and real $x$, $\lfloor x \rfloor \ge m$ if and only if
$x \ge m$; and since $\mathit{marginal\_fee} > 0$,
$\mathit{fee} / \mathit{marginal\_fee} \ge m$ if and only if
$\mathit{fee} \ge \mathit{marginal\_fee} \cdot m$, which is the conventional
fee of Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}.
\end{proof}

A transaction with more than $\mathit{block\_unpaid\_action\_limit} = 50$
unpaid actions is never included by the recommended algorithm, and nodes MAY
drop it, or any transaction with more unpaid actions than a configurable
limit (ZIP 317, ``Transaction relaying''). A relay policy may set that limit
to $0$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:fees-unpaid} it then drops exactly the
transactions that pay less than the conventional fee. The conventional fee
is therefore the least fee that passes every unpaid-action limit, whatever
its value. For
example, the migration transaction of \S\ref{sec:fees-padded} with
$\mathit{logical\_actions} = 3$ has one unpaid action at a fee of
$10{,}000$ zatoshi.

A logical action is not the unit of the block limits of ZIP 218,
``Shielded pool action limits'': these count the sum
$\mathit{nSpendsSapling} + \mathit{nOutputsSapling}$ of a block's Sapling
descriptions, not the maximum that Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical}
charges, so paying the conventional fee does not ensure that a transaction
fits a given block (\emph{Consensus Guide}, ``A block budget for shielded
work''). The limits add no transaction field. Nor does the block-level fee
contribution of ZIP 259, ``Rationale for no new transaction format'': ZIP
235, which NU7 deploys (ZIP 259, ``NU7 consensus changes''), removes from
circulation $\lfloor 6F/10 \rfloor$ of the total fee $F$ of the
non-coinbase transactions of a block, and the coinbase transaction claims
only the remaining $F - \lfloor 6F/10 \rfloor$ of it (ZIP 235, ``Consensus
Rule Changes''). The rule is specified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}); it
divides the fees of a block between removal and the miner, and changes
neither the fee that a transaction pays nor the conventional fee.

\section{Transaction construction}\label{sec:pipeline}

This section states how a wallet turns payments into transactions. It
defines the transaction plan and its balance equation; the pool in which
each payment and each change output lies; transfers to TEX addresses and
shielding transactions; input selection as a fixed point of the fee; change;
the outgoing viewing key of every output; the assembly of Orchard-protocol
bundles with dummy Actions in uniform order; and the authorisation of a
built transaction, with the theorem that the result pays what the plan
states. The input state is the spendable set of
Definition~\ref{def:spend-spendable}; the fee is the conventional fee of
\S\ref{sec:fees}.

\subsection{The transaction plan}\label{sec:pipeline-plan}

\begin{definition}[Payment and payment request]\label{def:plan-payment}
A \emph{payment} is a transfer of funds implemented by a single output of a
transaction: a triple $(a, v, m)$ of a recipient address $a$
(Definition~\ref{def:addr-recipient}), a value $v \in \N$ in zatoshi and an
optional memo $m$, a memo being admitted only when $a$ is memo-capable. A
\emph{payment request} is a finite map $R$ from indices to payments; it asks
for one transaction that pays every payment in its range (ZIP 321,
``Terminology''). ZIP 321 encodes payment requests as URIs
(\S\ref{sec:zip321-uri}); the label and message parameters of that encoding
are display data and enter no transaction.
\end{definition}

Construction has two phases. \emph{Planning} maps a payment request and the
wallet state, its spendable notes (Definition~\ref{def:spend-spendable}) and
its tip, to a plan exact to the zatoshi, and uses no spending key.
\emph{Execution} builds, proves and authorises the transactions of the plan
and records them (\S\ref{sec:lifecycle}); submission is a separate act.
Every choice of inputs, fee and change is fixed in the plan before a
spending key is used. No ZIP defines plans: the two-phase structure is
designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).

\begin{definition}[Transaction plan]\label{def:plan}
A \emph{transaction plan} is a non-empty sequence $P = (S_1, \dots,
S_\ell)$ of \emph{steps}. Each step describes one transaction by:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item its target height $H$ (Definition~\ref{def:spend-target}), anchor
  height $A$ (Definition~\ref{def:spend-anchor-height}) and expiry height
  $N$ (Definition~\ref{def:tx-expiry});
\item its \emph{spending account}, the account of the wallet to which every
  input of the step belongs;
\item its inputs, partitioned by pool (transparent, Sapling, Orchard,
  Ironwood), each either a note of the spending account spendable at $H$
  (Definition~\ref{def:spend-spendable}), a transparent UTXO of that
  account that meets its depth (\S\ref{sec:spend-confirmations}), or a
  reference $(j, i)$ to output $i$ of an earlier step $S_j$;
\item its payment outputs, each a payment of the request together with the
  pool assigned to it (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-routing});
\item its change outputs, each a value and a pool;
\item its fee $f \in \N$, computed under the conventional fee rule
  (Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}).
\end{enumerate}
A transparent output of a step may be marked \emph{ephemeral}: it pays a
fresh transparent address of the wallet and exists only to be spent by a
later step (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-tex}). Each step satisfies the
\emph{balance equation}
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{\text{inputs}} v
  = \sum_{\text{payments}} v + \sum_{\text{change}} v + f,
\]
the sums taken over all pools, ephemeral outputs counted among the payments
of their step. In the built transaction $f$ is the value remaining in the
transparent transaction value pool: the sum of the transparent inputs, minus
the transparent outputs, plus every shielded balancing value
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The balancing value'', Definitions
``Transparent transaction value pool'' and ``Balancing value'').
\end{definition}

Clause~(ii) restricts this section to steps funded by one account; ZIP 316
also admits transfers whose sending account is undetermined, a case that
enters only the choice of outgoing viewing key (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-ovk}).

\begin{definition}[Well-formed plan]\label{def:plan-wellformed}
A plan $(S_1, \dots, S_\ell)$ is \emph{well formed} if and only if
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item every reference $(j, i)$ among the inputs of step $S_k$ has $j < k$;
\item every output of an earlier step that a step spends is ephemeral; no
  step spends a change output of an earlier step;
\item no output, of the wallet or of a step, is spent twice within the plan;
\item every ephemeral output of a step is spent by exactly one later step.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Chaining through transparent outputs]
\label{prop:plan-chaining}
Let step $S_k$ of a plan spend output $i$ of an earlier step $S_j$, and let
$t_j$ be the transaction built for $S_j$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If the output is transparent, every field of the transaction of $S_k$
  is computable once $t_j$ is built, before $t_j$ is mined.
\item If the output is a shielded note of non-zero value, the proof that
  covers its spend (its Sapling spend proof, or the proof of its
  Orchard-protocol bundle) is not computable before $t_j$ is mined.
\item If the output is an Orchard-protocol note and the transaction of
  $S_k$ has version~$6$, its signature digest, and hence every
  spend-authorisation and binding signature of it, is computable once $t_j$
  is built; only proving waits for the mining of $t_j$.
\item If the output is a Sapling note, the signature digest of the
  transaction of $S_k$ is not computable before $t_j$ is mined.
\end{enumerate}
A plan whose steps are all completed before any of them is submitted
therefore passes value between steps only through transparent outputs,
which
Definition~\ref{def:plan-wellformed}~(ii) and the ephemeral outputs of
Definition~\ref{def:plan} realise.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~A transparent input names the outpoint $(\mathsf{txid}(t_j), i)$ and is
signed over a digest that commits to the value and script of the spent coin
(ZIP 244, ``S.2: transparent\_sig\_digest''). The identifier is a function
of the effecting data of $t_j$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests
and signatures'', Definition ``Transaction identifier''), and value and script
are fixed by $S_j$; none depends on the mining of $t_j$.

(b)~A proof for a spend of a note of non-zero value needs the note's
authentication path to the anchor (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authentication
paths''; condition A3 of the Action statement, which exempts only a consumed
note of value $0$). The path is a function of the note's position in its
tree, which is fixed only when $t_j$ is mined
(Definition~\ref{def:compact-position}).

(c)~The version-$6$ signature digest commits to no anchor and to no proof
(ZIP 229, ``Anchor commitment (version 6)''; \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Transaction digests and signatures'',
Definition ``Authorising-data digest; effecting and authorising data'' and
Definition ``Signature digest''). The effecting data of an Orchard-protocol
spend are computable before mining: its nullifier is
$\mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(\rho, \psi, \cmsym)$, a function of note
data and not of the position (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The nullifier''),
and $\rho$ of the note is fixed by the Action of $t_j$ that creates it;
the key $\rk$ and the commitment $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$ depend on keys,
values and fresh randomness. ZIP 374, ``Anchors and pre-authorization'',
states this workflow, and ZIP 318, ``Note preparation transactions'', uses
it.

(d)~A Sapling nullifier is computed from
\[
  \rho = \mathsf{MixingPedersenHash}(\cmsym, \mathsf{pos}),
\]
where
$\mathsf{pos}$ is the note's position (protocol specification,
\S``Computing $\rho$ values and Nullifiers''), and it is effecting data,
committed by the signature digest (ZIP 244, ``Signature Digest'').

For the final claim: by~(b) and~(d), a step that spends a shielded note of
non-zero value of an earlier step cannot be completed before that step's
transaction is mined, so a plan completed as a unit passes value only
through transparent outputs, which~(a) admits; a zero-valued
Orchard-protocol note carries no value.
\end{proof}

The partially created form of~(c) is that of \S\ref{sec:pczt-v2}. Fee and
change are fixed jointly: the change outputs enter the padded counts of
Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions} and hence the conventional fee, and the
fee determines the change value; \S\ref{sec:pipeline-change} resolves the
dependence.

Execution is not deterministic. The note seeds $\mathsf{rseed}$, hence
$\mathsf{esk}$, $\psi$ and $\rcm$, the value-commitment trapdoors $\rcv$,
the randomisers $\alpha$, the proofs and the permutations of
\S\ref{sec:pipeline-dummies} are drawn fresh (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Construction of a transaction''), so one plan executed twice yields
transactions with different effecting data and different transaction
identifiers. A plan fixes values and shape, not the transaction identifier.

\subsection{Payments to pools}\label{sec:pipeline-routing}

\begin{construction}[Output pool of a payment]\label{def:route}
The output of a payment $(a, v, m)$ is determined by $a$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item a transparent address yields a transparent output to it;
\item a TEX address yields a transparent P2PKH output of the second
  transaction of a ZIP 320 transfer (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-tex});
\item a Sapling address yields a Sapling output;
\item a unified address yields an output to its receiver of the most
  preferred type in the Priority List of \S\ref{sec:addr-ua} that the sender
  supports, which ZIP 316 makes a MUST (ZIP 316, ``Encoding of Unified
  Addresses''); an Orchard receiver is paid by an Ironwood-pool output;
\item an address with no receiver that the sender supports is refused, and
  so is a payment with a memo whose output is transparent.
\end{enumerate}
Case~(iv) is sound because both pools of the Orchard protocol share keys,
addresses and receivers (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Orchard protocol and
its two pools''; ZIP 326, ``Scanning for Ironwood-pool and Orchard-pool
notes'' and ``Receiving funds into the Ironwood pool'').

\emph{Orchard-pool rules.} Consensus admits no value into the Orchard pool:
for every transaction, $\mathsf{valueBalanceOrchard} \ge 0$; and every
Orchard-pool Action has cross-address transfers disabled, so the note it creates
lies at the expanded receiver of the note it consumes (ZIP 258, ``Consensus
rules from NU6.3 activation''; \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Chain state and pool
rules'', rules~(i) and~(ii), and \S``Bundle flags''). A wallet MUST NOT send
funds to any external receiver, its own included, in the Orchard pool (ZIP 326,
``Wallet key-generation restrictions''), and MUST NOT use its knowledge of the
internal addresses of all its accounts to send funds from one account to an
internal address of another (ZIP 326, ``Scanning for Ironwood-pool and
Orchard-pool notes''). A wallet that generates $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
\mathsf{true}$ keys MUST NOT send Orchard-pool funds to them at any point
(ZIP 326, ``Wallet key-generation restrictions''). This volume reads ``external
receiver'' as every receiver
that is not an internal-scope receiver (Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal})
of an account of the wallet.

\emph{Change.} A change output lies at an internal-scope address of the
spending account (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-change}) in the Ironwood pool, in the
Sapling pool, or, only as Proposition~\ref{prop:route-change} allows, in the
Orchard pool. The choice among these is wallet policy, designed but
unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}); a change output in a pool
other than that of the inputs moves value between pools, and the moved
amount is published in the balancing values.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Orchard-pool change]\label{prop:route-change}
In a transaction built by a wallet that follows
Construction~\ref{def:route}, every Orchard-pool output of non-zero value
lies at the expanded receiver of the note consumed by its own Action, a real
note or a fabricated zero-valued note, and that receiver is an
internal-scope receiver of the spending account. Hence no payment output
lies in the Orchard pool; non-zero change remains in the Orchard pool only
at internal-scope receivers of the spending account, with total at most the
value of the Orchard-pool notes spent; any other change lies in the Ironwood
or the Sapling pool.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
With $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 0$, condition A10 of the Action
statement (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Bundle flags'' and \S``Chain state and
pool rules'', rule~(i)) places each Orchard-pool output at the expanded receiver
of the note consumed in its Action. By the first rule of ZIP 326 under the
reading of Construction~\ref{def:route}, an output of non-zero value lies at
an internal-scope receiver of an account of the wallet, and by the second
that account is the spending account. A payment output is routed by
cases~(i) to~(iv) of Construction~\ref{def:route}, none of which yields an
Orchard-pool output. Let $V_{\mathrm{in}}$ and $V_{\mathrm{out}}$ be the
totals of the consumed and created Orchard-pool notes; the balancing value of
the Orchard pool is $V_{\mathrm{in}} - V_{\mathrm{out}} \ge 0$ (rule~(ii)),
so the Orchard-pool change, a part of $V_{\mathrm{out}}$, is at most
$V_{\mathrm{in}}$. Every other change output lies, by
Construction~\ref{def:route}, in the Ironwood or the Sapling pool.
\end{proof}

The following consequences are cited later rather than restated.
Orchard-pool bundles are priced by Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions} with
the requested count $s + o$; Orchard-pool fabricated outputs and spends are
assembled in \S\ref{sec:pipeline-dummies}; and the canonical migration
and note-preparation transactions of ZIP 318 are the exceptions to the
padding floor stated in \S\ref{sec:fees-padded}.

One Orchard-protocol spending key authorises spends in both
Orchard-protocol pools. Ironwood-pool and Orchard-pool spends use the same
full viewing key and the same spend authorising key $\ask$, each pool's
bundle with its own note commitment tree, anchor and nullifier set
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Key capabilities and scope'', \S``The Orchard
protocol and its two pools'' and \S``Chain state and pool rules''); the two
bundles of one transaction are built, proved and signed side by side.

Migration is specified. ZIP 2005 states that wallets SHOULD move all funds
they control, transparent, Sprout and Sapling funds included, into
recoverable Ironwood-pool notes as soon as practically possible, and that
other wallets are REQUIRED to be able to receive them (ZIP 2005,
``Proactive movement of funds to recoverable notes''). ZIP 318 specifies a
scheduled migration from the Orchard pool to the Ironwood pool; its
scheduling rules and privacy analysis are cited, not restated (ZIP 318,
``Specification'' and ``Privacy Implications''). Its block-count constants
are stated for a $75$-second block spacing; their treatment under the
$25$-second spacing of ZIP 218, which NU7 deploys (ZIP 259, ``NU7 consensus
changes''), is an open problem (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). The
privacy of the choice of source pool is stated in
\S\ref{sec:pipeline-selection}.

\subsection{TEX transfers and shielding transactions}\label{sec:pipeline-tex}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:pipeline-tex] fee(tr_1) = 5000 max(2, n) for n TEX payments (one
%   150-byte input, n 34-byte outputs): n = 1: 10,000; n = 2: 10,000;
%   n = 3: 15,000 zatoshi
% Cited numbers: 5000, 2, 150, 34: ZIP 317, 'Fee calculation'
\begin{construction}[Transfer to a TEX address]\label{def:tex-transfer}
A sender paying one or more TEX addresses from shielded funds MUST create
two transactions (ZIP 320, ``Design considerations for Senders''), realised
as two steps of one plan.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item The first transaction $\mathit{tr}_0$ spends shielded notes only and
  pays, in place of the $n$ TEX payments of values $v_1, \dots, v_n$, one
  ephemeral transparent output of value
  \[
    v_{\mathsf{eph}} := \textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n} v_i
      + \mathit{fee}(\mathit{tr}_1)
  \]
  to a fresh ephemeral transparent address of the wallet.
\item The second transaction $\mathit{tr}_1$ spends exactly that output and
  pays the $n$ TEX payments as P2PKH outputs (\emph{Crypto Guide},
  \S``The transparent layer's hashes''), with no memo and no change, so that
  its input equals its outputs plus its fee.
\end{enumerate}
Here $\mathit{fee}(\mathit{tr}_1)$ is the conventional fee of one P2PKH
input priced at $150$ bytes (Proposition~\ref{prop:fees-estimate}~(i), the
ephemeral key being a compressed key of the wallet) and $n$ P2PKH outputs of
$34$ bytes each:
\[
  \mathit{fee}(\mathit{tr}_1) = 5000 \cdot \max\bigl(2,\,
    \max(\lceil 150/150 \rceil,\, \lceil 34n/34 \rceil)\bigr)
  = 5000 \cdot \max(2, n) \text{ zatoshi},
\]
which is $10{,}000$ zatoshi for $n = 1$ and $n = 2$ and $15{,}000$ zatoshi
for $n = 3$. A single transaction that pays a TEX address and spends
shielded inputs is refused: a sender to a TEX address MUST spend only
transparent (P2SH or P2PKH) UTXOs in that transaction, and SHOULD pay only
transparent outputs in it (ZIP 320, ``Specification''). ZIP 320 does not
require that those UTXOs come from a single transparent address.
\end{construction}

The plan of Construction~\ref{def:tex-transfer} is well formed
(Definition~\ref{def:plan-wellformed}): its one reference points from
$\mathit{tr}_1$ to the ephemeral output of $\mathit{tr}_0$, which is spent
exactly once. By Proposition~\ref{prop:plan-chaining}~(a) both
transactions are completed before either is mined, and ZIP 315 exempts the
ephemeral UTXO from the confirmation floor
(\S\ref{sec:spend-confirmations}).

Ephemeral addresses are specified. A wallet based on ZIP 32 SHOULD choose
ephemeral addresses so that their private keys are recoverable from the
seed, SHOULD NOT choose them so that they can be linked between
transactions without the seed or the relevant transparent viewing keys, and
so SHOULD avoid collisions with the addresses of earlier outputs, change
outputs included; a wallet MUST recognise, and be able to spend, funds that
a recipient returns to an ephemeral address (ZIP 320, ``Design
considerations for Senders''). An ephemeral address reserved for a
construction that fails is not reused, since reuse would link the two
transactions; this rule is designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).

\begin{construction}[Shielding transaction]\label{def:shielding-tx}
A \emph{shielding transaction} is the transaction of a one-step plan with an
empty payment request. It spends transparent UTXOs of the spending account
and pays their total, less the conventional fee, to change outputs at an
internal-scope shielded receiver of that account
(Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}), with no other recipient and no
transparent output. Its outputs lie in the Ironwood pool, since no value
enters the Orchard pool (Construction~\ref{def:route}) and ZIP 2005 asks for
recoverable Ironwood-pool notes (ZIP 2005, ``Proactive movement of funds to
recoverable notes''); its balancing value in that pool is negative. Its
UTXOs may be spent with zero confirmations
(\S\ref{sec:spend-confirmations}). The value threshold at which a wallet
shields is wallet policy, designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{construction}

Shielding rules are specified. A wallet that receives transparent funds
SHOULD auto-shield them by default; it SHOULD NOT auto-shield from several
transparent addresses in one transaction, and SHOULD NOT use
\emph{opportunistic shielding}, the shielding of received transparent funds
within a user-initiated transaction, which would show its recipients the
link between the transparent addresses and the payment (ZIP 315,
``Long-term storage of funds''). Users MUST be able to disable
auto-shielding (ZIP 315, ``Auto-shielding''). Spent transparent UTXOs
SHOULD be sent only to an internal shielded receiver of the wallet, except
the ephemeral outputs of a ZIP 320 transfer (ZIP 315, ``Linkability of
transactions or addresses'').

Transparent inputs are public, so a transaction that spends UTXOs of
different transparent receivers links those receivers. ZIP 315 states that
UTXOs received on different transparent receivers SHOULD NOT be shielded in
one transaction, and that shielding several UTXOs of one address together
leaks nothing further (ZIP 315, ``Linkability of transactions or
addresses''). The same holds for ephemeral addresses, so a transaction
spends the ephemeral output of at most one ZIP 320 transfer.

\subsection{Input selection}\label{sec:pipeline-selection}

\begin{definition}[Covering selection]\label{def:sel-covering}
Fix a step with payments of total $\mathit{out}$, target height $H$, anchor
height $A$ and a number $k$ of change outputs
(\S\ref{sec:pipeline-change} fixes $k$). Let $U$ be the finite set of
inputs of the spending account that Definition~\ref{def:plan}~(iii) admits
at $(H, A)$, each $u \in U$ with value $v(u)$, and let
$\mathcal{S} \subseteq 2^{U}$ be the family of \emph{admissible}
selections, those that meet the pool rules stated at the end of this
subsection. For $I \in \mathcal{S}$ let $\mathit{shape}(I)$ be the public
counts of Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} of the step funded by $I$, with
its payments and $k$ change outputs, after padding
(Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}), and let
$\mathit{fee}(I) := \mathit{conventional\_fee}(\mathit{shape}(I))$
(Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}). A selection $I \in \mathcal{S}$ is
\emph{covering} if and only if
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{u \in I} v(u) \ge \mathit{out} + \mathit{fee}(I).
\]
\end{definition}

Adding an input never lowers a count of Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical},
so $\mathit{fee}$ is non-decreasing under inclusion, and a covering
selection is a fixed point of the fee: its total must cover a fee that
depends on it. A plan requires a covering selection for each step; any
procedure that returns one conforms. The following construction is one such
procedure.

\begin{construction}[Fee escalation]\label{def:sel-escalation}
Set $I_0 := \emptyset$ and $t_0 := 0$. At round $k \ge 0$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item compute the balance of the step on $I_k$
  (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-change} gives its exact form);
\item if $I_k$ is covering, return $I_k$;
\item otherwise let $r_k := \mathit{out} + \mathit{fee}(I_k) - t_k > 0$ be
  the shortfall, and choose $I_{k+1} \in \mathcal{S}$ with total
  $t_{k+1} := \sum_{u \in I_{k+1}} v(u) \ge t_k + r_k$; if no such
  $I_{k+1}$ exists, fail with insufficient funds.
\end{enumerate}
The choice of $I_{k+1}$ among the admissible sets that step~(3) allows is
the wallet's.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Termination of fee escalation]\label{prop:sel-termination}
Fee escalation terminates after finitely many rounds. On success the
returned selection is covering, and the fee of the step is the conventional
fee of its final padded shape. On failure at round $k$, no admissible
selection has total at least $\mathit{out} + \mathit{fee}(I_k)$. No bound on
the number of rounds is claimed beyond this finiteness.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A round that does not stop has $t_{k+1} \ge t_k + r_k > t_k$, so the totals
increase strictly. Every $t_k$ lies in the finite set
$\{\sum_{u \in I} v(u) : I \in \mathcal{S}\} \cup \{0\}$, which admits no
infinite strictly increasing sequence. On success the returned $I_k$ is
covering by step~(2), and its fee is $\mathit{fee}(I_k)$, the conventional
fee of $\mathit{shape}(I_k)$ by Definition~\ref{def:sel-covering}. On
failure, step~(3) found no admissible set of total at least
$t_k + r_k = \mathit{out} + \mathit{fee}(I_k)$.
\end{proof}

A failure does not show that no covering selection exists: an admissible
set $J$ with $\mathit{fee}(J) < \mathit{fee}(I_k)$ and total in
$[\mathit{out} + \mathit{fee}(J), \mathit{out} + \mathit{fee}(I_k))$ covers.
Fee escalation is therefore sound, returning only covering selections, and
complete only for choice rules that exclude this case.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:pipeline-selection] uneconomic input: value < marginal_fee = 5000
%   zatoshi
\begin{definition}[Uneconomic input]\label{def:sel-uneconomic}
An input $u$ is \emph{uneconomic} if and only if $v(u) <
\mathit{marginal\_fee}$, where $\mathit{marginal\_fee} = 5000$ zatoshi
(Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}). The strict inequality is ZIP
317's wording, ``below the marginal fee'' (ZIP 317, ``Requirements'' and
``Rationale for the chosen parameters'').
\end{definition}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, fees.py:
% [sec:pipeline-selection] lem:sel-uneconomic, cross-address enabled
%   bundle: rise of the padded count on one more spend (s, o, rise) =
%   (0, 0, 2), (0, 1, 0), (1, 1, 0), (1, 2, 0), (2, 2, 1)
% [sec:pipeline-selection] lem:sel-uneconomic, Orchard-pool bundle: rise of
%   the padded count on one more spend (s, o, rise) = (0, 0, 2), (0, 1, 0),
%   (1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 1), (2, 2, 1)
% [sec:pipeline-selection] lem:sel-uneconomic, Sapling bundle: rise of the
%   padded count on one more spend (s, o, rise) = (0, 0, 2), (0, 1, 0),
%   (1, 1, 0), (1, 2, 0), (2, 2, 1)
% [sec:pipeline-selection] one more 150-byte P2PKH input raises
%   logical_actions by 0 or 1; one more spend in an empty bundle raises it
%   by 2 (0 -> 2) in every bundle kind
\begin{lemma}[Cost of an additional input]\label{lem:sel-uneconomic}
Let $L$ be the number of logical actions of a step
(Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical}) and $L'$ that after adding one input.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item A P2PKH input priced at $150$ bytes gives $L' - L \in \{0, 1\}$.
\item A shielded spend added to a non-empty bundle with $s$ real spends and
  $o$ real outputs, $s + o \ge 1$, gives $L' - L \in \{0, 1\}$, and leaves
  the bundle's padded count unchanged if and only if $s + 1 \le \max(o, 2)$
  in an Orchard-protocol bundle with cross-address transfers enabled, $s + o
  = 1$ in an Orchard-pool bundle, and $s + 1 \le \max(o, 2)$ in a Sapling
  bundle.
\item A shielded spend that opens an empty bundle gives $L' - L = 2$, the
  padded count going from $0$ to $2$ for every kind of bundle.
\item The fee rises by $\mathit{marginal\_fee} \cdot (\max(2, L') -
  \max(2, L)) \le \mathit{marginal\_fee} \cdot (L' - L)$, and not at all
  while $L' \le \mathit{grace\_actions} = 2$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence an uneconomic input of positive value adds positive net value to the
step if and only if $\max(2, L') = \max(2, L)$: when padding absorbs its
spend, or while the step stays within the grace window.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i)~For every $x \in \N$, $\lceil (x + 150)/150 \rceil = \lceil x/150
\rceil + 1$; the transparent contribution is the maximum of this term and
the unchanged output term, so it rises by $0$ or $1$.

(ii)~and~(iii). By Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions} the contribution of a
non-empty bundle is $\max(\max(s, o), 2)$ with cross-address transfers
enabled, $\max(s + o, 2)$ in an Orchard-pool bundle, and $\max(s, \max(o,
2))$ for Sapling, whose outputs are padded to $\max(o, 2)$; an empty bundle
contributes $0$. Each non-empty form is $\max(x, c)$ with $c \ge 2$ and a
count $x$ that rises by exactly one, so it rises by $0$ or $1$, and by $0$
if and only if $x + 1 \le c$: that is $s + 1 \le \max(o, 2)$ in the first
and third forms, and $s + o + 1 \le 2$, which with $s + o \ge 1$ is $s + o
= 1$, in the second. A single spend in an empty bundle gives $\max(1, 2) =
2$ with cross-address transfers enabled, $\max(1 + 0, 2) = 2$ in the Orchard
pool and $\max(1, \max(0, 2)) = 2$ for Sapling. No other contribution
changes.

(iv)~From Definition~\ref{def:fees-conventional}, since $n \mapsto \max(2,
n)$ is non-decreasing and rises by at most the rise of $n$. For the final
claim: if $\max(2, L') = \max(2, L)$ the fee is unchanged and the input adds
$v(u) > 0$; otherwise the fee rises by at least
$\mathit{marginal\_fee} > v(u)$.

The instances $(s, o, \text{rise of the padded count})$ are, with
cross-address transfers enabled and for Sapling, $(0, 1, 0)$, $(1, 1, 0)$,
$(1, 2, 0)$ and $(2, 2, 1)$; in an Orchard-pool bundle $(0, 1, 0)$,
$(1, 1, 1)$, $(1, 2, 1)$ and $(2, 2, 1)$; and $(0, 0, 2)$ for the empty
bundle of every kind.
\end{proof}

ZIP 315 states that automatic shielding and automatic or opportunistic
migration SHOULD NOT be applied to inputs whose cost of shielding or
migrating exceeds their economic value (ZIP 315, ``Auto-shielding''); under
the conventional fee, Lemma~\ref{lem:sel-uneconomic} decides which inputs
these are.

The choice of source pool is constrained by privacy. Value that crosses
pools is published in the balancing values (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``The balancing value'', Remark ``Disclosure by the balancing value''), so
selecting inputs in the pool of the payment outputs keeps the amount
private, and ZIP 315 states the aim of minimising pool crossing (ZIP 315,
``Linkability of transactions or addresses''). Wallets MUST NOT
automatically combine funds across pools to satisfy a transfer, since that
could reveal the total the user holds in a pool (ZIP 315, ``Information
leakage for transfers between pools''). One policy serving these rules is
designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}): order the
shielded pools with the pool of the outputs first, then the other
Orchard-protocol pool, then Sapling, or, for Sapling outputs, Sapling, then
Ironwood, then Orchard; remove the pools that the user's policy forbids; and
spend from the first pool whose notes alone cover the payment.

Transparent sources and recipients are specified. A wallet MUST NOT send
funds to a transparent address unless all of the source funds come from
shielded pools, and this SHOULD be a single shielded pool (ZIP 315,
``Linkability of transactions or addresses''); the ZIP 320 transfer of
Construction~\ref{def:tex-transfer} meets this rule, its first transaction
spending only shielded notes. A wallet SHOULD NOT spend funds from a
transparent address in a transaction with an external recipient unless the
user gives explicit consent for that transaction, and MAY restrict transfers
further (ZIP 315, ``Allowed transfers''). The admissible family
$\mathcal{S}$ of Definition~\ref{def:sel-covering} consists of the
selections that meet the rules of this and the preceding paragraph, those
of \S\ref{sec:pipeline-tex}, and the wallet's policy.

\subsection{Change}\label{sec:pipeline-change}

\begin{construction}[Balance and change]\label{def:change}
\emph{Change} is the excess of a step's inputs over its payments and fee,
returned to the wallet in $k \ge 0$ outputs whose pool follows
Construction~\ref{def:route}. Let $\mathit{in}$ be the total of the inputs,
over all pools and ephemeral inputs included, $\mathit{out}$ the total of
the payment outputs, and $f_k$ the conventional fee of the padded shape with
$k$ change outputs, which is non-decreasing in $k$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item If $\mathit{in} < \mathit{out} + f_0$, fail with shortfall
  $\mathit{out} + f_0 - \mathit{in}$, the requirement fed back to selection
  (Construction~\ref{def:sel-escalation}).
\item If $\mathit{in} = \mathit{out} + f_0$, the step has no shielded input
  or output and no change memo is requested, set $k := 0$ and fee $f_0$.
\item Otherwise let $k$ be the least number of change outputs, $1$, or a
  larger number chosen by the wallet to split change across notes, a choice
  designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}), subject to
  $\mathit{in} \ge \mathit{out} + f_k$. The change is
  $c := \mathit{in} - \mathit{out} - f_k \ge 0$, divided among the $k$
  outputs, each carrying the change memo if one is requested. If
  $\mathit{out} + f_0 \le \mathit{in} < \mathit{out} + f_1$, no $k \ge 1$
  qualifies: the step fails with requirement
  $\mathit{out} + f_1 - \mathit{in}$, or, if $\mathit{in} > \mathit{out} +
  f_0$, may instead set $k := 0$ and fold the positive remainder
  $\mathit{in} - \mathit{out} - f_0$ into the fee; the plan records which.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Balance invariant.} Before building, the transparent, Sapling, Orchard
and Ironwood balances of the step, each the value of the step's inputs in
that pool minus the value of its outputs in that pool, sum to the planned
fee. A sum above the fee means that change was omitted; a sum below it,
insufficient funds.

Change goes to an internal-scope address of the spending account (ZIP 32,
``Orchard internal key derivation''; Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal};
ZIP 316, ``Deriving Internal Keys''), its diversifier index a wallet policy;
non-zero change remains in the Orchard pool only at an internal-scope
receiver of the spending account, the receiver of the note consumed in its
Action (Proposition~\ref{prop:route-change}). A zero-valued fabricated
output at the receiver of a spent note is not change.
\end{construction}

The balance invariant is the balance equation of Definition~\ref{def:plan}
with every term moved to one side, summed pool by pool.

\begin{proposition}[Zero-valued change]\label{prop:change-zero}
Let a step have a shielded input or output, and let
Construction~\ref{def:change} complete it with $k \ge 1$ change outputs, of
total $0$ when $\mathit{in} = \mathit{out} + f_k$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The public counts of the step are the same function of its inputs,
  payments and $k$ whether the change is $0$ or positive.
\item If change were instead omitted at exact balance, the counts at exact
  balance would differ from those with change exactly when one more output
  of the change pool raises that pool's padded count or opens a bundle
  (Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}). An observer who knows the payment
  outputs, such as a party that receives every payment, and sees the counts
  would then infer that the inputs sum exactly to the payments plus the fee.
\item In the Ironwood pool, bundles with equal public leakage that differ
  only in whether the change is $0$ or positive are computationally
  indistinguishable.
\end{enumerate}
The zero-valued output also carries a requested change memo. Where padding
already absorbs the change output, as in the two-Action Orchard-pool bundle
of ZIP 318, the omission of~(ii) reveals nothing (ZIP 318, ``Canonical
migration transaction structure'').
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i)~and~(ii) follow from Construction~\ref{def:change} and
Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}: the counts depend on $k$, which is at
least $1$ whatever the value of $c$, and omission replaces $k$ by $0$ at
exact balance only. (iii)~is \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Privacy'', Theorem
``Privacy of an Action'', under its preconditions (P1) to (P4) and its
assumptions: Actions whose private inputs have equal public leakage are
indistinguishable, and a created note's value is a private input, not public
leakage. No claim is made for Sapling outputs beyond~(i).
\end{proof}

A zero-valued note needs no witness. Condition A3 of the Action statement,
$v^{\mathsf{old}} \cdot (\mathit{root} - \mathit{rt}) = 0$, checks the
authentication path only of a consumed note of non-zero value
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Action statement''), so a zero-valued change
note can later be spent without tracking its path.

Only a step with no shielded input or output and no change memo omits change
at exact balance. The second transaction $\mathit{tr}_1$ of
Construction~\ref{def:tex-transfer} is the instance, and a change memo is not
carried there. A change note of value $v < \mathit{marginal\_fee}$ is an
uneconomic input when it is later spent (Definition~\ref{def:sel-uneconomic}),
and adds positive net value only where Lemma~\ref{lem:sel-uneconomic} gives
no rise of the fee. The wallet's policy for such change is designed but
unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}), constrained by the
following proposition.

\begin{proposition}[Fee uniformity]\label{prop:change-fee}
The fee of a transaction is public, the value remaining in its transparent
transaction value pool, and its conventional fee is a function of public
fields (Definitions~\ref{def:fees-logical} and~\ref{def:fees-conventional}),
so every observer computes both. A transaction whose fee differs from the
conventional fee of its shape is distinguished from every transaction of
that shape that pays the conventional fee. Folding a remainder into the fee
(Construction~\ref{def:change}, case~(3)) produces exactly such a fee
whenever the remainder is positive. The folding threshold is the wallet's
policy, designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The fee is the sum of the transparent input values and the balancing
values, minus the transparent output values, each a public field
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The balancing value''); the transparent sizes
and the shielded counts of Definition~\ref{def:fees-logical} are public
fields. The predicate ``the fee differs from the conventional fee of the
shape'' is therefore a function of the public data; it holds for a
transaction with a folded remainder $\mathit{in} - \mathit{out} - f_0 > 0$,
whose fee exceeds $f_0$, and fails for every transaction of the same shape
that pays the conventional fee. ZIP 317 states that non-standard fees may
reveal specific users or wallets (ZIP 317, ``Security and Privacy
considerations'').
\end{proof}

\subsection{Outgoing viewing keys}\label{sec:pipeline-ovk}

\begin{definition}[Outgoing viewing key of an output]\label{def:ovk-select}
For a transfer, the sender determines:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item the \emph{sending account}: the account that the transfer names, if
  it names one; otherwise, if every fund used comes from addresses of one
  account, that account, a rule that ZIP 316 makes a SHOULD; otherwise the
  sending account is undetermined;
\item the \emph{preferred sending protocol}, the most preferred receiver
  type (the Priority List of \S\ref{sec:addr-ua}) among the funds used:
  Orchard if any Orchard-protocol note, of either pool, is spent, else
  Sapling, else transparent.
\end{enumerate}
The \emph{outgoing viewing key of an output} is, with the sending account
determined, that account's external or internal $\ovk$ of the preferred
protocol at the account level, external for a payment and internal for a
wallet-internal output such as change or a shielding output (SHOULD); with
the sending account undetermined, the external or internal $\ovk$ of the
full viewing key of one address of the preferred protocol from which funds
are sent, for example the first (SHOULD) (ZIP 316, ``Usage of Outgoing
Viewing Keys''). The Orchard external $\ovk$ is that of the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}, Construction ``Diversifier key and outgoing viewing key'' in
\S``Viewing keys'', and the internal one that of
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}; the transparent pair is that of
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-tovk}, the two halves of
$I_{\ovk} = \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{c}}([\mathtt{0xD0}] \,\|\,
\mathsf{ser}_P(\mathsf{pk}))$; the Sapling keys are those of ZIP 32,
``Sapling internal key derivation''. The choice governs the outgoing
ciphertext $C_{\mathsf{out}}$ of every payment and change output. Its
construction, which also admits $\ovk = \bot$ with a uniformly random
$\mathsf{ock}$, is cited (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The outgoing
ciphertext'', Construction ``Outgoing ciphertext''; protocol
specification, \S``Sending Notes (Sapling)'' and \S``Sending Notes
(Orchard)'').
\end{definition}

For change the rule gives the internal $\ovk$. The specification also
permits no $\ovk$: with $\ovk = \bot$ the sender draws $\mathsf{ock}$ and
the outgoing plaintext uniformly, no viewing key recovers the outgoing
plaintext of the change, and the wallet finds its change by trial decryption
under the internal $\ivk$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Trial decryption and
note acceptance''; protocol specification, \S``Sending Notes (Orchard)'').
Encrypting change with no $\ovk$ departs from the SHOULD of ZIP 316.

The holder of an account's external $\ovk$ therefore recovers the recipient,
value and memo of the account's Ironwood-pool payment outputs
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The outgoing ciphertext'', Construction
``Recovery with $\ovk$''), and those of
its change only with the internal $\ovk$.

\subsection{Padding, dummies, and shuffling}\label{sec:pipeline-dummies}

\begin{construction}[Assembly of an Orchard-protocol bundle]
\label{def:dummy-assembly}
Fix an Orchard-protocol bundle with $s$ real spends and $o$ real outputs,
and let $n$ be its padded Action count (Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}),
or $n = 1$ for the Ironwood-pool bundle of a canonical migration
transaction, or $n = 16$ for the Orchard-pool bundle of a note-preparation
transaction (\S\ref{sec:fees-padded}). The wallet sets
$\mathsf{enableSpends} = \mathsf{enableOutputs} = 1$, the flags that ZIP 315
notes depend under normal circumstances only on whether a transaction is a
coinbase transaction (ZIP 315, ``Kinds of information leakage'').
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\Alph*)]
\item \emph{Cross-address transfers enabled}, an Ironwood-pool bundle with
  $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 1$, the normal case of ZIP 229 (ZIP 229,
  ``\texttt{enableCrossAddress} polarity''), which the wallet uses for
  every Ironwood-pool bundle. Pad the list of real spends with $n - s$
  dummy consumed notes and the list of real outputs with $n - o$ dummy
  created notes (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Actions, bundles, and dummy notes'',
  Definition ``Dummy notes''), each at an independent fresh random address; draw
  independent uniform permutations $\sigma$ and $\tau$ of $\{1, \dots, n\}$;
  place spend $j$ in Action $\sigma(j)$ and output $j$ in Action $\tau(j)$.
\item \emph{Orchard pool}, cross-address transfers disabled
  (Construction~\ref{def:route}). Form
  \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item for each real spend, one Action whose output is a fabricated
    zero-valued note at the spent note's address, with its
    $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$ filled with random bytes (MUST), since a real
    encryption would let a holder of that receiver's $\ivk$ link the
    spend's nullifier to the address (ZIP 326, ``Fabricated same-address
    outputs and randomized note ciphertexts'');
  \item for each real output, one Action whose spend is a fabricated
    zero-valued note at that output's address, a real spend signed with the
    account's $\ask$ for that receiver, carrying no dummy spending key (ZIP
    374, ``Fabricated same-address outputs (NU6.3)'');
  \item $n - s - o$ fully dummy Actions, whose dummy spend and dummy output
    share one common fresh random address, the only Actions that carry a
    dummy spending key;
  \end{enumerate}
  then draw a uniform permutation $\pi$ of $\{1, \dots, n\}$ and place the
  $j$-th Action so formed at position $\pi(j)$.
\end{enumerate}
The second Action of the canonical Orchard-pool bundle of ZIP 318 is of
kind~(B)(iii) when that bundle carries no change, and of kind~(B)(ii) when
it does (ZIP 318, ``Canonical migration transaction structure''). A dummy
output at an address independent of its Action's consumed note exists only
in bundles with cross-address transfers enabled; in kind~(B)(iii) the dummy
output shares the fresh random address of its dummy spend. A dummy spend
coexists with any anchor, since condition A3 of the Action statement checks no
path for a zero-valued consumed note. Per Action, $\rcv$, $\alpha$ and the
created note's $\mathsf{rseed}$ are drawn fresh, and $\rho$ of the created note
is the Action's $\nfsym^{\mathsf{old}}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Construction
of a transaction''). A Sapling bundle samples $\rcv$ and $\alpha$ per spend and
$\rcv$ per output, pads its outputs as in Definition~\ref{def:fees-actions}, and
its binding signing key is the sum of the spends' $\rcv$ minus the sum of the
outputs' (protocol specification, \S``Balance and Binding Signature
(Sapling)''). The builder keeps the permutations, which are not part of the
transaction, so that later roles (\S\ref{sec:pczt}) attach per-output data to
the right Action.
\end{construction}

\begin{figure}[tp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\footnotesize, >={Stealth[length=1.6mm]},
  bx/.style={draw=#1, fill=#1, fill opacity=0.14, text opacity=1,
    line width=0.6pt, rounded corners=1.5pt, minimum width=24mm,
    minimum height=5.5mm, inner sep=2pt, align=center},
  ac/.style={draw=black!45, line width=0.6pt, rounded corners=2pt,
    minimum width=20mm, minimum height=5.5mm, inner sep=2pt},
  ed/.style={->, line width=0.5pt, black!60},
  hd/.style={font=\footnotesize\bfseries, anchor=west}]
% (A) cross-address enabled: n = 2, s = 1, o = 2
\node[hd] at (-1.3,0.8) {(A) Ironwood pool, cross-address transfers
  enabled};
\node[bx=tierspend] (s1) at (0,0) {real spend $n_1$};
\node[bx=gray] (s2) at (0,-1) {dummy spend};
\node[ac] (a1) at (4.5,0) {Action 1};
\node[ac] (a2) at (4.5,-1) {Action 2};
\node[bx=tierrecv] (o1) at (9,0) {payment};
\node[bx=tierrecv] (o2) at (9,-1) {change};
\draw[ed] (s1.east) -- node[pos=0.25,above]{$\sigma$} (a2.west);
\draw[ed] (s2.east) -- (a1.west);
\draw[ed] (o1.west) -- (a1.east);
\draw[ed] (o2.west) -- node[pos=0.25,below]{$\tau$} (a2.east);
% (B) Orchard pool: s = 1, o = 1, n = 2
\node[hd] at (-1.3,-2.1) {(B) Orchard pool, cross-address transfers
  disabled};
\node[bx=tierspend] (p1) at (0,-2.9) {real spend at $X$};
\node[bx=tierin] (q1) at (9,-2.9) {$0$ at $X$, random $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$};
\node[bx=tierin] (p2) at (0,-3.9) {$0$ at $Y$};
\node[bx=tierrecv] (q2) at (9,-3.9) {change at $Y$};
\node[ac] (b1) at (4.5,-2.9) {Action 2};
\node[ac] (b2) at (4.5,-3.9) {Action 1};
\draw[ed] (p1.east) -- node[pos=0.5,above]{(i)} (b1.west);
\draw[ed] (q1.west) -- (b1.east);
\draw[ed] (p2.east) -- node[pos=0.5,below]{(ii)} (b2.west);
\draw[ed] (q2.west) -- (b2.east);
\node[font=\scriptsize, align=center] at (4.5,-4.6)
  {positions permuted by $\pi$};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{Assembly of Orchard-protocol bundles with $n = 2$ Actions
(Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}). (A)~An Ironwood-pool bundle with
one real spend and two real outputs: the padded spend list and the output
list are permuted independently by $\sigma$ and $\tau$, so any spend may
pair with any output. (B)~An Orchard-pool bundle with one real spend at
receiver $X$ and one change output at an internal receiver $Y$ of the
spending account: each real side is paired with a fabricated zero-valued
side at its own receiver, the one beside the real spend with a random
$C_{\mathsf{enc}}$, and only the order of the Actions is permuted, by
$\pi$. Red marks real spends, green real outputs, blue fabricated sides and
grey dummies.}
\label{fig:pipeline-dummies}
\end{figure}

\begin{proposition}[Uniform order and pairing of Actions]
\label{prop:dummy-order}
In a bundle with cross-address transfers enabled built by
Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}, the position of each spend and the
pairing of spends with outputs are uniform and independent of the order of
the plan: the map from spend index to Action position is a uniform
permutation, and the spend-to-output pairing is a uniform permutation
independent of it. In an Orchard-pool bundle the order of the Actions is a
uniform permutation independent of the order of the plan. Positions
therefore carry no information about the plan beyond the Action count; for
an Ironwood-pool bundle, the public data of the Actions reveal no more than
their public leakage.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
With $\sigma$ and $\tau$ independent uniform permutations of $\{1, \dots,
n\}$ applied to the padded spend and output lists, Action $i$ pairs spend
$\sigma^{-1}(i)$ with output $\tau^{-1}(i)$, so spend $j$ is paired with
output $\pi(j)$ for $\pi := \tau^{-1} \circ \sigma$. The map $(\sigma, \tau)
\mapsto (\sigma, \pi)$ is a bijection of pairs of permutations, since
$\tau = \sigma \circ \pi^{-1}$; it maps the uniform distribution to the
uniform distribution, so $\pi$ is uniform and independent of $\sigma$. The
plan's order enters only as the indexing of the lists, which both
permutations randomise. The Orchard-pool case is the single uniform
permutation $\pi$. The last clause is \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Privacy'',
Theorem ``Privacy of an Action'', under its preconditions (P1) to (P4) and its
assumptions, applied to the bundle as a whole. No indistinguishability is
claimed between the kinds (B)(i), (B)(ii) and (B)(iii) of Orchard-pool Actions,
whose bundles that theorem does not cover.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Authorisation of a transaction}\label{sec:pipeline-authorise}

The transaction carries the consensus branch identifier in force at its
target height $H$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The transaction format'').
From NU6.3 activation, transaction versions $5$ and $6$ are valid, and
version $4$ is valid only before NU7 activation (ZIP 2003,
``Specification''; ZIP 259, ``Abstract''); a transaction with an
Ironwood-pool component is of version $6$. The lock time is specified: the
field $\mathsf{nLockTime}$ is public and may distinguish a transaction if
used, and SHOULD be zero (ZIP 315, ``Kinds of information leakage''); the
wallet sets it to $0$, and ZIP 318 lists $\mathsf{lock\_time} = 0$ among the
SHOULDs of its canonical migration transaction structure (ZIP 318,
``Canonical migration transaction structure''). The reason is the one
proved for the fee in Proposition~\ref{prop:change-fee}: a public field
set otherwise than by every other wallet distinguishes the transaction. The
expiry height is that of \S\ref{sec:tx-expiry}.

\begin{construction}[Authorising a built transaction]\label{def:authorise}
Let a transaction be built from one step by
Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}, every field of its effecting data
fixed.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item Compute its signature digest $\mathsf{SigHash}$ over the effecting
  data (ZIP 244, ``Signature Digest'', as amended by ZIP 229, ``Anchor
  commitment (version 6)'', which omits the anchors from the version-$6$
  digest; \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'').
  Every spend-authorisation signature and every binding signature signs
  $\mathsf{SigHash}$.
\item For each Orchard-protocol Action of either pool, with the Action's
  randomiser $\alpha$, set $\rsk := \ask + \alpha$ and
  $\mathsf{spendAuthSig} := \mathsf{Sign}_{\rsk}(\mathsf{SigHash})$
  (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Randomised validating keys''). Every Action
  carries one: a fully dummy Action's spend is signed with its own random
  key, and a fabricated zero-valued spend beside a real Orchard-pool output
  with the account's $\ask$, not with a dummy key. Each Sapling spend
  likewise signs $\mathsf{SigHash}$ under its randomised key (protocol
  specification,
  \href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#spendauthsig}%
  {\S``Spend Authorization Signature (Sapling and Orchard)''}).
\item For each bundle, set $\mathsf{bsk} := \sum \rcv \bmod p_{\Vesta}$ over
  its Actions, check it against $\mathsf{bvk}$, and sign $\mathsf{SigHash}$
  under $\mathsf{bsk}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The binding
  signature''); the Sapling $\mathsf{bsk}$ is that of
  Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}.
\item Sign each transparent input under the key of its address, by the
  scheme of the \emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The transparent layer's schemes:
  ECDSA and BIP-340 over secp256k1'', over the signature digest of that
  input (ZIP 244, ``S.2: transparent\_sig\_digest'').
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Keys.} One Orchard-protocol $\ask$ serves both scopes and both pools
(Construction~\ref{def:zip32-internal}; ZIP 32, ``Orchard internal key
derivation''). One Sapling $\ask$ serves both scopes, the internal scope
differing in $\mathsf{nsk}$, which enters the proof and the nullifier, not
the signature (ZIP 32, ``Deriving a Sapling internal spending key''), so a
Sapling spend is added under the scope of its note. Each transparent input
needs the key of its own address.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Scope of the signature digest]\label{lem:authorise-order}
The signature digests of version-$5$ and version-$6$ transactions commit to
the effecting data and, for transparent inputs, to the values and scripts of
the coins spent (ZIP 244, ``S.2: transparent\_sig\_digest''), excluding
proofs and signatures, and the
version-$6$ digest also excludes the anchors. Hence steps~(2) to~(4) of
Construction~\ref{def:authorise} need no proof; proofs and signatures can be
computed in either order; in version~$6$, signing can precede the choice of
anchor; and the transaction identifier is unchanged by proofs, by
signatures and, in version~$6$, by anchors.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'',
Definition ``Signature digest'', $\mathsf{SigHash}$ has the header and shielded
children of $\mathsf{txid}$ and the transparent signature digest as its
children, all computed over effecting data and, for the transparent signature
digest, the values and scripts of the coins spent; proofs, signatures and
transparent scripts are authorising data, under the authorising-data digest
(Definition ``Authorising-data digest; effecting and authorising data''). ZIP
229, ``Anchor commitment (version 6)'', moves the anchors to the authorising
data in version~$6$; in version~$5$ they are effecting data (ZIP 244,
``Signature Digest''). A step of Construction~\ref{def:authorise} computes a
signature over $\mathsf{SigHash}$ from keys and randomisers, and the binding key
from the $\rcv$ values, none of which is a proof, so the order of proving and
signing is free. The transaction identifier is a function of the effecting data
alone (Definition ``Transaction identifier''). The multi-party form of the lemma
is that of \S\ref{sec:pczt-combine}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Payment integrity]\label{thm:pipeline-integrity}
Let a well-formed plan (Definition~\ref{def:plan-wellformed}) be executed by
Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly} and Construction~\ref{def:authorise},
with Orchard-protocol keys generated with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} =
\mathsf{false}$. In each resulting transaction:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item each payment is paid by exactly one output, in the pool that
  Construction~\ref{def:route} assigns, to the routed receiver, of the
  payment's value and with its memo, none for a transparent output;
\item every other output is a change output of
  Construction~\ref{def:change}, an ephemeral output of the step, or a
  zero-valued dummy or fabricated output;
\item the fee equals the planned fee: the conventional fee of the final
  shape, or, where Construction~\ref{def:change} folds a remainder into it,
  that fee plus the remainder.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, for a version-$6$ transaction whose signing keys meet hypotheses
(H) and (S) of \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'',
Proposition ``Bundle binding'', and under the assumptions of that proposition
and of its Theorem ``Spend authority'' (\S``Authorisation of spends''), no other
party produces, except with negligible probability, an accepted transaction that
carries any of the transaction's Orchard-protocol randomised validating keys
$\rk$ with different effecting data. The theorem claims content, not inclusion:
inclusion further requires that no spent nullifier be revealed first, that the
anchor block remain on the best chain and that inclusion occur by the expiry
height, none of which the wallet controls (\S\ref{sec:lifecycle}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i)~and~(ii). By Definition~\ref{def:plan} each payment is one output of
its step, placed by Construction~\ref{def:route}, and the only other outputs
of a step are its change and ephemeral outputs.
Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly} adds only notes of value $0$: dummy
created notes, fabricated outputs and the outputs of fully dummy Actions.
The padding therefore creates no output of non-zero value, and each real
output keeps the receiver, value and memo that the plan gives it.

(iii)~In the built transaction the fee is the value remaining in the
transparent transaction value pool, the transparent inputs minus the
transparent outputs plus the balancing value of each shielded pool, and each
balancing value is the value consumed minus the value created in its pool.
Since dummy and fabricated notes have value $0$, the remaining value is the
