total of the step's inputs minus the total of its payment, ephemeral and
change outputs, which is the planned fee by the balance equation of
Definition~\ref{def:plan} and the balance invariant of
Construction~\ref{def:change}; the planned fee is $f_k$, or $f_0$ plus the
folded remainder.

Binding. Every signature of the transaction signs $\mathsf{SigHash}$
(Construction~\ref{def:authorise}), which covers the effecting data
(Lemma~\ref{lem:authorise-order}). By \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction
digests and signatures'', Proposition ``Bundle binding'', an accepted
transaction that carries some $\rk_i$ of the transaction carries the same
effecting data, except with negligible probability; Theorem ``Spend authority''
excludes a valid signature under a key of the wallet on a digest that the wallet
did not sign. For transparent and Sapling signatures the commitment to the
effecting data is that of ZIP 244; no lower volume proves the unforgeability of
those schemes, and none is claimed here.
\end{proof}

The wallet records each of its shielded outputs from the data it built the
output with: the address, the value, $\mathsf{rseed}$ and the memo, and,
for an Orchard-protocol output, $\rho$, the nullifier of the spend of its
Action. For such an output,
\[
  \mathsf{esk} = \mathrm{ToScalar}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{rseed}}
  ([\mathtt{0x04}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rho))\bigr),
\]
and the note plaintext follow without any viewing key (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Encryption to the recipient'', and \S``The outgoing
ciphertext'', Construction ``Recovery with $\ovk$''). A fabricated zero-valued
Orchard-pool output at a spent note's receiver carries a random
$C_{\mathsf{enc}}$, which trial decryption under any $\ivk$ rejects except with
negligible probability (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Encryption to the
recipient'', Assumption ``Confidentiality and integrity of ChaCha20-Poly1305'';
ZIP 326, ``Fabricated same-address outputs and
randomized note ciphertexts''). Non-zero Orchard-pool change lies only at
internal-scope receivers (Proposition~\ref{prop:route-change}), so scanning
finds it under the internal $\ivk$ (Definition~\ref{def:scan-keys}), and no
external $\ivk$ is needed for it (ZIP 326, ``Wallet key-generation
restrictions'').

\section{Partially created transactions (PCZT)}\label{sec:pczt}

This section states the protocol by which the construction, proving and
authorisation of one transaction are divided among several parties (ZIP
374). It defines the partially created transaction, its versions and the
kinds of its fields; the roles that act on it and the modification state
they maintain; its fields, its canonical empty bundles and its encoding; the
version-$2$ rules for deferred anchors and the Ironwood bundle; construction
and IO finalisation; signing, with the theorem that a Signer's signatures
authorise exactly what it checked; proving; combining and redaction, with
the order independence of proving and signing and the data a PCZT
discloses; and spend finalisation and extraction.

\subsection{The PCZT and its requirements}\label{sec:pczt-object}

\begin{definition}[Partially created transaction]\label{def:pczt}
A \emph{partially created transaction} (PCZT) of one transaction is a value
of the typed record of \S\ref{sec:pczt-fields} for one PCZT version,
exchanged among the parties to the creation of that transaction. It carries
the data each role needs for its step (adding inputs and outputs, proving,
signing, extracting) and accumulates proofs and signatures until the
transaction is complete; since it carries all data a signer needs, a signer
can be offline. A PCZT consists of a \emph{Global} record and one
\emph{bundle} per protocol: transparent, Sapling, Orchard and, from
version~$2$, Ironwood. Every bundle is present in every PCZT, empty bundles
included, because metadata of a protocol may have to be held before it is
known whether that protocol has inputs or outputs (ZIP 374, ``Abstract'',
``Specification'' and ``Structure'').
\end{definition}

The PCZT protocol is specified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}) in ZIP
374, Revision~$0$, whose status is that of Table~\ref{tab:intro-zips}; no
consensus rule depends on it. It covers transactions of versions~$5$
and~$6$ only: it supports neither Sprout nor version~$4$ or earlier, since
version~$4$ predates the ZIP 244 digest and several of the role separations
of this section, the independence of proving from signing in particular,
fail for it (ZIP 374, ``Specification''). The protocol serves signing
devices that hold spending keys but can neither compute proofs nor hold a
whole transaction; proof delegation, the prover needing note data and the
full viewing key but no spending key; transactions with several signers
(threshold signing, the transparent multisignature wallets of ZIP 48) or
several contributors of inputs, whose parallel contributions are merged;
and, for version~$6$, pre-authorisation, in which the signatures precede the
choice of anchors, the witnesses and the proofs (ZIP 374, ``Motivation'').

ZIP 374, ``Requirements'', states six requirements, recorded here as the
ZIP's and not proved as such:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(R\arabic*)]
\item every intermediate state of the creation of a version-$5$ or
  version-$6$ transaction is representable, and adding inputs and outputs,
  finalising the input and output set, attaching lookup data, proving,
  signing, merging parallel contributions and extracting are each
  performable by distinct entities, in an order constrained only by data
  dependencies;
\item no spending key material is required, the keys of dummy spends
  excepted, which authorise nothing of value and are removable before any
  Signer sees the PCZT;
\item a Signer determines from the PCZT alone what it authorises (amounts,
  recipients, and the correspondence between the fields it signs and the
  values shown to its user), and data a Signer does not need is removable;
\item merging two PCZTs of the same transaction is deterministic and fails
  on any conflict;
\item for version~$6$, signatures can be produced while anchors, witnesses
  and proofs are absent, and supplying them later leaves the signatures
  valid;
\item the encoding is compact and strictly versioned, so that no data is
  read under another version's rules.
\end{enumerate}
Requirement~(R3) is made a theorem in \S\ref{sec:pczt-signing}, (R5) a
proposition in \S\ref{sec:pczt-v2} and (R4) a construction in
\S\ref{sec:pczt-combine}.

\begin{definition}[PCZT versions]\label{def:pczt-versions}
Version~$1$ represents transactions of version~$5$ (ZIP 225); version~$2$
represents transactions of versions~$5$ and~$6$ (ZIP 229). The PCZT version
does not fix the transaction version: a version-$2$ PCZT carries a
transaction of version~$5$ or~$6$, subject to the per-field rules, and a
version-$1$ PCZT only one of version~$5$. Relative to version~$1$,
version~$2$ adds the Ironwood bundle; a note version in
$\{\mathsf{V2}, \mathsf{V3}\}$ per Orchard-protocol bundle; anchors that may
be absent; and an encoding that writes a bundle equal to its slot's
canonical empty bundle as absent (ZIP 374, ``Versioning''). The fields are
made exact in \S\ref{sec:pczt-fields} and the encoding in
\S\ref{sec:pczt-encoding}.
\end{definition}

No field can be added within a version: the encoding is positional, and a
parser cannot skip a field it does not know. A PCZT has no non-proprietary
key--value maps, so every field of a version is known in advance and a
Signer's view of a PCZT is complete; the proprietary maps are the only
extension point within a version, and MUST be used for internal processes
only. The format evolves by new versions, which the four-byte version of
the header distinguishes. Implementations SHOULD parse every specified
version and SHOULD emit the oldest version able to represent a PCZT's state
when interoperating with entities that may lack the newest; conversion
between versions is lossless exactly when the target version represents the
state (ZIP 374, ``Extensibility'' and ``Proprietary Use fields''). The
states that version~$1$ cannot represent are listed in
\S\ref{sec:pczt-encoding}.

\begin{definition}[Field kinds of a PCZT]\label{def:pczt-kinds}
Every field of a PCZT is of one of three kinds (ZIP 374, ``Structure''):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{effecting data}, required fields that are part of the final
  transaction and are always present, committed to by the signature digest
  (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'');
\item \emph{authorising data}, initially absent and filled as roles act:
  proofs, signatures, binding signing keys and, in a transaction of
  version~$6$, the anchors;
\item \emph{context data}, which enables roles to act and never enters the
  final transaction: note openings, keys, witnesses, derivation records and
  address strings.
\end{enumerate}
In a transaction of version~$5$ the anchors are effecting data (ZIP 374,
``Anchors and pre-authorization'').
\end{definition}

The signature digest commits to every non-transparent effecting field as
soon as any input is signed, so no shielded analogue of the transparent
hash types that leave inputs or outputs open exists. The transparent and
the shielded modifiable flags of \S\ref{sec:pczt-roles} are therefore kept
apart, so that a purely transparent PCZT behaves as a partially signed
Bitcoin transaction of BIP 174 and BIP 370 does (ZIP 374, ``Separate
Shielded Modifiable Flag from Transparent Modifiable Flags'').

\subsection{Roles and the modification state}\label{sec:pczt-roles}

\begin{definition}[Roles]\label{def:pczt-roles}
A \emph{role} is a partial map from PCZTs to PCZTs, undefined where the role
fails; a role that draws randomness is a family of such maps indexed by its
random coins, and the Combiner maps a finite set of PCZTs to one. The roles,
with the parties that may execute them (ZIP 374, ``Specification'' and
``Roles''), are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Creator} (a single entity): chooses the transaction version and
  consensus branch identifier, and sets the Global fields and the empty
  bundles;
\item \emph{Constructor}: adds transparent inputs and outputs, Sapling
  spends and outputs, and Orchard-protocol Actions;
\item \emph{IO Finalizer} (anyone): declares the input and output set
  final, computes the binding signing keys and signs dummy spends;
\item \emph{Updater} (anyone): adds data later roles need, such as
  derivation records, full viewing keys, witnesses and, for version~$6$,
  anchors;
\item \emph{Redactor} (anyone): removes data later roles do not need;
\item \emph{Prover} (a holder of the note data): adds proofs;
\item \emph{Signer} (a holder of a spending key): adds signatures;
\item \emph{Combiner} (anyone): merges PCZTs;
\item \emph{Spend Finalizer} (anyone): assembles transparent unlocking
  scripts;
\item \emph{Transaction Extractor} (anyone): checks completeness and
  produces the transaction.
\end{enumerate}
The lifecycle runs the Creator, the Constructors, the IO Finalizer and the
Updaters, then the Provers and the Signers, the Combiners and the
Redactors, and last the Spend Finalizer and the Transaction Extractor. A
\emph{Verifier} is not a role: it is an inspection of a PCZT's internal
consistency, for example the relations of \S\ref{sec:pczt-signing},
performed on behalf of an entity, such as a hardware signer, that trusts it
and cannot perform the checks itself (ZIP 374, ``Verifier'').
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Modification state]\label{def:pczt-modifiable}
The Global field $\mathsf{tx\_}\allowbreak\mathsf{modifiable} \in \{0,1\}^8$,
bits numbered from
the least significant, has the following bits (ZIP 374, ``Global'' and
``Combiner''):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item bit~$0$, transparent inputs modifiable, and bit~$1$, transparent
  outputs modifiable: each set by the Creator, checked, and possibly
  cleared, by a Constructor before it adds an input or output of its kind,
  cleared by the IO Finalizer when shielded spends or outputs exist, and
  merged by AND;
\item bit~$2$, a signature with hash type $\mathsf{SIGHASH\_SINGLE}$ is
  present: cleared by the Creator, set by a Signer that adds such a
  signature, read by a Constructor, which then preserves the pairing of each
  such input with its output, and merged by OR;
\item bits~$3$ to~$6$: zero, which ZIP 374 requires in version~$1$ and the
  Combiner requires of every PCZT it merges;
\item bit~$7$, shielded modifiable: set by the Creator, checked, and
  possibly cleared, by a Constructor before it adds a shielded spend or
  output, cleared by the IO Finalizer when shielded spends or outputs exist
  and by every Signer, and merged by AND.
\end{enumerate}
The Creator's value is therefore $1000\,0011$ in binary.
\end{definition}

Signing freezes the effects. After adding a shielded signature a Signer
clears bits~$0$, $1$ and~$7$: every shielded signature, spend-authorisation
and binding alike, is on the one signature digest, which commits to all
effecting data (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and
signatures'', Proposition ``Bundle binding''), and no shielded hash type
leaves an input or output open. After adding a transparent signature it
clears bit~$0$ unless the hash type has $\mathsf{ANYONECANPAY}$, clears
bit~$1$ unless its base type is $\mathsf{NONE}$, sets bit~$2$ if its base
type is $\mathsf{SINGLE}$, with or without $\mathsf{ANYONECANPAY}$, and
clears bit~$7$ (ZIP 374, ``Global'' and ``Signer'').

A wallet executing a plan (Definition~\ref{def:plan}) acts as Creator,
Constructor, IO Finalizer and Updater. It builds the bundles by
Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly} and then, as Updater, attaches to
each non-dummy Orchard-pool, Ironwood-pool and Sapling spend the derivation
record of \S\ref{sec:pczt-fields}, with the seed fingerprint of
Construction~\ref{def:zip32-seedfp} and the path
$(32', \mathit{coin\_type}', \mathit{account}')$ of
Definition~\ref{def:zip32-account}; to each transparent input the BIP 44
path $(44', \mathit{coin\_type}', \mathit{account}', \mathit{scope},
\mathit{index})$, the last two components non-hardened; and to each output
that pays a payment of the plan (Definition~\ref{def:plan-payment}) that
payment's address string as $\mathsf{user\_address}$, which a Signer checks
(\S\ref{sec:pczt-signing}) (ZIP 374, ``Updater'' and ``Zip32Derivation'';
ZIP 32; BIP 44).

\begin{figure}[tp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\scriptsize, >={Stealth[length=1.6mm]},
  rl/.style={draw=#1, fill=#1, fill opacity=0.12, text opacity=1,
    line width=0.6pt, rounded corners=1.5pt, align=left,
    text width=64mm, inner sep=3pt},
  sd/.style={rl=#1, text width=62mm},
  ed/.style={->, line width=0.5pt, black!60}]
\node[rl=black!45] (cr) at (0,0) {\textbf{Creator} (single entity).
  Writes Global and the canonical empty bundles;
  $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable} := 1000\,0011$; anchors if v5.};
\node[rl=black!45, below=3mm of cr] (co) {\textbf{Constructor}. Checks
  bits 0, 1, 7; writes inputs, outputs, Actions: effecting data, $\rcv$,
  $\alpha$, note data, dummy keys.};
\node[rl=tierfull, below=3mm of co] (io) {\textbf{IO Finalizer}. Needs every
  $\rcv$ and dummy key; writes $\mathsf{bsk}$ and dummy-spend signatures;
  clears bits 0, 1, 7 and the dummy keys.};
\node[rl=black!45, below=3mm of io] (up) {\textbf{Updater}. Writes
  derivation records, $\fvk$, witnesses, $\mathsf{user\_address}$; anchors

\node[sd=tierin, below left=6mm and -28mm of up] (pr) {\textbf{Prover}.

\draw[ed] (si.south) -- (cb.north east);

Red marks the one role that needs the spend authorising key $\ask$, amber

\subsection{Fields}\label{sec:pczt-fields}

\begin{definition}[PCZT fields]\label{def:pczt-fields}
\emph{Types.} The types $\mathsf{u8}$, $\mathsf{u32}$ and $\mathsf{u64}$
are the unsigned integers of that width, $\mathsf{i128}$ the signed
integers of $128$ bits and $\mathsf{bool} = \{\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{true}\}$; $\mathbb{B}^{n}$ is the set of byte strings of length $n$,
$T^{n}$ the sequences of $n$ elements of $T$, and $T_1 \times T_2$ the
pairs; $\mathsf{Opt}(T)$ is $T$ together with an absent value $\bot$;
$\mathsf{List}(T)$ is the finite sequences over $T$, $\mathsf{Map}(K, V)$
the finite maps from $K$ to $V$, and $\mathsf{Str}$ the UTF-8 strings;
$\mathsf{Enum}\{A_0, A_1, \dots\}$ is a set of tags, $A_i$ with index $i$. A
\emph{derivation record} is a value of $D := \mathbb{B}^{32} \times
\mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u32})$, a seed fingerprint
(Construction~\ref{def:zip32-seedfp}) and a derivation path, an index with
bit~$31$ set being hardened. Let $P := \mathsf{Map}(\mathsf{Str},
\mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$, the type of a proprietary map. Each record
below lists its fields in order, the order of the encoding
(\S\ref{sec:pczt-encoding}); a field marked (v1) or (v2) is present only in
that version (ZIP 374, ``Structure'' and the sections of each record).
{\raggedright
\begin{description}[leftmargin=0pt, itemsep=2pt]
\item[Global.] $\mathsf{tx\_version} \in \mathsf{u32}$, which is $5$ in
  version~$1$ and $5$ or $6$ in version~$2$;
  $\mathsf{version\_group\_id} \in \mathsf{u32}$, consistent with it (ZIP
  225, ZIP 229); $\mathsf{consensus\_branch\_id} \in \mathsf{u32}$, which
  for $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 6$ names an upgrade at which version~$6$ is
  supported, NU6.3 or later, that of NU7 being \texttt{0x77190AD9} (ZIP 259,
  ``NU7 network constants''); $\mathsf{fallback\_lock\_time} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u32})$, absence meaning $0$; $\mathsf{expiry\_height}
  \in \mathsf{u32}$ (ZIP 203); $\mathsf{coin\_type} \in \mathsf{u32}$, the
  SLIP 44 coin type, which is not part of the transaction and against which
  roles check network-specific data; $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable} \in
  \mathsf{u8}$ (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-modifiable}); $\mathsf{proprietary}
  \in P$.
\item[Transparent bundle.] $\mathsf{inputs} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathrm{TI})$
  and $\mathsf{outputs} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathrm{TO})$. An input
  $\mathrm{TI}$: $\mathsf{prevout\_txid} \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$;
  $\mathsf{prevout\_index} \in \mathsf{u32}$; $\mathsf{sequence} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u32})$, absence meaning $2^{32} - 1$;
  $\mathsf{required\_time\_lock\_time} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u32})$, at
  least $500{,}000{,}000$; $\mathsf{required\_height\_lock\_time} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u32})$, in $\{1, \dots, 499{,}999{,}999\}$;
  $\mathsf{script\_sig} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$;
  $\mathsf{value} \in \mathsf{u64}$; $\mathsf{script\_pubkey} \in
  \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8})$; $\mathsf{redeem\_script} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$; $\mathsf{partial\_signatures}
  \in \mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{33}, \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$;
  $\mathsf{sighash\_type} \in \mathsf{u8}$; $\mathsf{bip32\_derivation} \in
  \mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{33}, D)$; four preimage maps, for RIPEMD160,
  SHA256, HASH160 and HASH256, of types $\mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{20},
  \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$, $\mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{32},
  \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$, $\mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{20},
  \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$ and $\mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{32},
  \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$ in that order; $\mathsf{proprietary} \in P$.
  An output $\mathrm{TO}$: $\mathsf{value} \in \mathsf{u64}$;
  $\mathsf{script\_pubkey} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8})$;
  $\mathsf{redeem\_script} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$;
  $\mathsf{bip32\_derivation} \in \mathsf{Map}(\mathbb{B}^{33}, D)$;
  $\mathsf{user\_address} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{Str})$;
  $\mathsf{proprietary} \in P$.
\item[Sapling bundle.] $\mathsf{spends} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathrm{SS})$;
  $\mathsf{outputs} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathrm{SO})$; $\mathsf{value\_sum}
  \in \mathsf{i128}$, the net value of the spends minus the outputs;
  $\mathsf{anchor} \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$ (v1) or
  $\mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$ (v2); $\mathsf{bsk} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$. A spend $\mathrm{SS}$: effecting
  $\cvsym, \nfsym, \rk \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$; $\mathsf{zkproof} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{192})$; $\mathsf{spend\_auth\_sig} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{64})$; $\mathsf{recipient} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{43})$; $\mathsf{value} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u64})$; $\rcm \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$ and
  $\mathsf{rseed} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$, at most one set, by
  whether the note predates ZIP 212; $\rcv \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; the proof generation key $(\aksym,
  \mathsf{nsk}) \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32} \times \mathbb{B}^{32})$;
  $\mathsf{witness} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u32} \times
  (\mathbb{B}^{32})^{32})$; $\alpha \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; a
  derivation record in $\mathsf{Opt}(D)$; $\mathsf{dummy\_ask} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; $\mathsf{proprietary} \in P$. An output
  $\mathrm{SO}$: effecting $\cvsym, \mathsf{cmu}, \mathsf{epk} \in
  \mathbb{B}^{32}$ and the ciphertexts $C_{\mathsf{enc}}, C_{\mathsf{out}}
  \in \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8})$, whose lengths depend on the transaction
  version; $\mathsf{zkproof} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{192})$;
  $\mathsf{recipient} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{43})$; $\mathsf{value}
  \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u64})$; $\mathsf{rseed}, \rcv, \mathsf{ock} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; a derivation record in $\mathsf{Opt}(D)$;
  $\mathsf{user\_address} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{Str})$;
  $\mathsf{proprietary} \in P$. The Sapling constructions are those of the
  protocol specification and are cited, not restated.
\item[Orchard bundle.] $\mathsf{actions} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathrm{OA})$;
  $\mathsf{flags} \in \mathsf{u8}$, with bit~$0$ $\mathsf{enableSpends}$,
  bit~$1$ $\mathsf{enableOutputs}$, bit~$2$ $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress}$
  (v2; zero in version~$1$ and in the Orchard bundle of every transaction
  mined under NU6.3 or later, the rule of Construction~\ref{def:route}), and
  bits~$3$ to~$7$ zero; $\mathsf{value\_sum} \in \mathsf{u64} \times
  \mathsf{bool}$; $\mathsf{anchor} \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$ (v1) or
  $\mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$ (v2); $\mathsf{note\_version} \in
  \mathsf{Enum}\{\mathsf{V2}, \mathsf{V3}\}$ (v2), where $\mathsf{V2}$ is the
  note plaintext of ZIP 212, lead byte \texttt{0x02}, and $\mathsf{V3}$ that
  of ZIP 2005, lead byte \texttt{0x03}, which MUST be $\mathsf{V2}$ in the
  Orchard bundle and is implicitly $\mathsf{V2}$ in version~$1$;
  $\mathsf{zkproof} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8}))$, one proof
  for the bundle; $\mathsf{bsk} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$. An
  Action $\mathrm{OA}$: $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}} \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$; a spend
  $\mathrm{OS}$; an output $\mathrm{OO}$; $\rcv \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$, one per Action, since an Action has one net
  value commitment (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Value commitments''). A spend
  $\mathrm{OS}$: effecting $\nfsym, \rk \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$;
  $\mathsf{spend\_auth\_sig} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{64})$;
  $\mathsf{recipient} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{43})$, a raw Orchard
  address; $\mathsf{value} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u64})$; $\rho,
  \mathsf{rseed} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; $\fvk \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{96})$, the encoding of $(\aksym, \nksym,
  \rivk)$; $\mathsf{witness} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u32} \times
  (\mathbb{B}^{32})^{32})$, a position and a $32$-level authentication
  path; $\alpha \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; a derivation record in
  $\mathsf{Opt}(D)$; $\mathsf{dummy\_sk} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$;
  $\mathsf{proprietary} \in P$. In a transaction of version~$6$ the witness
  may be absent until proving (\S\ref{sec:pczt-v2}). An output
  $\mathrm{OO}$: effecting $\cmx, \mathsf{epk} \in \mathbb{B}^{32}$ and
  $C_{\mathsf{enc}}, C_{\mathsf{out}} \in \mathsf{List}(\mathsf{u8})$;
  $\mathsf{recipient} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{43})$; $\mathsf{value}
  \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{u64})$; $\mathsf{rseed}, \mathsf{ock} \in
  \mathsf{Opt}(\mathbb{B}^{32})$; a derivation record in $\mathsf{Opt}(D)$;
  $\mathsf{user\_address} \in \mathsf{Opt}(\mathsf{Str})$;
  $\mathsf{proprietary} \in P$.
\item[Ironwood bundle] (v2). The fields of the Orchard bundle, with
  $\mathsf{note\_version} = \mathsf{V3}$ and its anchor a root of the
  Ironwood note commitment tree (\S\ref{sec:pczt-v2}).
\end{description}
}
Which role sets, requires or clears each field is as ZIP 374 states per
field; this section restates it where it defines a role.
\end{definition}

Every transparent input's $\mathsf{value}$ and $\mathsf{script\_pubkey}$ are
required, not optional, because the signature digest commits to the value
and script of every coin a transaction spends (ZIP 244, as amended by ZIP
229; \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures''), and
the binding signatures are on that digest (ZIP 374, ``TransparentInput'').

\begin{remark}[The sign of an Orchard-protocol value sum]
The pair $\mathsf{value\_sum} = (m, b)$ denotes the net value of the
bundle's spends minus its outputs, but ZIP 374 does not state the meaning
of $b$: a gap in the specification. This volume reads $b = \mathsf{true}$
as the net value $-m$ and $b = \mathsf{false}$ as $+m$; the reading is
designed but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). We write
$v^{\mathsf{balance}}$ for the integer that a bundle's $\mathsf{value\_sum}$
denotes; for Sapling it is the $\mathsf{i128}$ value itself.
\end{remark}

A Signer MUST parse the $\mathsf{user\_address}$ of each output that carries
one and confirm that it contains the output's recipient, directly or as a
receiver of a unified address (ZIP 374, ``TransparentOutput'',
``SaplingOutput'' and ``OrchardOutput''; Definition~\ref{def:addr-container}
and Construction~\ref{def:addr-string}). The anchors, witnesses and
commitments that these fields carry are those of the \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``The note commitment tree'', with its subsections ``Anchors'' and
``Authentication paths''; they are cited, not rebuilt.

\begin{definition}[Canonical empty bundles]\label{def:pczt-empty}
The \emph{canonical empty bundle} of each slot is (ZIP 374, ``v2
Encoding''):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item transparent: empty input and output lists;
\item Sapling: empty spend and output lists, $\mathsf{value\_sum} = 0$,
  $\mathsf{anchor} = \bot$, $\mathsf{bsk} = \bot$;
\item Orchard: no Actions, $\mathsf{flags} = 0000\,0011$ in binary (spends
  and outputs enabled), $\mathsf{value\_sum} = (0, \mathsf{false})$,
  $\mathsf{anchor} = \bot$, $\mathsf{note\_version} = \mathsf{V2}$,
  $\mathsf{zkproof} = \mathsf{bsk} = \bot$;
\item Ironwood: no Actions, $\mathsf{flags} = 0000\,0111$ in binary
  (spends, outputs and cross-address transfers enabled),
  $\mathsf{value\_sum} = (0, \mathsf{false})$, $\mathsf{anchor} = \bot$,
  $\mathsf{note\_version} = \mathsf{V3}$, $\mathsf{zkproof} = \mathsf{bsk}
  = \bot$.
\end{enumerate}
A bundle that differs from it in any field, its flags, note version or
anchor included, is not canonical empty.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Encoding}\label{sec:pczt-encoding}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, pczt.py:
% [sec:pczt-encoding] varint limits: u32 at most 5 bytes (last byte <=
%   0x0f), u64 at most 10; header 8 bytes (PCZT = 50 43 5a 54, version
%   02 00 00 00)
\begin{construction}[PCZT encoding]\label{def:pczt-encoding}
The encoding of a PCZT of version $n$ is
\[
  \texttt{50 43 5a 54} \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(n) \,\|\,
  \mathit{body}_n,
\]
the first four bytes being the ASCII string \texttt{PCZT}. A string that
does not begin with this $8$-byte header is not a PCZT encoding, and a
parser that meets an unknown version MUST NOT parse the remainder (ZIP 374,
``Encoding''). The body is built from the following encodings of the types
of Definition~\ref{def:pczt-fields} (ZIP 374, ``Data type encodings''):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\mathsf{u8}$: one raw byte;
\item $\mathsf{u32}$ and $\mathsf{u64}$: $\mathsf{varint}(x)$, the digits
  of $x$ in base $128$, least significant first, one per byte, with bit~$7$
  set on every byte but the last. Encoders MUST emit the shortest form. A
  $k$-bit integer takes at most $\lceil k/7 \rceil$ bytes, $5$ for
  $\mathsf{u32}$ and $10$ for $\mathsf{u64}$, and in an encoding of that
  length the last byte contributes only the high $k \bmod 7$ bits, so the
  fifth byte of a $\mathsf{u32}$ is at most \texttt{0x0f}; decoders MUST
  reject encodings that exceed either bound and MAY accept non-minimal
  encodings within them;
\item $\mathsf{i128}$: $\mathsf{varint}(\mathsf{zigzag}(x))$, with
  $\mathsf{zigzag}(x) = 2x$ for $x \ge 0$ and $2|x| - 1$ for $x < 0$;
\item $\mathsf{bool}$: the byte \texttt{0x00} for $\mathsf{false}$ or
  \texttt{0x01} for $\mathsf{true}$, other bytes rejected;
\item $\mathbb{B}^{n}$, $T^{n}$ and pairs: the concatenation of the
  elements, with no length prefix, so that a witness path is $1024$ raw
  bytes;
\item $\mathsf{Opt}(T)$: the byte \texttt{0x00} for $\bot$, and
  \texttt{0x01} followed by the value otherwise, other tag bytes rejected;
\item $\mathsf{List}(T)$ and $\mathsf{Str}$: the varint of the number of
  elements, of bytes for a string, followed by the elements; invalid UTF-8
  is rejected;
\item $\mathsf{Map}(K, V)$: the varint of the number of entries, followed
  by each key and its value. Encoders MUST emit the entries in strictly
  ascending lexicographic byte order of the unencoded keys, without
  duplicates; decoders MAY accept other orders and duplicates;
\item $\mathsf{Enum}$: the varint of the tag's index;
\item a record: the concatenation of its fields in the order of
  Definition~\ref{def:pczt-fields}, without tags or padding, a field absent
  from version $n$ being omitted entirely.
\end{enumerate}
Encoders MUST NOT emit data after the last field; decoders MAY ignore or
reject it. \emph{Layout} (ZIP 374, ``v1 Encoding'' and ``v2 Encoding''):
$\mathit{body}_1$ is the Global record followed by the transparent, Sapling
and Orchard bundles, each Action encoded as $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$, spend,
output, $\rcv$. The body $\mathit{body}_2$ is the Global record followed by
the four bundles, transparent, Sapling, Orchard and Ironwood, each as a
value of $\mathsf{Opt}$; a bundle equal to its slot's canonical empty bundle
(Definition~\ref{def:pczt-empty}) MUST be encoded as absent and any other as
present, and a parser MUST restore the canonical empty bundle from absence.
Encoding in version~$1$ MUST fail when $\mathsf{tx\_version} \ne 5$, when
the Ironwood bundle is not canonical empty, when the Orchard note version
is not $\mathsf{V2}$, or when an anchor required before signing a
transaction of version~$5$ is absent. The version-$1$ anchor is a string of
$32$ bytes, so an anchor that is absent but not yet required, such as that
of a version-$5$ bundle without spends (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-v2}), has
no version-$1$ encoding either, and ZIP 374 states none: a gap in the
specification. The states version~$1$ cannot represent are therefore those
of the first three conditions and those with an absent anchor, among them
every state with a canonical empty Sapling or Orchard bundle
(Definition~\ref{def:pczt-empty}).
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Canonicity of the encoding]\label{lem:pczt-canonical}
For every version $n$ and every PCZT $P$ representable in version $n$,
exactly one byte string is an output for $P$ permitted to an encoder that
obeys the MUST rules of Construction~\ref{def:pczt-encoding}, and distinct
PCZTs have distinct encodings. Canonicity is a property of encoder output:
since decoders may accept non-minimal varints, unordered or repeated map
entries and trailing data, several strings may decode to one PCZT.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By induction on the type structure. \emph{Uniqueness.} The permitted
encoding of each primitive is a function of its value: the shortest
base-$128$ representation is unique, its last byte non-zero unless the
value is $0$; the map $\mathsf{zigzag}$ is a bijection from the integers
onto the naturals; the tags of options, booleans and enumerations are fixed;
the counts of lists and strings are determined by the value. A finite map
admits exactly one strictly ascending order of its entries without
duplicates, since lexicographic order on byte strings is total. A record
concatenates uniquely encoded fields in a fixed order. In version~$2$ the
presence of each bundle is determined by equality with
Definition~\ref{def:pczt-empty}, and no trailing data is permitted.
\emph{Injectivity.} Each encoding is self-delimiting given its type, being
of fixed length or a count followed by self-delimiting elements, so a
decoder that reads the fields in order recovers every value; an absent
version-$2$ bundle is restored as the canonical empty bundle, the only
bundle encoded as absent. The header fixes $n$, so encodings of distinct
versions differ as well.
\end{proof}

The canonical-empty rule is exact equality so that two copies of one PCZT
encode identically whatever software produced them, and re-encoding cannot
move a bundle between its present and absent forms (ZIP 374, ``Omitting
empty bundles in PCZT v2'').

% compute/OUTPUT.md, pczt.py:
% [sec:pczt-encoding] ex:pczt-minimal, coin type 133: 32 bytes
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset  0  magic                50 43 5a 54
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset  4  version              02 00 00 00
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset  8  tx_version           06
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset  9  version_group_id     98 ed 92 c4 0d
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 14  consensus_branch_id  d9 95 e4 b8 07
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 19  fallback_lock_time   00
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 20  expiry_height        80 ad e2 04
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 24  coin_type            85 01
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 26  tx_modifiable        83
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 27  proprietary          00
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 28  transparent          00
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 29  sapling              00
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 30  orchard              00
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 31  ironwood             00
% [sec:pczt-encoding] ex:pczt-minimal, coin type 1: 31 bytes
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 24  coin_type            01
% [sec:pczt-encoding]   offset 25  tx_modifiable        83
% [sec:pczt-encoding] version_group_id 0xD884B698 -> 98 ed 92 c4 0d; branch
%   id 0x77190AD9 -> d9 95 e4 b8 07; expiry 10,000,000 -> 80 ad e2 04; coin
%   type 133 -> 85 01 (2 bytes), 1 -> 01 (1 byte), so every later offset
%   shifts by one
\begin{example}[A minimal version-2 PCZT]\label{ex:pczt-minimal}
The Creator's PCZT (\S\ref{sec:pczt-construct}) for a transaction of
version~$6$, with version group identifier \texttt{0xD884B698} (ZIP 229),
consensus branch identifier \texttt{0x77190AD9} (NU7, ZIP 259), expiry
height $10{,}000{,}000$, coin type $133$ (Mainnet), no fallback lock time,
no proprietary entries and every bundle canonical empty, encodes in version~$2$
as the $32$ bytes of Table~\ref{tab:pczt-minimal}. With coin type $1$ the
coin type takes the one byte \texttt{01}, the total is $31$ bytes, and
every later field moves one byte earlier: the position of a field depends
on the values before it, which is why no field can be added within a
version.
\end{example}

\begin{table}[tp]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabular}{rll}
\toprule
Offset & Bytes & Field \\
\midrule
$0$ & \texttt{50 43 5a 54} & header, \texttt{PCZT} \\
$4$ & \texttt{02 00 00 00} & version $2$ \\
$8$ & \texttt{06} & $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 6$ \\
$9$ & \texttt{98 ed 92 c4 0d} & $\mathsf{version\_group\_id}$ \\
$14$ & \texttt{d9 95 e4 b8 07} & $\mathsf{consensus\_branch\_id}$ \\
$19$ & \texttt{00} & $\mathsf{fallback\_lock\_time} = \bot$ \\
$20$ & \texttt{80 ad e2 04} & $\mathsf{expiry\_height}$ \\
$24$ & \texttt{85 01} & $\mathsf{coin\_type} = 133$ \\
$26$ & \texttt{83} & $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$, bits $0$, $1$, $7$ \\
$27$ & \texttt{00} & empty proprietary map \\
$28$ to $31$ & \texttt{00 00 00 00} & four absent bundles \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{The minimal version-$2$ PCZT of Example~\ref{ex:pczt-minimal}:
offset, bytes and field, in the encoding of
Construction~\ref{def:pczt-encoding}.}
\label{tab:pczt-minimal}
\end{table}

\subsection{PCZT v2: deferred anchors and the Ironwood bundle}
\label{sec:pczt-v2}

In version~$2$ the Ironwood bundle carries the Ironwood-pool component of a
transaction of version~$6$, with the structure, field meaning and role
interactions of the Orchard bundle, the Ironwood pool being a second value
pool of the Orchard protocol (ZIP 229; \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The
Orchard protocol and its two pools''). It differs in three respects: its
$\mathsf{enableCrossAddress}$ bit is meaningful and normally $1$; its anchor
is a root of the Ironwood note commitment tree, distinct from the Orchard
tree; and its note version MUST be $\mathsf{V3}$. Every role MUST reject a
PCZT with $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 5$ whose Ironwood bundle is not canonical
empty (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-empty}) (ZIP 374, ``IronwoodBundle (PCZT
v2)'').

\begin{definition}[Anchors and witnesses in version 2]\label{def:pczt-v2}
The following rules hold (ZIP 374, ``Anchors and pre-authorization'').
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item For $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 5$, every shielded bundle's anchor is set
  when the bundle's first spend is added and never changes, since the
  version-$5$ digest commits to it.
\item For $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 6$, which requires version~$2$, an anchor
  MAY be absent until proving; an Updater MAY set an absent anchor, or
  replace a set one, at any time before the Prover runs for that bundle;
  setting or replacing an anchor MUST clear every proof already in that
  bundle, the Sapling spends' proofs or the Orchard-protocol bundle's
  proof, since a proof takes the anchor as a primary input; and the witness
  of a Sapling or Orchard-protocol spend MAY be absent until proving, and is
  set by an Updater once known.
\item Whenever a bundle has both an anchor and witnesses, the Updater that
  sets either MUST check that every witness of a spend of non-zero value
  roots to the anchor, and the Prover MUST check the same before proving.
\end{enumerate}
Deferral is governed by the transaction version, not by the PCZT version: a
transaction of version~$5$ in a version-$2$ PCZT fixes its anchors as in
version~$1$. Absence is an absent option, not an all-zero string, because
zero is an element of $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$ and so a syntactically valid
anchor (ZIP 374, ``Optional anchors in PCZT v2'').
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Deferred anchors]\label{prop:pczt-defer}
Let $P$ be a PCZT of a transaction of version~$6$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item If every spend of $P$ is signed, replacing the anchor $\mathit{rt}$ of
  a bundle and its proof $\pi$ by any anchor $\mathit{rt}'$ and a proof
  $\pi'$ valid for it leaves every signature valid and the transaction
  identifier unchanged: re-anchoring needs only re-proving.
\item A spend of an Orchard-protocol note whose creating transaction is not
  yet mined can be fully signed before that note is in the tree, and its
  signatures remain valid once an anchor, a witness and a proof are
  supplied: every field that the signature digest commits to is computable
  from the note data, and the anchor is not among them.
\end{enumerate}
Neither holds for a transaction of version~$5$, and~(ii) fails for a
Sapling spend in version~$6$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i)~The version-$6$ digest and transaction identifier omit the anchors,
which are authorising data (ZIP 229, ``Anchor commitment (version 6)''),
and by \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'',
Proposition ``Bundle binding'', replacing $(\mathit{rt}, \pi)$ by
$(\mathit{rt}', \pi')$ changes neither the transaction identifier nor any signed
message, so every signature remains valid.

(ii)~The effecting fields of an Orchard-protocol spend are $\nfsym$, $\rk$
and its Action's $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$. The nullifier is
$\mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(\rho, \psi, \cmsym)$ (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``The nullifier''), a function of the key and of note data, with
$\rho$ fixed by the creating Action and $\psi$ and $\cmsym$ derived from
$\mathsf{rseed}$, $\rho$, the address and the value; it does not depend on the
note's position. The key $\rk$ and the commitment $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$ depend
on keys, values and fresh randomness only.

For version~$5$ the digest commits to the anchors (ZIP 244, ``Signature
Digest''), so replacing an anchor invalidates every signature, which
refutes~(i); and an anchor fixed before the note is in the tree is the root
of a tree state that does not contain the note, for which no authentication
path of the note exists except with negligible probability (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Anchors'', Proposition ``Membership soundness''), so a spend of
non-zero value
signed then admits no valid proof, so no signature made before the note is
in the tree survives completion, which refutes~(ii). A Sapling nullifier
depends on the note's position in the tree (protocol specification,
\S``Computing $\rho$ values and Nullifiers''), which is unknown until the
note is mined, and is effecting data; for Sapling only~(i) holds.
\end{proof}

ZIP 374 calls the two workflows re-anchoring and the spending of unmined
notes; Proposition~\ref{prop:plan-chaining}~(c) is the latter for the steps
of a plan.

\subsection{Construction and IO finalisation}\label{sec:pczt-construct}

The transaction of a PCZT is of version~$5$, with version group identifier
\texttt{0x26A7270A} (protocol specification, \S``Transaction Consensus
Rules''), or of version~$6$, with
\texttt{0xD884B698} (ZIP 229), whose consensus branch identifier names
NU6.3 or a later upgrade; version~$4$ is not representable, and is invalid
from NU7 on (ZIP 2003, deployed by ZIP 259). An Ironwood component requires
version~$6$, the mixed case being rejected by the rule of
\S\ref{sec:pczt-v2}. The consensus branch identifier fixes the rules in
force, and a role that does not recognise it cannot check the PCZT against
them (ZIP 374, ``Global'').

\emph{Creator.} The Creator chooses $\mathsf{tx\_version}$,
$\mathsf{version\_group\_id}$ and $\mathsf{consensus\_branch\_id}$, sets the
other Global fields with $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable} = 1000\,0011$ in binary,
and sets every bundle to its canonical empty bundle
(Definition~\ref{def:pczt-empty}); for $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 5$ it sets
the Sapling and Orchard anchors, and for $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 6$ it MAY
leave them to an Updater (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-v2}) (ZIP 374,
``Creator'').

\emph{Constructor.} Before adding anything the Constructor MUST check the
bit of $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$ for the kind added
(Definition~\ref{def:pczt-modifiable}), and it updates the bundle's
$\mathsf{value\_sum}$. Per Orchard-protocol Action, in the notation of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, it sets $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}} :=
\mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}_{\rcv}(v^{\mathsf{old}} -
v^{\mathsf{new}})$ for a fresh $\rcv$ (\S``Value commitments''); the
nullifier $\nfsym^{\mathsf{old}}$ of the consumed note (\S``The
nullifier''); a uniform $\alpha$ and $\rk := \aksym^{\PP} +
[\alpha]\,G^{\mathsf{SpendAuth}}$ (\S``Randomised validating keys''); and
the created note with $\rho^{\mathsf{new}} := \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}}$, its
$\cmx$ (\S``Note commitments''), $\mathsf{epk}$ and $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$
(\S``Encryption to the recipient'') and $C_{\mathsf{out}}$ (\S``The
outgoing ciphertext''); together with the context fields of the spend and
the output. Dummy and fabricated Actions and their order are those of
Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}, only fully dummy Actions carrying
$\mathsf{dummy\_sk}$. Spends and outputs MUST agree with the bundle flags.
When the bundle's anchor is set, each spend added carries a witness rooting
to it, or, for a note of value zero, whose path the Action statement does
not check, any placeholder; for $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 6$ a spend MAY be
added without a witness, even of a note not yet in the tree; for
$\mathsf{tx\_version} = 5$ every non-dummy spend is of a note in the tree,
with a witness to the anchor. A transparent input with a lock-time
requirement is added only if its lock type is compatible with those of the
other inputs and, when inputs are already signed, it leaves the lock time
unchanged (\S\ref{sec:pczt-extract}) (ZIP 374, ``Constructor'' and
``OrchardBundle'').

\begin{construction}[IO finalisation]\label{def:pczt-io-final}
Once the input and output set is final, the IO Finalizer performs the
following steps, failing where stated (ZIP 374, ``IO Finalizer'').
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item If shielded spends or outputs exist, it clears bits~$0$, $1$ and~$7$
  of $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$.
\item For each Orchard-protocol bundle with Actions $1, \dots, n$, it
  requires every $\rcv_i$, sets $\mathsf{bsk} := \sum_i \rcv_i \in
  \F_{p_{\Vesta}}$, computes
  \[
    \mathsf{bvk} := \textstyle\sum_i \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i -
      [v^{\mathsf{balance}}]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
  \]
  with $v^{\mathsf{balance}}$ the integer that the bundle's
  $\mathsf{value\_sum}$ denotes (\S\ref{sec:pczt-fields}), and fails unless
  $[\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = \mathsf{bvk}$ (\emph{Ironwood
  Guide}, \S``The binding signature''). The Orchard and the Ironwood
  bundles are finalised separately, each by this computation on its own
  Actions.
\item For the Sapling bundle, it sets $\mathsf{bsk}$ to the sum of the
  spends' $\rcv$ minus the sum of the outputs' $\rcv$, checked in the same
  way against the Sapling binding validating key (protocol specification,
  \S``Balance and Binding Signature (Sapling)'').
\item It computes the signature digest over the effecting data
  (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures''), signs
  each dummy spend under the spend authorising key derived from its
  $\mathsf{dummy\_sk}$ or given by its $\mathsf{dummy\_ask}$, randomised by
  the spend's $\alpha$, and clears that key.
\item For each bundle with $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 0$, it MUST
  verify that each Action's output recipient equals its spend recipient,
  and fail otherwise.
\end{enumerate}
Equality of the raw recipients in step~(5) implies condition A10 of the
Action statement, equality of the expanded receivers $(\gd, \pkd)$
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Action statement''), and is stricter only
for two diversifiers with one diversified base. An Action that violates
A10, for which no valid proof exists, therefore fails the step, which
reports the violation at the role responsible for it. ZIP 374 keys the step
on the value of the bit alone, but before NU6.3 activation bit~$2$ of the
Orchard bundle's flags is reserved as $0$ while cross-address transfers are
unrestricted (ZIP 229, Rationale, ``\texttt{enableCrossAddress}
polarity''); read literally, the step then rejects an Orchard bundle that
pays another address, a gap in the specification. This volume applies the
step only where bit~$2$ carries the meaning of
$\mathsf{enableCrossAddress}$, and compares expanded receivers as A10 does;
both readings are designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Value-sum consistency]\label{lem:pczt-valuesum}
Let an Orchard-protocol bundle have Actions with $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i =
\mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}_{\rcv_i}(v^{\mathsf{net}}_i)$, and
let $v^{\mathsf{balance}}$ be the integer that its $\mathsf{value\_sum}$
denotes. Step~(2) of Construction~\ref{def:pczt-io-final} passes if and
only if $\sum_i v^{\mathsf{net}}_i \equiv v^{\mathsf{balance}}
\pmod{p_{\Vesta}}$, and $\mathsf{bsk}$ is then a binding signing key whose
validating key is $\mathsf{bvk}$. The check reads no value
$v^{\mathsf{net}}_i$, which may therefore have been redacted.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
With $\mathsf{bsk} = \sum_i \rcv_i$,
\[
  \mathsf{bvk} - [\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
  = \Bigl[\textstyle\sum_i v^{\mathsf{net}}_i - v^{\mathsf{balance}}\Bigr]
    \,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
which is $\mathcal{O}$ if and only if the coefficient vanishes modulo
$p_{\Vesta}$, since $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is a non-identity point of the
group of prime order $p_{\Vesta}$. This is \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``The binding signature'', Proposition ``Balance and key knowledge'',
part~(i). No independence of $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$
is used. The check computes $\mathsf{bvk}$ from the commitments and
$\mathsf{value\_sum}$ alone.
\end{proof}

The binding signing key cannot wait for the Transaction Extractor, since
every $\rcv$ would then travel to it and open every value commitment to
every intermediate party; nor can Constructors maintain it incrementally,
since serial Constructors would have to update it and parallel ones, whose
results a Combiner merges, must not. One role run once the set is final
removes both problems, and the same role signs dummy spends so that no
Signer parses spending-key material (ZIP 374, ``Adding an IO Finalizer
role'').

\subsection{Signing}\label{sec:pczt-signing}

\emph{Digests.} The Signer derives the effecting data from the PCZT and
computes the shielded signature digest $\mathsf{SigHash}$ itself (ZIP 244
for version~$5$, as amended by ZIP 229 for version~$6$; \emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures''), neither proofs nor
signatures entering it; for each transparent input it signs, it computes
that input's digest under the input's $\mathsf{sighash\_type}$ (ZIP 244,
``S.2: transparent\_sig\_digest'') and signs it by the scheme of the
\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The transparent layer's schemes: ECDSA and BIP-340
over secp256k1''. A Signer that cannot sign under an input's hash type MUST
NOT sign that input (ZIP 374, ``TransparentInput'').

\emph{Orchard-protocol spends.} For an Action it signs, with $\alpha$
present, the Signer sets $\rsk := \ask + \alpha$, fails unless $\rk =
[\rsk]\,G^{\mathsf{SpendAuth}}$, and sets $\mathsf{spend\_auth\_sig}$ to the
RedPallas signature on $\mathsf{SigHash}$ under $\rsk$ (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Randomised validating keys''); Ironwood-pool Actions are signed
in the same way in their own bundle. A signature produced elsewhere, for
example by a hardware device, is attached if and only if it is valid under
the Action's $\rk$ on $\mathsf{SigHash}$. The Signer then updates
$\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$ (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-modifiable}) (ZIP 374,
``Signer'' and ``OrchardSpend'').

\begin{definition}[Signer relations]\label{def:pczt-signer-checks}
For an Orchard-protocol Action whose explicit data are present, with
$v^{\mathsf{old}}$ and $v^{\mathsf{new}}$ the values of its spend and its
output, the \emph{Signer relations} are, in the notation of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}} =
  \mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}_{\rcv}(v^{\mathsf{old}} -
  v^{\mathsf{new}})$ (\S``Value commitments'');
\item the consumed note rebuilt from ($\mathsf{recipient}$,
  $v^{\mathsf{old}}$, $\rho$, $\mathsf{rseed}$) has nullifier $\nfsym$ under
  the $\nksym$ of $\fvk$ (\S``The nullifier''), and $\fvk$ owns the
  recipient: $\pkd = [\ivk]\,\gd$ for the $\ivk$ that $\fvk$ determines
  (\S``Diversified addresses'');
\item $\rk = \aksym^{\PP} + [\alpha]\,G^{\mathsf{SpendAuth}}$ for the
  $\aksym$ of $\fvk$ (\S``Randomised validating keys'');
\item $\cmx$ is the commitment of the created note rebuilt from
  ($\mathsf{recipient}$, $v^{\mathsf{new}}$, $\mathsf{rseed}$) with
  $\rho^{\mathsf{new}} = \nfsym$ (\S``Note commitments'');
\item for an output that is not fabricated, $\mathsf{esk}$ derived from
  $\mathsf{rseed}$ and $\rho^{\mathsf{new}}$ gives $\mathsf{epk}$ and a
  $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$ that decrypts to a plaintext with the explicit
  recipient, value and $\mathsf{rseed}$ (\S``Encryption to the
  recipient''), and, when $\mathsf{ock}$ is present, $C_{\mathsf{out}}$
  decrypts under it to $(\pkd, \mathsf{esk})$ (\S``The outgoing
  ciphertext'').
\end{enumerate}
A fabricated zero-valued output at a spent note's receiver carries a random
$C_{\mathsf{enc}}$ (Construction~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}) and is exempt
from~(e): a Signer MUST NOT reject it on that ground alone, and SHOULD
classify it as a tolerable dummy, checking~(d) if it wishes (ZIP 374,
``Signer'', ``Verifier'' and ``Fabricated same-address outputs (NU6.3)'').
\end{definition}

\emph{Obligations before signing} (ZIP 374, ``Signer''). A Signer MUST
reject a PCZT that carries any $\mathsf{dummy\_sk}$ or $\mathsf{dummy\_ask}$:
the IO Finalizer signs dummy spends and clears these keys, so their presence
asks the Signer to parse spending-key material. It MUST, for each output
with a $\mathsf{user\_address}$, parse it and confirm that it contains the
output's recipient (\S\ref{sec:pczt-fields}). It SHOULD verify that the
explicit values and note data it shows its user are consistent with the
effecting data it signs, through the relations of
Definition~\ref{def:pczt-signer-checks}; a field absent because it was
redacted is not an inconsistency, and only a present field that contradicts
the effecting data is. The relations need the value and note commitments,
BLAKE2b and the functions built on it, nullifier derivation, key agreement,
the key derivation and the AEAD of note encryption, the signature digest
and RedPallas signing (RedJubjub for Sapling), and no proof system.

\begin{theorem}[Signer safety]\label{thm:pczt-signer}
Let a Signer hold the $\ask$ of an Orchard-protocol key generated with
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, and suppose that for every PCZT $P$ it
signs it computes $m_P := \mathsf{SigHash}(T_P)$ from the effecting data
$T_P$ of $P$, performs the obligations above, verifies relations~(a)
to~(e) of Definition~\ref{def:pczt-signer-checks}, with all their fields
present, for every Action whose spend it signs, displays the values,
recipients and address strings so checked, and signs under keys
$\ask + \alpha$ no message other than such digests $m_P$. Under the
assumptions of \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Authorisation of spends'', Theorem
``Spend authority'', and Assumption ``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b''
(\S``Transaction digests and signatures''), except with negligible probability:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item every accepted Action that consumes a note of the key carries a
  signature on some $m_P$;
\item every accepted transaction that carries an Action with a randomised
  validating key $\rk$ under which the Signer signed has the effecting data
  $T_Q$ of some PCZT $Q$ that the Signer signed with an Action of key
  $\rk$; in it the Actions whose spends the Signer signed consume and
  create the notes, with the values and recipients, that the Signer
  displayed for $Q$, and every other effecting field, balancing values and
  transparent data included, equals that of $Q$.
\end{enumerate}
The theorem concerns content, not inclusion.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i)~The Signer is the signing oracle of the \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Authorisation of spends'', Definition ``Spend-authority experiment'',
queried with $(\alpha, m_P)$ for $\alpha$ taken from the PCZT and so possibly
chosen by an adversary, which that experiment admits; the full viewing key that
the PCZT discloses is given to the adversary there. Theorem ``Spend authority''
bounds by a negligible function the probability of an accepted Action that
consumes a note of the key without a queried digest.

(ii)~Let $T'$ be accepted and carry an Action with key $\rk$ and a
signature $\sigma'$ valid under $\rk$ on $\mathsf{SigHash}(T')$. The
reduction of the proof of Theorem ``Spend authority'', which simulates the
key holder from $\aksym^{\PP}$ and answers queries with adversarial
randomisers, turns a triple $(\rk, \mathsf{SigHash}(T'), \sigma')$ that is
not a triple of an answer of the Signer into a forgery in \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``RedPallas unforgeability'', Proposition ``Unforgeability under
re-randomisation''; hence,
except with negligible probability, $\mathsf{SigHash}(T') = m_Q$ for a PCZT
$Q$ that the Signer signed under $\rk$. The collision case of the proof of
Proposition ``Bundle binding'' uses only that each node of the digest tree
hashes a string from which its children and fields are recovered uniquely,
which holds equally for the version-$5$ tree (ZIP 244, ``TxId Digest'' and
``Signature Digest''), where the anchors are among the hashed fields and are
effecting data (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-kinds}); it therefore applies to both
transaction versions and gives $T' = T_Q$ as effecting data,
except with negligible probability under Assumption ``Collision resistance
of BLAKE2b''. The digest covers the value and script of each transparent
input (\S\ref{sec:pczt-fields}), so a false value in $Q$ yields a digest
that validation does not compute and a signature that is not accepted. For
each Action whose spend the Signer signed, relations~(a) and~(d) make the
displayed data an opening of $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$ and of $\cmx$, and by
Lemma ``Hiding and binding of net value commitments'' and Proposition
``Hiding and binding of note commitments'' of the \emph{Ironwood Guide} no
other opening is feasible; relation~(b) identifies the consumed note, whose
commitment fixes its value; relation~(e) makes the created note decryptable
by its recipient (Proposition ``Decryption correctness''); and the check of
$\mathsf{user\_address}$ ties each recipient to the displayed string.
\end{proof}

\emph{Pre-authorisation} (ZIP 374, ``Signer'' and ``Anchors and
pre-authorization''). For a transaction of version~$6$ a Signer MAY sign
while the shielded anchors, witnesses or proofs are absent, since
signatures commit to none of them (Proposition~\ref{prop:pczt-defer}). A
Signer asked to do so SHOULD be aware, and where applicable make its user
aware, that a spend without a witness may be of a note that never exists
on chain: the signature authorises the spend whether or not the note is
created. For a transaction of version~$5$ the anchors are in the digest and
MUST be present before signing.

\begin{lemma}[Binding key after IO finalisation]\label{lem:pczt-bsk}
If IO finalisation (Construction~\ref{def:pczt-io-final}) succeeded for an
Orchard-protocol bundle, and its commitments $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$ and
its $\mathsf{value\_sum}$ are unchanged afterwards, then a binding signature
under $\mathsf{bsk}$ on the signature digest is valid under the
$\mathsf{bvk}$ that validation derives from the transaction's
$\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$ and balancing value $v^{\mathsf{balance}}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Validation computes $\mathsf{bvk}$ from the same commitments and balancing
value (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The binding signature'', Definition ``Binding
validating key''), and IO finalisation checked that it equals
$[\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:pczt-valuesum}).
Correctness of RedPallas in the binding-signature instance (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``The binding signature'', Construction ``Binding signature'') gives
validity.
\end{proof}

The hypothesis of Lemma~\ref{lem:pczt-bsk} holds for every PCZT reached by
the roles after finalisation: $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$ is required effecting
data, which no later role changes and the merge of \S\ref{sec:pczt-combine}
keeps equal, and a finalised bundle merges only with a bundle of equal
Action count and $\mathsf{value\_sum}$.

\subsection{Proving}\label{sec:pczt-proving}

Per Orchard-protocol Action the Prover requires the spend's recipient,
value, $\rho$, $\mathsf{rseed}$, $\fvk$, witness and $\alpha$, the output's
recipient, value and $\mathsf{rseed}$, and the Action's $\rcv$; the
bundle's anchor MUST be set, and before proving the Prover MUST verify that
the path of the witness of every spend of non-zero value roots to it, and
fail otherwise. For Sapling it requires per spend the recipient, the value,
$\rcm$ or $\mathsf{rseed}$, $\rcv$, the proof generation key and the
witness, and per output the recipient, the value, $\mathsf{rseed}$ and
$\rcv$. The Prover needs no spending key; a delegated Prover learns the
note data of the bundle but gains no spending authority
(\S\ref{sec:pczt-combine}) (ZIP 374, ``Prover'').

\begin{construction}[Proof creation from a PCZT]\label{def:pczt-prove}
For an Orchard-protocol bundle with no Actions the Prover does nothing.
Otherwise, for each Action $i$, it rebuilds the consumed note from its
explicit data and the created note from its explicit data with
$\rho^{\mathsf{new}}_i = \nfsym_i$; forms the primary input
\[
  \bigl(\mathit{rt},\, \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i,\, \nfsym_i,\, \rk_i,\,
  \cmx_i,\, \mathsf{enableSpends},\, \mathsf{enableOutputs},\,
  \mathsf{disableCrossAddress}\bigr)
\]
with $\mathit{rt}$ the bundle's anchor, and the auxiliary input from the
witness, the two notes, $\alpha_i$, the components of $\fvk_i$ and
$\rcv_i$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Action statement''); and fails if
the pair does not satisfy the Action statement, in particular if $\fvk_i$
does not own the consumed note or $\cmx_i$ differs from the commitment of
the rebuilt created note. It then creates one Halo 2 proof for all Actions
of the bundle (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Action circuit and the Halo 2
proof''; \emph{Halo 2 Guide}, \S``The Halo 2 proof system'') and sets
$\mathsf{zkproof}$. The Ironwood bundle is proved in the same way with its
own anchor. The primary input consists of effecting data and the anchor
only.
\end{construction}

An Action whose $\rk$ is the identity point $\mathcal{O}$ is invalid
(protocol specification, \S``Action Descriptions''; ZIP 256, ``Orchard
identity rk''), and the Transaction Extractor MUST refuse it (ZIP 374,
``Transaction Extractor''). A Prover that refuses it as well spares a
proof of a transaction that cannot be valid; ZIP 374 does not state that
check for the Prover, and it is designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).

\subsection{Combining, redaction, and privacy}\label{sec:pczt-combine}

\begin{construction}[Binary merge]\label{def:pczt-merge}
The \emph{merge} $P \sqcup Q$ of two PCZTs is defined, or fails, as
follows (ZIP 374, ``Combiner'').
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Global.} The fields $\mathsf{tx\_version}$,
  $\mathsf{version\_group\_id}$,
  $\mathsf{consensus\_}\allowbreak\mathsf{branch\_id}$,
  $\mathsf{fallback\_lock\_time}$, $\mathsf{expiry\_height}$ and
  $\mathsf{coin\_type}$ MUST be equal. The field $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$
  merges bitwise, bits~$0$, $1$ and~$7$ by AND and bit~$2$ by OR, bits~$3$
  to~$6$ being required zero on both sides.
\item \emph{Lists} of inputs, outputs, spends and Actions. If either side's
  bundle is IO-finalised, its $\mathsf{bsk}$ being present, both sides have
  equally many entries and equal $\mathsf{value\_sum}$. Otherwise the
  entries at the positions common to both lists merge pairwise, and the
  extra entries of the longer list are appended provided the shorter
  side's $\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$ permits additions of that kind, the
  merge failing otherwise. The merged shielded bundle's
  $\mathsf{value\_sum}$ is that of the side each of whose lists of the
  bundle is at least as long as the other side's, the merge failing if
  neither side is; with lists of equal lengths the two MUST be equal.
  ZIP 374 leaves this unstated, so it is designed but unspecified
  (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\item \emph{Required fields} of merged entries, and the bundle
  $\mathsf{flags}$, $\mathsf{note\_version}$ and, in version~$1$,
  $\mathsf{anchor}$, MUST be equal.
\item \emph{Optional fields} merge as $x \sqcup \bot = \bot \sqcup x = x$,
  and $x \sqcup y = x$ if $x = y$, failing otherwise; in version~$2$ this
  rule covers the anchors.
\item \emph{Maps} merge by union of their keys, the values of a common key
  being required equal.
\end{enumerate}
The Combiner of $k$ PCZTs merges them into one that contains every field of
each, or fails. It MUST NOT combine PCZTs that do not represent the same
transaction, that is, PCZTs not derived by roles from a common ancestor,
which rules~(1) to~(3) check structurally. Once the input and output set is
final, the effecting data determine the transaction identifier, which then
identifies the PCZT; for version~$6$, re-anchored copies keep that
identifier.
\end{construction}

BIP 174 lets a combiner keep one of two conflicting values and discard the
other. The PCZT merge requires equality instead, which is always possible
because every field is typed and known in advance, so every conflict fails
at the Combiner and none diverges later (ZIP 374, ``Combiner rejecting
non-identical duplicates'').

\begin{lemma}[Commutativity of the merge]\label{lem:pczt-merge}
For PCZTs $P$ and $Q$, the merge $P \sqcup Q$ is defined if and only if
$Q \sqcup P$ is, and then the two are equal.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By cases on the rules of Construction~\ref{def:pczt-merge}. The equality
tests of rules~(1) and~(3) are symmetric; AND and OR are commutative; the
rule for optional fields and the union of maps with equal values on common
keys are symmetric; the condition on IO-finalised bundles is symmetric; in
rule~(2) the longer list and the side whose permission is consulted are
determined by the lengths, not by the order of the arguments, and so is the
merged $\mathsf{value\_sum}$, which is required equal at equal lengths; and
the
pairwise merges of common entries commute by induction on the structure of
the entries.
\end{proof}

ZIP 374 requires further that combining SHOULD be functionally
order-independent, $\mathrm{Combine}(f_A(p), f_B(p)) = f_A(f_B(p)) =
f_B(f_A(p))$ for participants that apply $f_A$ and $f_B$ to $p$. That is a
requirement on roles, not a property proved here for all of them;
Proposition~\ref{prop:pczt-order} proves it for the Prover and the Signer.

\begin{proposition}[Order independence of proving and signing]
\label{prop:pczt-order}
Let $p$ be an IO-finalised PCZT on which the Prover
(Construction~\ref{def:pczt-prove}) and a Signer (\S\ref{sec:pczt-signing})
are both defined, the anchors being set for proving and, for a transaction
of version~$5$, fixed before signing (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-v2}). Fix the
random coins of both roles. Then $\mathrm{Prove}(\mathrm{Sign}(p))$ and
$\mathrm{Sign}(\mathrm{Prove}(p))$ are defined and both equal
$\mathrm{Prove}(p) \sqcup \mathrm{Sign}(p)$: the two roles run in either
order, or in parallel on copies whose outputs are merged by
Construction~\ref{def:pczt-merge}. For a transaction of version~$6$ signing
may moreover precede the setting of the anchors
(Proposition~\ref{prop:pczt-defer}). If a copy is redacted before either
role, and retains the fields that role reads, the equality holds up to the
redacted fields, which the merge restores from the other side.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Signing.} The signed digest is computed from effecting data only,
excluding proofs and signatures (Lemma~\ref{lem:authorise-order}), and the
relations of Definition~\ref{def:pczt-signer-checks} read effecting and
context data; $\mathrm{Prove}$ writes neither, so $\mathrm{Sign}$ reads the
same values on $p$ and on $\mathrm{Prove}(p)$ and, with its coins fixed,
writes the same signatures and bits. \emph{Proving.} The primary input of
the Action statement consists of effecting data and the anchor, and the
auxiliary input of context data (Construction~\ref{def:pczt-prove};
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Action statement''); $\mathrm{Sign}$ writes
none of them, and the Prover reads no spending key, so $\mathrm{Prove}$
writes the same proofs on $p$ and on $\mathrm{Sign}(p)$. \emph{Merge.} The
role $\mathrm{Prove}$ sets only proof fields, absent in $p$, and
$\mathrm{Sign}$ only signatures, absent in $p$, and bits of
$\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$, which it only clears, or sets in the case of
bit~$2$; the bundle of $p$ is finalised on both sides with equal counts and
value sums. Neither role writes a required field, so rule~(3) holds, and a
transparent signature is a new key of $\mathsf{partial\_signatures}$,
merged by rule~(5). Rules~(1) to~(5) of Construction~\ref{def:pczt-merge}
therefore give $\mathrm{Prove}(p) \sqcup \mathrm{Sign}(p) =
\mathrm{Prove}(\mathrm{Sign}(p)) = \mathrm{Sign}(\mathrm{Prove}(p))$. ZIP
374, ``Prover'', states the same independence.
\end{proof}

\emph{Redactor} (ZIP 374, ``Redactor'' and the field descriptions). Anyone
may clear fields that later roles do not need: per Orchard-protocol Action
the spend's $\mathsf{spend\_auth\_sig}$, recipient, value, $\rho$,
$\mathsf{rseed}$, $\fvk$, witness, $\alpha$, derivation record, dummy key
and proprietary entries, the output's recipient, value, $\mathsf{rseed}$,
$\mathsf{ock}$, derivation record, $\mathsf{user\_address}$ and proprietary
entries, and $\rcv$; the corresponding Sapling fields; and the transparent
and Global lookup data. A PCZT with inputs of several independent Signers is
sent to each with only what that Signer needs, for example without the
other Signers' values of $\alpha$.

\begin{proposition}[Disclosure by a PCZT]\label{prop:pczt-disclosure}
A PCZT carries strictly more than its transaction: for each shielded spend
or output it may carry the counterparty address, the value, the note
randomness, $\rcv$, which opens $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$, the full viewing
key of the spending account, derivation records, $\mathsf{ock}$, which opens
$C_{\mathsf{out}}$, and address strings. Every recipient of a PCZT at a
stage learns all data present at that stage; a delegated Prover learns the
note data of the spends and outputs but gains no spending authority; the
extracted transaction contains none of these data. Participants SHOULD send
a PCZT only to entities needed for the remaining roles and SHOULD redact
what those do not need, and wallets SHOULD protect stored PCZTs as they
protect their note records (ZIP 374, ``Privacy Implications'').
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The listed fields are context data (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-kinds}), which
extraction omits (\S\ref{sec:pczt-extract}); a recipient reads every field
present. The Prover's data (Construction~\ref{def:pczt-prove}) contain
$\fvk$ but no $\ask$, and an accepted spend of a note of the key without a
signature under $\ask + \alpha$ on a digest that the key holder signed is
excluded, except with negligible probability, by \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Authorisation of spends'', Theorem ``Spend authority'', under its
assumptions, whose adversary holds the full viewing key.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Spend finalisation and extraction}\label{sec:pczt-extract}

\emph{Spend Finalizer} (ZIP 374, ``Spend Finalizer'' and
``TransparentInput''; BIP 11; BIP 16). For each transparent input with
enough data, the Spend Finalizer builds $\mathsf{script\_sig}$: for P2PKH,
the push of the one signature, with its hash-type byte, under the key that
hashes to the address, followed by the push of that key; for P2SH
multisignature, the opcode $\mathsf{OP\_0}$, the pushes of $m$ signatures
in the key order of the redeem script and the push of the redeem script,
failing with fewer than $m$ signatures. It MUST fail on a signature whose
hash type is not the input's $\mathsf{sighash\_type}$. It SHOULD then clear
the input's redeem script, partial signatures, derivation records and
preimages. It keeps $\mathsf{value}$ and $\mathsf{script\_pubkey}$,
required fields that the digest needs, and $\mathsf{sequence}$ and the
lock-time requirements, which the digest and
Definition~\ref{def:pczt-locktime} read at extraction; ZIP 374's ``all
other optional data'' would include these last fields, a gap in the
specification.

\begin{definition}[Lock-time determination]\label{def:pczt-locktime}
If no transparent input has a required time or height lock, the lock time
is $\mathsf{fallback\_lock\_time}$, or $0$ if it is absent. Otherwise the
lock type is one supported by every input that states a requirement, an
input that states both types supporting both; an input that states neither
supports both. If both types remain possible, the height type is chosen;
if none remains, the requirements are unsatisfiable. The lock time is the
maximum required value of the chosen type (ZIP 374, ``Determining Lock
Time'').
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Transaction extraction]\label{def:pczt-extract}
The Transaction Extractor checks, failing on any violation (ZIP 374,
``Transaction Extractor''), that
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\mathsf{tx\_version}$, $\mathsf{version\_group\_id}$ and
  $\mathsf{consensus\_branch\_id}$ name a supported format, and the
  lock-time requirements are satisfiable
  (Definition~\ref{def:pczt-locktime});
\item for $\mathsf{tx\_version} = 5$ the Ironwood bundle is canonical empty;
\item every $\mathsf{script\_sig}$ is set;
\item every Sapling spend has its proof and signature, and every Sapling
  output its proof;
\item every Orchard-protocol bundle with $n \ge 1$ Actions has a
  $\mathsf{zkproof}$ of the canonical length of $2720 + 2272\,n$ bytes
  (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The transaction format''), and every Action a
  $\mathsf{spend\_auth\_sig}$;
\item every non-empty shielded bundle has its anchor and $\mathsf{bsk}$;
\item no $\rk$ is $\mathcal{O}$.
\end{enumerate}
It then sets each bundle's balancing value to $v^{\mathsf{balance}}$,
failing if it lies outside the signed $64$-bit range of the transaction
field; sets the lock time by Definition~\ref{def:pczt-locktime}; computes
$\mathsf{SigHash}$; for each bundle derives $\mathsf{bvk}$ from its value
commitments and balancing value, fails unless it matches $\mathsf{bsk}$,
and signs $\mathsf{SigHash}$ under $\mathsf{bsk}$, by RedPallas for the
Orchard and Ironwood bundles and by RedJubjub for Sapling
(Lemma~\ref{lem:pczt-bsk}); and outputs the transaction, without any
context data (ZIP 229). It SHOULD then verify every proof and signature of
that transaction, each $\mathsf{spend\_auth\_sig}$ under its $\rk$ on
$\mathsf{SigHash}$ included, so that an invalid transaction is found before
submission.
\end{construction}

A party that holds an Orchard-protocol output's recipient, value and
$\mathsf{rseed}$, with $\rho^{\mathsf{new}}$ the $\nfsym$ of its Action's
spend, recovers the full note plaintext, memo included, without
$\mathsf{ock}$ or any viewing key, by the derivation of $\mathsf{esk}$
stated with Theorem~\ref{thm:pipeline-integrity}
(\S\ref{sec:pipeline-authorise};
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Encryption to the recipient'', Proposition
``Decryption correctness''; ZIP 212). The random $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$ of a
fabricated output decrypts to nothing, as the construction intends.

\section{The transaction lifecycle}\label{sec:lifecycle}

This section states what a wallet does with a transaction between its
creation and a terminal state: the encoding it submits and the identifier by
which it tracks the transaction; the rule that a transaction is recorded
before it is released, with the completeness of the record that follows;
submission and rebroadcast; the status observations that update the record;
and the terminal states, re-sending and the consensus branch. A transaction
is \emph{created} when it is built, proved and authorised from one step of a
plan (Definition~\ref{def:plan}; Constructions~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}
and~\ref{def:authorise}). The record is that of
Definition~\ref{def:spend-record}.

\subsection{Submission and the transaction identifier}
\label{sec:life-submit}

The wallet serialises a created transaction in the encoding of its version:
version~$5$ (ZIP 225, ``Transaction Format'') or version~$6$ (ZIP 229,
``Transaction Format''), the only versions in force; a transaction with
Ironwood-pool Actions has version~$6$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The
transaction format'', Construction ``Version-6 transaction''; protocol
specification, \S``Transaction Encoding and Consensus''). The argument of
$\mathsf{Submit}$ (Definition~\ref{def:service-queries}) is this byte
string. The encoding is cited, not restated.

The wallet tracks a transaction $t$ by its \emph{transaction identifier}
$\mathsf{txid}(t)$: the root of the BLAKE2b-256 digest tree of ZIP 244 over
the effecting data of $t$,
\[
  \mathsf{txid}(t) = \mathsf{h}\bigl(\texttt{ZcashTxHash\_} \,\|\, \beta,\
    d_{\mathsf{hdr}} \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{tr}} \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{Sap}}
    \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{Orc}} \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{Irw}}\bigr),
\]
where $\mathsf{h}(P, x)$ is BLAKE2b-256 of $x$ under the $16$-byte
personalisation $P$, $\beta$ is the $4$-byte little-endian consensus branch
identifier, and the child $d_{\mathsf{Irw}}$ is absent in version~$5$
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'', Definition
``Transaction identifier'', for version~$6$, and Remark ``Version 5''; ZIP 244,
``txid\_digest''; ZIP 229, ``Transaction Identifiers, Auth Digests, and
Signature Digests''; protocol specification, \S``Transaction Identifiers''). The
header digest $d_{\mathsf{hdr}}$ hashes, under $\texttt{ZTxIdHeadersHash}$, the
$4$-byte little-endian encodings of $\mathsf{header}$,
$\mathsf{nVersionGroupId}$, $\mathsf{nConsensusBranchId}$, $\mathsf{lock\_time}$
and $\mathsf{nExpiryHeight}$ (ZIP 244, ``T.1: header\_digest''), so the expiry
height $N$ of Definition~\ref{def:tx-expiry} is effecting data. The wallet
computes $\mathsf{txid}(t)$ from $t$ and needs no response of the server to know
it.

\begin{lemma}[Non-malleability of the identifier]\label{lem:life-txid}
Let $t$ be a transaction of version~$5$ or~$6$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Replacing the authorising data of $t$, namely its proofs, signatures
  and transparent scripts and, in version~$6$, its anchors
  ($\mathsf{anchorSapling}$, $\mathsf{anchorOrchard}$ and
  $\mathsf{anchorIronwood}$, ZIP 229, ``Anchor commitment (version 6)'';
  \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'',
  Definition ``Authorising-data digest; effecting and authorising data''),
  leaves $\mathsf{txid}(t)$ unchanged.
\item Under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Collision resistance of
  BLAKE2b'' (\S``Transaction digests and signatures''), no efficient algorithm
  outputs, except with negligible probability, two transactions with different
  effecting data and equal identifiers.
\item Under the same assumption, a transaction built for one consensus
  branch and the same payment built for another have different
  identifiers, except with negligible probability.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i)~Authorising data lie under the authorising-data digest and under no
node of the tree whose root is $\mathsf{txid}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Transaction digests and signatures'', Definition ``Authorising-data
digest; effecting and authorising data'').
(ii)~Each node of the tree hashes an injective encoding (fixed-size fields
and length-prefixed scripts, concatenated in order) of effecting data
or of the digests of its children (ZIP 244, ``txid\_digest''). If the
effecting data differ, the inputs of some node differ. A node whose inputs
differ either has equal digests, which is a collision, or passes different
digests to its parent, whose inputs then differ; at the root, equal
identifiers from different inputs are a collision. (iii)~The branch identifier
enters both the root personalisation $\texttt{ZcashTxHash\_} \,\|\, \beta$
and the header digest through $\mathsf{nConsensusBranchId}$, so the two root
pairs differ, and equal identifiers are a collision (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Transaction digests and signatures'', Remark ``Consensus-branch binding'').
\end{proof}

By~(i) and~(ii), the identifier tracks a pending transaction from its
creation on, before and after any re-signing or re-proving. The identifier of
versions up to~$4$, the double SHA-256 of the encoding (protocol
specification, \S``Transaction Identifiers''), is malleable; it belongs to no
