version in force and is not used.

\begin{construction}[Recording and submission]
The following rules are designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\begin{enumerate}[label=(L\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Recording before release.} The wallet enters each transaction
  $t$ it creates into its transaction record
  (Definition~\ref{def:spend-record}), which locks the notes $t$ spends
  (Definition~\ref{def:spend-lock}), in storage that survives restarts and
  rollbacks (Definition~\ref{def:tree-rollback}, item~(v)), before it
  submits $t$ or releases its bytes to any other party. It never removes an
  entry of the record.
\item \emph{Multi-step plans.} For a plan of $\ell > 1$ steps
  (Definitions~\ref{def:plan} and~\ref{def:plan-wellformed}), the wallet
  creates every step before it records any, records all $\ell$ transactions
  in one update of the record, and submits them after that update, in step
  order. A construction that fails at some step leaves no recorded, hence
  by~(L1) no transmittable, prefix. Every step can be created before any is
  mined, since a well-formed plan chains steps only through transparent
  outputs (Proposition~\ref{prop:plan-chaining}); the transfer to a TEX
  address of Construction~\ref{def:tex-transfer} is the instance (ZIP 320,
  ``Design considerations for Senders'').
% .wip/leftovers/scripts/wallet_expiry_margin.py: ZIP 204 threshold 3; smallest
%   relayed margin N - T is 4 if t must not be expired at T + 1 + 3, 3 if it
%   must not be expired at T + 3
\item \emph{Submission.} The wallet submits $t$ by
  $\mathsf{Submit}(\mathit{bytes})$, whose response is
  $\mathsf{accepted}(\mathsf{txid})$ or $\mathsf{rejected}$
  (Definition~\ref{def:service-queries}). Neither response changes the record.
  It submits at a tip $T$ that leaves a margin of blocks, a wallet parameter,
  before the last includable height $N$ (Definition~\ref{def:tx-expiry}), rather
  than at $T = N - 1$. The margin $N - T$ exceeds the threshold of ZIP 204,
  ``Transaction Expiry'': a node SHOULD NOT relay a transaction that will
  expire within $3$ blocks of its view of the tip.
\item \emph{Rebroadcast.} While $t$ is pending and unmined
  (Definition~\ref{def:life-states}), the wallet resubmits it at intervals
  whenever, at its tip $T$, $N = 0$ or $T < N$
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:tx-includable}) and no transaction included in its view
  of the chain spends an input of $t$. For $N = 0$ this continues until $t$
  is mined or an input of $t$ is spent by a mined transaction. The rule is
  motivated by ZIP 203, ``Specification'', under which nodes drop a
  transaction from their mempools at its expiry.
\end{enumerate}
\end{construction}

The semantics of the two responses of $\mathsf{Submit}$ are those of the
server's node and are fixed by no ZIP: $\mathsf{accepted}$ means that the
node admitted $t$ to its mempool, $\mathsf{rejected}$ that it refused it.
Under clause~(a) of
Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}, acceptance implies neither propagation to
other nodes nor inclusion in a block, since relay policy is local to each
node and may refuse a transaction that consensus still admits in the next
block (Lemma~\ref{lem:tx-includable}); and rejection does not imply that
$t$ is invalid. Rule~(L3) keeps a margin for this reason. Each submission is
an observation of the server (Proposition~\ref{prop:priv-submit}).

\begin{proposition}[Completeness of the transaction record]
\label{prop:life-record}
Let the wallet follow rule~(L1), and let no party other than the wallet
hold the bytes of a created transaction $t$ before the wallet submits them.
Then, if a block of any chain includes a transaction with the effecting data
of $t$, the record contains $t$, and the included transaction has identifier
$\mathsf{txid}(t)$, except with negligible probability. The converse fails: a
recorded transaction that the network never received is stale, not lost,
and, for $N \ne 0$, reaches the
terminal state expired unmined of Definition~\ref{def:life-states}; for
$N = 0$ it stays pending by item~(c) until the end of its consensus branch
is known, and then reaches expired unmined with the bound $N^{*}$
(``Consensus-branch bound'' below).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A block includes a transaction only if the party that assembled the block
held its bytes. The first party other than the wallet to hold bytes of $t$
received them by the wallet's submission, by hypothesis; by~(L1) the wallet
recorded $t$ before that event, and the entry survives restarts and
rollbacks and is never removed. A valid transaction with the effecting data of
$t$ carries, for each spend of $t$ other than a dummy, a signature under a key
of the wallet on the signature digest those data fix; except with negligible
probability no party obtains such a signature except from bytes the wallet
released (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``RedPallas unforgeability'', Proposition
``Unforgeability under re-randomisation'', for Orchard-protocol spends; for
transparent and Sapling spends the unforgeability of those schemes is assumed,
as in \S\ref{sec:pipeline-authorise}, where no lower volume proves it). Bytes
derived from those of $t$ by another party with the effecting data of $t$ differ
from $t$ at most in authorising data, so the included transaction has identifier
$\mathsf{txid}(t)$ by part~(i) of the lemma above. The converse fails because
recording does not imply delivery.
\end{proof}

Where a partially created transaction is extracted by another party
(``Spend finalisation and extraction'', \S\ref{sec:pczt-extract}), that
party holds the bytes of $t$ before any submission, and the hypothesis is
arranged differently: the wallet records $\mathsf{txid}(t)$, $N$ and the
inputs of $t$, locking them, before it releases the partially created
transaction to any party that may extract it, and at a point where the
effecting data of $t$ are final. After IO finalisation
(Construction~\ref{def:pczt-io-final}) of a partially created transaction
with shielded spends or outputs, or, for one that is transparent-only, once
signatures without $\mathsf{SIGHASH\_ANYONECANPAY}$ and without
$\mathsf{SIGHASH\_NONE}$ have cleared bits~$0$ and~$1$ of
$\mathsf{tx\_modifiable}$ (ZIP 374, ``Global''), the inputs and outputs are
final, the
lock time is determined by Definition~\ref{def:pczt-locktime}, and the
proofs and signatures still to be added are authorising data; by part~(i) of
the lemma the extracted transaction then has the recorded identifier, and
the proof above applies with release in place of submission. The entry
acquires the encoding of $t$ when the wallet obtains it.

\begin{remark}[Retained data]
From creation until it is mined, $t$ exists only in the wallet's record,
in remote mempools and, for an extracted partially created transaction, at
the extracting party. Once the wallet holds the encoding of $t$
(Definition~\ref{def:spend-record}, item~(i)) the record keeps it, and
rule~(L4) needs it; before that the wallet does not rebroadcast $t$. It may
hold data that the chain never will: a transaction that ends expired and
unmined lies on no chain that agrees with the server's up to $N$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:life-expired}), and its entry may be the only trace of it.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Status tracking and terminal states}\label{sec:life-status}

\begin{remark}[Scope of scanning]
Scanning detects a transaction only through a nullifier of the watched set
$W$ (Definition~\ref{def:spend-watched}) or an output accepted under the
wallet's scanning keys (Construction~\ref{def:scan-block}). A recorded
transaction that spends no note of $W$ and has no such output, every fully
transparent transaction among them, is invisible to scanning. Its fate is
learnt only by queries $\mathsf{Status}(\mathsf{txid})$
(Definition~\ref{def:service-status}), each of which puts its identifier
into the view of the server (Proposition~\ref{prop:priv-txq}).
\end{remark}

For a recorded transaction $t$ write $N$ for its expiry height, $h_t$ for
its mined height ($\bot$ while unmined), $h^{\mathrm{obs}}_t$ for its
earliest observation height, and $u_t$ for the latest tip at which the
server reported it unmined ($\bot$ initially): items~(ii), (iv), (v)
and~(vi) of Definition~\ref{def:spend-record}. A \emph{status observation}
$(s, T)$ is a response $s$ of $\mathsf{Status}(\mathsf{txid}(t))$ received
while the wallet's view of the chain has tip $T$.

\begin{construction}[Status transitions]
The record entry of $t$ changes only as follows.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item On an observation $(s, T)$ with $s \in \{\mathsf{unknown},
  \mathsf{unmined}\}$: $u_t := T$.
\item On an observation $(\mathsf{mined}(h'), T)$: $h_t := h'$,
  $h^{\mathrm{obs}}_t := \min(h^{\mathrm{obs}}_t, h')$ and $u_t := \bot$.
\item Scanning a block of height $h'$ in which $t$ is wallet-relevant
  (Construction~\ref{def:scan-block}) has the effect of~(ii).
\item A rollback to $r$ (Definition~\ref{def:tree-rollback}) sets $h_t :=
  \bot$ if $h_t > r$, and $u_t := \min(u_t, r)$ if $u_t \ne \bot$, since
  a report at a tip above $r$ concerned blocks that the rollback discards.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Query termination.} The wallet issues $\mathsf{Status}(\mathsf{txid}(t))$
for a pending transaction $t$ with $h_t = \bot$: if $N \ne 0$, until $u_t
\ge N$, that is, until $t$ is reported unmined at a tip of at least $N$; if
$N = 0$, for as long as $t$ is pending and unmined. No rule stops at a
first report of $\mathsf{unmined}$ or at a fixed depth after first
observation. A rollback that lowers $u_t$ below $N$, or sets $h_t := \bot$,
resumes the queries. The transitions and the termination rule are designed
but unspecified (Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}); the termination rule
rests on the consensus rule of Definition~\ref{def:tx-expiry} (ZIP 203,
``Specification''). For an entry with $N_t$ unknown
(Definition~\ref{def:spend-record}), the wallet obtains the encoding by
$\mathsf{Tx}(\mathsf{txid})$ (Definition~\ref{def:service-queries}); until
then it queries as for $N = 0$, does not rebroadcast, and its inputs are
locked by condition~(d) of Definition~\ref{def:spend-lock}.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Expired unmined is final on its chain]\label{lem:life-expired}
Assume clause~(a) of Assumption~\ref{ass:service-hbc}. Let $t$ be a
transaction created by the wallet, with expiry height $N \ne 0$, and let
$(s, T)$ be an observation with $s \in \{\mathsf{unknown},
\mathsf{unmined}\}$ and $T \ge N$, answered from a best chain $C$ of the
server (\emph{Consensus Guide}, \S``Work, and the best chain'') whose blocks
at heights at most $N$ are those of the wallet's view. Then no chain whose
blocks at heights at most $N$ are those of $C$ includes $t$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The wallet's view has tip $T \ge N$, so $C$ has blocks at every height up
to $N$. By clause~(a), $s$ is the status of $\mathsf{txid}(t)$ on $C$
(Definition~\ref{def:service-status}): no block of $C$ contains $t$, so no
block of height at most $N$ of a chain that agrees with $C$ up to $N$ does.
The transaction $t$ is not a coinbase transaction, and by
Lemma~\ref{lem:tx-includable} the expiry rule permits it in no block of
height greater than $N$ (protocol specification, \S``Transaction Consensus
Rules''; ZIP 203, ``Specification'').
\end{proof}

A reorganisation with fork point below $N$ reopens the question, since the
new chain need not agree with $C$ up to $N$. The wallet meets it as a
continuity error (Definition~\ref{def:scan-continuity}), and the rollback of
Construction~\ref{def:tree-recover} to some $r < N$ sets $u_t \le r < N$ by
transition~(iv), which resumes the queries.

Let $B$ be the birthday of the wallet (Definition~\ref{def:scan-birthday})
and $\mathcal{H}$ its retained block boundaries
(Definition~\ref{def:tree-retained}). Write $r_{\min}$ for the least element
of $\mathcal{H} \setminus \{B - 1\}$, the lowest rollback point of the
rollback window, and $r_{\min} := B - 1$ when that set is empty.

\begin{definition}[Terminal states]\label{def:life-states}
A recorded transaction $t$ is \emph{pending} from its recording until it
reaches one of the following states.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item \emph{Mined}: $h_t \ne \bot$ and $h_t \le r_{\min}$, so that no
  rollback to a point of the window unmines $t$. The state is terminal
  relative to the rollback window only, since consensus bounds no
  reorganisation depth (Remark~\ref{rem:tree-window}); a deeper rollback,
  to $B - 1$ or to a fetched chain state in steps~(1) and~(3) of
  Construction~\ref{def:tree-recover}, returns $t$ to pending by
  transition~(iv).
\item \emph{Expired unmined}: $N \ne 0$, $h_t = \bot$ and $u_t \ge N$,
  which gives the conclusion of Lemma~\ref{lem:life-expired} when the
  server's chain agrees with the wallet's view up to $N$; a disagreement
  surfaces as a continuity error (Definition~\ref{def:scan-continuity}),
  whose rollback to $r < N$ returns $t$ to pending by transition~(iv). Its
  inputs are released
  (Proposition~\ref{prop:spend-lock}, every later target height exceeding
  $N$), its entry is kept, and the user is notified.
\item For $N = 0$, a value chosen explicitly: $t$ remains pending until it
  is mined, rebroadcast by rule~(L4), or, once the end of its consensus
  branch is known, until it reaches state~(b) with the bound $N^{*}$ of
  that branch (``Consensus-branch bound'' below). Its mining is detected by
  scanning through $W$, from which a spend, $N = 0$ included, removes a
  nullifier only once mined (Definition~\ref{def:spend-watched}), or by
  continued status queries.
\end{enumerate}
The states and their transitions are designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). The notification is specified: the
wallet should notify the user of expired transactions that must be re-sent,
and user interfaces and services must never rely on zero-confirmation
transactions (ZIP 203, ``Wallet behavior and UI'').
\end{definition}

\begin{figure}[tp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[
  font=\footnotesize,
  >={Stealth[length=2mm]},
  st/.style={rectangle, rounded corners=2pt, draw=tierin, line width=0.8pt,
    fill=tierin!8, align=center, inner sep=4pt, minimum height=9mm},
  tm/.style={st, draw=tierrecv, fill=tierrecv!12, line width=1.1pt},
  tx/.style={st, draw=tierfull, fill=tierfull!12, line width=1.1pt},
  ed/.style={->, line width=0.6pt, black!70},
  rb/.style={->, line width=0.7pt, tierspend, dashed},
  lb/.style={font=\scriptsize, inner sep=1.5pt, align=center},
]
\node[st] (cr) at (-0.6,0) {created};
\node[st] (pu) at (4,0) {pending, unmined\\$h_t = \bot$};
\node[st] (pm) at (9.2,0) {pending, mined\\$h_t > r_{\min}$};
\node[tm] (mt) at (9.2,-3.2) {mined\\$h_t \le r_{\min}$};
\node[tx] (ex) at (4,-3.2) {expired unmined\\$N \ne 0$, $u_t \ge N$};
\draw[ed] (cr) -- node[lb, above] {record, (L1)} node[lb, below]
  {inputs locked} (pu);
\draw[ed] ([yshift=2mm]pu.east) -- node[lb, above]
  {(ii) $\mathsf{mined}(h')$,} node[lb, below] {(iii) scan}
  ([yshift=2mm]pm.west);
\draw[rb] ([yshift=-2.5mm]pm.west) -- node[lb, below] {(iv) $r < h_t$}
  ([yshift=-2.5mm]pu.east);
\draw[ed] (pm) -- node[lb, right] {window\\advances} (mt);
\draw[rb] (mt.west) -- node[lb, above right, pos=0.5]
  {(iv) deeper\\rollback} ([xshift=-2mm]pu.south east);
\draw[ed] ([xshift=-4mm]pu.south) -- node[lb, left]
  {(i) at $T \ge N$} ([xshift=-4mm]ex.north);
\draw[rb] ([xshift=4mm]ex.north) -- node[lb, right, pos=0.4]
  {(iv) $r < N$} ([xshift=4mm]pu.south);
\draw[ed] (pu.north west) .. controls +(-0.3,1.2) and +(-0.9,1.2)
  .. node[lb, above] {Submit, (L3), (L4); (i) at $T < N$}
  ([xshift=4mm]pu.north);
\node[lb, anchor=west, text width=3.2cm, align=left] at (-1.3,-2.3)
  {$N = 0$: pending until mined or until its branch ends ($N^{*}$)};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The states of a recorded transaction $t$
(Definition~\ref{def:life-states}). Solid edges are transitions of the
record: recording by rule~(L1), which locks the inputs of $t$; submission,
rebroadcast and reports $\mathsf{unmined}$ or $\mathsf{unknown}$ at a tip
below $N$, which leave $t$ pending; a report $\mathsf{mined}(h')$ or the
scan of a block containing $t$, transitions~(ii) and~(iii); burial below the
lowest rollback point $r_{\min}$ of the window; and, for $N \ne 0$, a report
of an unmined $t$ at a tip $T \ge N$, after which
Lemma~\ref{lem:life-expired} excludes $t$ from every chain that agrees with
the server's up to $N$. Dashed red edges are rollbacks, transition~(iv):
below $h_t$ from either mined state, from the terminal one only by a
rollback deeper than the window, and below $N$ from expired unmined. The
green and amber states are terminal; a transaction with $N = 0$ stays
pending until mined, unless its consensus branch ends.}
\label{fig:lifecycle}
\end{figure}

\begin{remark}[Re-sending]
Re-sending an expired payment usually spends the same TXOs, and ZIP 315
lists which spends of given TXOs are repeated across transactions among the
kinds of information leakage, the case arising when a previous transaction
expired (ZIP 315, ``Kinds of information leakage''): the repeated spends
link the expired transaction to its replacement. Selecting other notes
avoids that link, but the nullifiers that the expired transaction revealed
still link it to any later transaction that spends those notes (ZIP 315,
``Rationale for anchor selection''). A wallet that re-sends accepts one of
the two links. ZIP 315, ``Note selection'', records as a TODO, not as a
rule, that such revealed nullifiers be spent in a note-management
transaction; how to avoid both links is an open problem
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{remark}

\emph{Consensus-branch bound.} The consensus branch bounds a pending transaction
as well as its expiry height does. An authorisation binds the consensus branch
identifier through $\beta$ and $\mathsf{nConsensusBranchId}$, which must equal
the identifier of the branch under which $t$ is validated, so a transaction
authorised for one branch is invalid under another whatever its expiry height
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'', Remark
``Consensus-branch binding'', under its Assumption ``Collision resistance of
BLAKE2b''; ZIP 244, ``txid\_digest'' and ``T.1: header\_digest''; ZIP 259,
``Backward compatibility''; protocol specification, \S``Transaction Consensus
Rules''). A pending transaction built for a branch that ends at a height $X$,
with $N = 0$ or $X \le N$, can be mined only below $X$. Once $X$ is known, write
$N^{*} := X - 1$ if $N = 0$ or $N \ge X$, and $N^{*} := N$ otherwise. Rule~(L4),
the query termination of Construction ``Status transitions'', state~(b) of
Definition~\ref{def:life-states} and conditions~(b) and~(c) of
Definition~\ref{def:spend-lock} apply with $N^{*}$ in place of $N$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:life-expired} holds with $N^{*}$, by the branch binding in place
of the expiry rule. Thus $t$ becomes expired unmined from a report at a tip of
at least $N^{*}$, and its inputs are released for the rebuild at every target
height $H \ge X$. This rule is designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). To pay across the activation of the next
branch, the wallet rebuilds and re-authorises the payment for the branch in
force at its target height (Definition~\ref{def:spend-target}): a new
transaction, with a new identifier by part~(iii) of the lemma of
\S\ref{sec:life-submit} and a new entry of the record.

\section{Payment requests (ZIP 321)}\label{sec:zip321}

This section states the encoding by which a payee states payments to a
payer. It gives the URI grammar of ZIP 321, the grouping of parameters into
payments and the conditions under which a parser accepts a URI; then the
meaning of the address, amount, memo, label and message parameters, the
capacity of one request, and the passage from an accepted request to a
transaction plan.

\subsection{The URI grammar}\label{sec:zip321-uri}

A payment request precedes planning (\S\ref{sec:pipeline-plan}): the payee
states to the payer, in a form that a wallet reads mechanically, the
recipient addresses, amounts and memos of the payments it asks for, and ZIP
321 fixes this encoding as a URI scheme. Its terms \emph{payment} and
\emph{payment request} (ZIP 321, ``Terminology'') are those of
Definition~\ref{def:plan-payment}. Everything that follows once the
recipients are fixed is constructed in the earlier sections of this volume
and in the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Note encryption'', \S``Trial
decryption and note acceptance'', \S``Authentication paths'',
\S``Anchors'' and \S``Transaction digests and signatures''; this section
re-derives none of it. The status of ZIP 321 is that of
Table~\ref{tab:intro-zips}.

\begin{definition}[Payment request URI]\label{def:zip321-request}
Let $L$ be the set of strings derived from \texttt{zcashurn} in the
following grammar in ABNF (RFC 5234), reproduced from ZIP 321, ``URI
Syntax'', with its longest rule continued on an indented line:
{\small
\begin{verbatim}
zcashurn       = "zcash:" ( zcashaddress [ "?" zcashparams ]
                 / "?" zcashparams )
zcashaddress   = 1*( ALPHA / DIGIT )
zcashparams    = zcashparam [ "&" zcashparams ]
zcashparam     = [ addrparam / amountparam / assetparam / memoparam
                 / messageparam / labelparam / otherreqparam / otherparam ]
NONZERO        = %x31-39
DIGIT          = %x30-39
paramindex     = "." NONZERO 0*3DIGIT
addrparam      = "address"   [ paramindex ] "=" zcashaddress
amountparam    = "amount"    [ paramindex ] "=" 1*DIGIT [ "." 1*8DIGIT ]
assetparam     = "req-asset" [ paramindex ] "=" *base64url
labelparam     = "label"     [ paramindex ] "=" *qchar
memoparam      = "memo"      [ paramindex ] "=" *base64url
messageparam   = "message"   [ paramindex ] "=" *qchar
paramname      = ALPHA *( ALPHA / DIGIT / "+" / "-" )
otherreqparam  = "req-" paramname [ paramindex ] [ "=" *qchar ]
otherparam     = paramname [ paramindex ] [ "=" *qchar ]
qchar          = unreserved / pct-encoded / allowed-delims / ":" / "@"
allowed-delims = "!" / "$" / "'" / "(" / ")" / "*" / "+" / "," / ";"
\end{verbatim}}
\noindent Here \texttt{ALPHA}, \texttt{unreserved} and \texttt{pct-encoded}
are those of RFC 3986, Appendix~A, and \texttt{base64url} is the alphabet of
RFC 4648, Section~5, in which the values $62$ and $63$ are written
\texttt{-} and \texttt{\_}, with padding omitted. The grammar is read as
follows: a parameter whose name is \texttt{address}, \texttt{amount},
\texttt{label}, \texttt{memo}, \texttt{message} or \texttt{req-asset} is
derived only by its own production, and \texttt{otherreqparam} and
\texttt{otherparam} derive only parameters with other names. A
\emph{payment request URI} is a string of $L$ that meets the acceptance
conditions~(a) to~(f) stated below in this subsection.
\end{definition}

Without the reading, \texttt{otherparam} would derive
\texttt{amount=1\%30}, with name \texttt{amount}, no index and a
\texttt{qchar} value; ZIP 321, ``Invalid Examples'', rejects a URI with
this parameter.

\emph{Surface forms.} The grammar derives no \texttt{//} after the scheme,
so a payment request URI has no authority component and is not
hierarchical in the sense of RFC 3986, Section~1.2.3; ZIP 321, ``Invalid
Examples'', rejects \texttt{zcash://} followed by an address. A URI
$\texttt{zcash:}A\texttt{?}P$, with $A$ a \texttt{zcashaddress}, MUST be
treated as $\texttt{zcash:?address=}A\texttt{\&}P$ (ZIP 321, ``URI
Semantics''). A request for a single payment SHOULD place its address in
the hier-part; several payments are written with \texttt{addrparam} at
distinct indices (same section). By this equivalence and condition~(b)
below, a URI $\texttt{zcash:}A\texttt{?address=}B\texttt{\&}\dots$ is not
accepted: after the rewriting it carries \texttt{address} twice at the empty
index.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, zip321.py:
% [sec:zip321-uri] paramindex = '.' NONZERO 0*3DIGIT: indices 1..9999 (9999
%   values, no leading zeros, not 0), plus the empty index: at most 10,000
%   payments
\emph{Indices and grouping.} Identify the empty index with $0$ and let
$J := \{0, 1, \dots, 9999\}$. The \emph{rendering} $\iota$ maps $0$ to the
empty string and $i \ge 1$ to $\texttt{.} \,\|\, \mathrm{dec}(i)$, where
$\mathrm{dec}(i)$ is the decimal representation of $i$ without leading
zeros. The strings that match \texttt{[ paramindex ]} are the empty string
and the strings $\texttt{.}\,d_1 \cdots d_k$ with $1 \le k \le 4$, $d_1 \in
\{\texttt{1}, \dots, \texttt{9}\}$ and $d_2, \dots, d_k$ decimal digits;
these are exactly the decimal representations without leading zeros of the
integers $1$ to $9999$. Hence $\iota$ is a bijection from $J$ onto the
index suffixes, and its inverse is parsing. The strings \texttt{.0},
\texttt{.01} and \texttt{.10000} match no production, and ZIP 321,
``Invalid Examples'', rejects \texttt{address.0=} and \texttt{amount.0=}.
Parameters whose suffixes denote the same index $i \in J$, the empty index
included, belong to one payment $P_i$; the order of the parameters is
insignificant, and indices need not be consecutive (ZIP 321, ``URI
Semantics''). A URI therefore decodes to a finite map $i \mapsto P_i$ on a
subset of $J$, and carries at most $|J| = 10{,}000$ payments: $9999$ at
indices $1$ to $9999$ and one at the empty index.

\emph{Lexical rules.} Percent encoding (RFC 3986, Section~2.1) occurs only
inside \texttt{qchar}, that is, only in the values of \texttt{label},
\texttt{message}, \texttt{otherreqparam} and \texttt{otherparam}.
Addresses, amounts, indices and parameter names are literal, so
\texttt{amount=1\%30}, \texttt{\%61mount=1} and an address beginning with
\texttt{\%74} are not in $L$ (ZIP 321, ``URI Syntax'' and ``Invalid
Examples''). The values of \texttt{memo} and \texttt{req-asset} are
\texttt{base64url} without padding, and implementations MUST NOT accept the
characters \texttt{+}, \texttt{/} and \texttt{=}, which occur only in the
standard base64 alphabet (ZIP 321, ``URI Syntax''; RFC 4648, Section~5).

\emph{Acceptance.} A parser accepts a string $u$ as a payment request URI
only if all of the following hold (ZIP 321, ``URI Syntax'', ``URI
Semantics'' and ``Forward compatibility''):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $u \in L$ under the reading of Definition~\ref{def:zip321-request}; a
  purported ZIP 321 URI that cannot be so parsed MUST NOT be accepted;
\item after the hier-part address is rewritten as \texttt{address} at the
  empty index, no pair of a parameter name and an index occurs twice (MUST
  NOT);
\item every index that carries a parameter other than \texttt{address}
  carries an \texttt{address} (MUST);
\item $u$ names no parameter with prefix \texttt{req-} that the parser does
  not recognise, since such a parameter makes the entire URI invalid (MUST);
  other unrecognised parameters are ignored (SHOULD);
\item the address, amount and memo rules of \S\ref{sec:zip321-params} hold;
\item $u$ decodes to at least one payment, a payment request containing one
  or more payments (ZIP 321, ``Terminology'').
\end{enumerate}
The prefix \texttt{req-} is part of the name of a parameter defined by a
later revision of ZIP 321, not a modifier of an arbitrary parameter; the
parameters \texttt{address}, \texttt{amount}, \texttt{label},
\texttt{memo} and \texttt{message} never carry it, since every conformant
parser is required to understand them (ZIP 321, ``Forward
compatibility''). An accepted URI asks for one transaction: implementations
SHOULD construct a single transaction that pays every address of the
request (ZIP 321, ``URI Semantics'').

\emph{Custom assets.} The parameter \texttt{req-asset} is specified in ZIP
321, ``Custom Assets'', with an encoding defined by ZIP 227, which the
protocol described in this volume does not include. A parser of this
protocol does not recognise \texttt{req-asset}, so by condition~(d) a URI
that carries it is invalid to that parser, and the further rule that
\texttt{amount} and \texttt{req-asset} exclude each other at one index
(ZIP 321, ``URI Semantics'') never applies.

\begin{remark}[Case of literals]\label{rem:zip321-case}
Quoted strings in ABNF are case-insensitive (RFC 5234, Section~2.3), so
$L$ contains strings beginning \texttt{ZCASH:} and strings with the
parameters \texttt{AMOUNT=1} and \texttt{REQ-ASSET=}; the value ranges
\texttt{NONZERO} and \texttt{DIGIT}, given as \texttt{\%x} ranges, and the
\texttt{base64url} alphabet are case-sensitive. ZIP 321 does not address
case: it states neither whether such URIs are to be accepted nor whether
\texttt{amount} and \texttt{AMOUNT} at one index are the same parameter for
condition~(b). The volume states the grammar as written and resolves
neither question; no parser's behaviour is normative. Class: open problem
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Parameter semantics}\label{sec:zip321-params}

\emph{Addresses.} Implementations SHOULD check that each
\texttt{zcashaddress} is a valid encoding of an address other than a Sprout
address under protocol specification, \S``Encodings of Addresses and
Keys'': a transparent address (\S``Transparent Addresses'', Base58Check), a
Sapling payment address (\S``Sapling Payment Addresses'', Bech32, ZIP 173)
or a unified address (\S``Unified Payment Addresses and Viewing Keys'' and
ZIP 316, Bech32m, BIP 350). Address formats later added to that section
SHOULD be supported; where the network of the request is known, each
address SHOULD be checked to belong to it; and every requirement of ZIP 316
applies to a unified address (ZIP 321, ``URI Semantics''). Sprout
addresses MUST NOT be supported (same section). ZIP 321 gives as its reason
the restriction of ZIP 211 on adding value to the Sprout pool; in the
protocol described, version-$4$ transactions are invalid, and Sprout
transfers exist only in transactions of versions $2$ to $4$, so Sprout
funds are unspendable (ZIP 2003, ``Abstract''; ZIP 259, ``Abstract'';
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The transaction format''). A TEX address (ZIP
320) is in no section of the specification, and ZIP 321 names none; its
reading as the recipient address of item~4 of
Definition~\ref{def:addr-recipient} is designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}). The addresses of an accepted request
are thus recipient addresses in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:addr-recipient}.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, zip321.py:
% [sec:zip321-params] COIN = 10^8; MAX_MONEY = 21,000,000 x 10^8 =
%   2,100,000,000,000,000 zatoshi
% Cited numbers: 21,000,000 ZEC: ZIP 321, 'ZEC Transfer Amount'
\begin{construction}[Amount codec]\label{def:zip321-amount}
Let $\mathsf{COIN} = 10^8$ zatoshi per ZEC (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
Definition ``Unit of value'' in \S``Notes'') and $\mathsf{MAX\_MONEY} = 2.1
\cdot 10^{15}$ zatoshi (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The transaction
format''), so that $21{,}000{,}000$ ZEC is $\mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}$ zatoshi.
For a string $x$ of decimal digits, let $\mathrm{int}(x)$ be the natural
number it denotes, leading zeros ignored, and let $\mathtt{0}^k$ be the
string of $k$ characters \texttt{0}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Parse.} Let $s$ match \texttt{1*DIGIT [ "." 1*8DIGIT ]}, and
  write $s = c$ or $s = c \,\|\, \texttt{.} \,\|\, f$ with $c$ its whole
  part and $f$ its fraction. Set $z := \mathrm{int}(f \,\|\,
  \mathtt{0}^{8-|f|})$, or $z := 0$ without a fraction, and
  \[
    v := \mathrm{int}(c) \cdot \mathsf{COIN} + z .
  \]
  Accept $s$ if and only if $v \le \mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}$; an accepted $s$
  denotes $v$ zatoshi, and $\mathrm{parse}(s) := v$.
\item \emph{Render.} For $v \in \{0, \dots, \mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}\}$ write
  $v = c \cdot \mathsf{COIN} + z$ with $0 \le z < \mathsf{COIN}$. Let
  $\mathrm{render}(v)$ be $\mathrm{dec}(c)$ if $z = 0$, and otherwise
  $\mathrm{dec}(c) \,\|\, \texttt{.} \,\|\, f$, where $f$ is the
  $8$-digit zero-padded decimal representation of $z$ with its trailing
  characters \texttt{0} removed.
\end{enumerate}
Leading zeros of the whole part and trailing zeros of the fraction are
ignored, the bound $\mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}$ is ZIP 321's ``MUST NOT be greater
than 21000000 ZEC'', and a string with a comma or other grouping separator,
with an empty whole part (\texttt{.5}), an empty fraction (\texttt{50.}) or
more than $8$ fraction digits matches no production (ZIP 321, ``ZEC
Transfer Amount''). ZIP 321 fixes only the value that an amount denotes;
the rendering is canonical, not required.
\end{construction}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, zip321.py:
% [sec:zip321-params] lem:zip321-amount: parse(render(v)) = v for v in {0,
%   1, 10^8, MAX_MONEY - 1, MAX_MONEY} and 10^5 random v in [0,
%   MAX_MONEY]; render(MAX_MONEY) = 21000000, render(1) = 0.00000001
\begin{lemma}[Round trip of amounts]\label{lem:zip321-amount}
For every integer $v$ with $0 \le v \le \mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}$, the string
$\mathrm{render}(v)$ matches \texttt{1*DIGIT [ "." 1*8DIGIT ]} and
$\mathrm{parse}(\mathrm{render}(v)) = v$. The map $\mathrm{parse}$ is not
injective. For every accepted $s$, $\mathrm{render}(\mathrm{parse}(s))$ is
the unique string of value $\mathrm{parse}(s)$ whose whole part has no
leading zero unless it is the single character \texttt{0}, whose fraction
has no trailing zero, and which has no fraction when $\mathrm{parse}(s)$ is
a multiple of $\mathsf{COIN}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $v = c \cdot \mathsf{COIN} + z$ with $0 \le z < \mathsf{COIN}$. The
string $\mathrm{dec}(c)$ is non-empty. If $z = 0$, then $\mathrm{render}(v)
= \mathrm{dec}(c)$ matches the production, and $\mathrm{parse}$ returns $c
\cdot \mathsf{COIN} = v$. If $z \ne 0$, the $8$-digit form of $z$ has a
non-zero digit, so removing its trailing zeros leaves a fraction $f$ with
$1 \le |f| \le 8$, and the production matches. Appending $8 - |f|$ zeros
to $f$ restores the $8$-digit form of $z$, so $\mathrm{parse}$ returns $c
\cdot \mathsf{COIN} + z = v$, and accepts since $v \le \mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}$.
The strings \texttt{50}, \texttt{050} and \texttt{50.00} all denote
$5{,}000{,}000{,}000$ zatoshi, so $\mathrm{parse}$ is not injective.
Finally, an accepted $s$ determines $(\mathrm{int}(c), z)$, which are the
quotient and remainder of $\mathrm{parse}(s)$ by $\mathsf{COIN}$ since $z <
\mathsf{COIN}$. A string of the stated form is fixed by these two numbers:
its whole part is $\mathrm{dec}(\mathrm{int}(c))$, and its fraction is
absent if $z = 0$ and otherwise the $8$-digit form of $z$ without trailing
zeros. That string is $\mathrm{render}(\mathrm{parse}(s))$.
\end{proof}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, zip321.py:
% [sec:zip321-params] amount examples (ZIP 321): 1 -> 100,000,000; 123.456
%   -> 12,345,600,000; 0.789 -> 78,900,000; 50 -> 5,000,000,000; 050 ->
%   5,000,000,000; 50.00 -> 5,000,000,000; 0.5 -> 50,000,000; 00.500 ->
%   50,000,000; 21000000 -> 2,100,000,000,000,000; 21000000.00000001 ->
%   invalid; 50,000.00 -> invalid; 50,00 -> invalid; 50. -> invalid; .5 ->
%   invalid; 0.123456789 -> invalid
% [sec:zip321-uri] paramindex = '.' NONZERO 0*3DIGIT: indices 1..9999 (9999
%   values, no leading zeros, not 0), plus the empty index: at most 10,000
%   payments
\emph{Examples.} On the examples of ZIP 321, ``ZEC Transfer Amount'' and
``Examples'', Construction~\ref{def:zip321-amount} gives the following. The
parameter \texttt{amount=1} denotes $100{,}000{,}000$ zatoshi,
\texttt{amount=123.456} denotes $12{,}345{,}600{,}000$ zatoshi, and
\texttt{amount.1=0.789} denotes $78{,}900{,}000$ zatoshi for the payment at
index $1$. The values \texttt{50}, \texttt{050} and \texttt{50.00} denote
$5{,}000{,}000{,}000$ zatoshi, and \texttt{0.5} and \texttt{00.500} denote
$50{,}000{,}000$ zatoshi. The value \texttt{21000000} denotes
$\mathsf{MAX\_MONEY} = 2{,}100{,}000{,}000{,}000{,}000$ zatoshi and is
accepted, while \texttt{21000000.00000001} exceeds $\mathsf{MAX\_MONEY}$ and
is rejected. The values \texttt{50,000.00}, \texttt{50,00}, \texttt{50.},
\texttt{.5} and \texttt{0.123456789} are invalid. The parameters
\texttt{address.0=} and \texttt{amount.0=} are invalid, their index having a
leading zero, and so is any parameter with the five-digit suffix
\texttt{.10000}.

\emph{Absent amount.} ZIP 321 gives a payment a value only ``if an
\texttt{amount} parameter is provided'' (ZIP 321, ``ZEC Transfer
Amount''). A payment without one has no value under ZIP 321, while
Definition~\ref{def:plan-payment} requires a value, so the payer fixes the
value before a plan is formed. Class: designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:intro-classes}).

% compute/OUTPUT.md, zip321.py:
% [sec:zip321-params] memo: base64url without padding; a full 512-byte memo
%   is ceil(512 x 8 / 6) = 683 characters; ZIP 321's example
%   'VGhpcyBpcyBhIHNpbXBsZSBtZW1vLg' (30 characters) decodes to the 22
%   bytes 'This is a simple memo.'; the no-memo value 0xF6 || 0^511 encodes
%   as '9g'; '+', '=' and 684 characters are rejected
\begin{construction}[Memo codec]\label{def:zip321-memo}
A \emph{memo} is a $512$-byte string $M$, the memo field of a note
plaintext (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The note plaintext''; protocol
specification, \S``Encodings of Note Plaintexts and Memo Fields''). Let
$\mathrm{b64u}$ be the encoding of RFC 4648, Section~5, with padding
omitted, and let $\mathrm{strip}(M)$ be $M$ with its trailing
$\mathtt{0x00}$ bytes removed.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Encode.} $\mathrm{Enc}(M) := \mathrm{b64u}(\mathrm{strip}(M))$,
  a string of at most $\lceil 512 \cdot 8 / 6 \rceil = 683$ characters.
\item \emph{Decode.} Reject a value that contains a character outside the
  \texttt{base64url} alphabet, in particular \texttt{+}, \texttt{/} or
  \texttt{=}, or whose length is congruent to $1$ modulo $4$, a length to
  which no octet string encodes. Otherwise let $b$ be its decoding under
  RFC 4648, Section~5, and require $|b| \le 512$ (MUST); then
  $\mathrm{Dec}(\mathit{value}) := b \,\|\, \mathtt{0x00}^{512 - |b|}$
  (ZIP 321, ``Query Keys'').
\end{enumerate}
A payment without a \texttt{memo} parameter has no memo
(Definition~\ref{def:plan-payment}); its output, if shielded, carries the
memo field that ZIP 302 designates as supplying no memo,
$\mathtt{0xF6} \,\|\, \mathtt{0x00}^{511}$, whose encoding is \texttt{9g}
(ZIP 302, ``Specification''). A parameter \texttt{memo=} with the empty
value differs from an absent parameter: it decodes to
$\mathtt{0x00}^{512}$, which a reader under ZIP 302 reads as the empty
text. If the address at the index of a \texttt{memo} parameter does not
permit memos, that is, is not memo-capable in the sense of predicate~(i)
of Definition~\ref{def:addr-recipient}, as for a transparent or TEX
address, parsers MUST consider the entire URI invalid (ZIP 321, ``Query
Keys''), since a memo travels only in a note plaintext.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Round trip of memos]\label{lem:zip321-memo}
For every memo $M$, $\mathrm{Dec}(\mathrm{Enc}(M)) = M$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The string
$\mathrm{Enc}(M)$ uses only the \texttt{base64url} alphabet, and an
unpadded encoding of $n$ octets has length $\lceil 8n/6 \rceil$, never
congruent to $1$ modulo $4$, so decoding does not reject it. The encoding
$\mathrm{b64u}$ is injective with inverse the decoding of RFC 4648, so $b =
\mathrm{strip}(M)$, and $|b| \le 512$. The bytes that $\mathrm{strip}$
removes are zeros, so appending $512 - |b|$ zeros to $b$ returns $M$.
\end{proof}

ZIP 321 does not say whether a value whose final character carries non-zero
unused bits (RFC 4648, Section~3.5) is accepted; the encoding $\mathrm{Enc}$
produces none. Stripping trailing zeros gives the shortest encoding and is
not required: $\mathrm{b64u}(M)$ decodes to the same memo. As an instance,
ZIP 321's example value \texttt{VGhpcyBpcyBhIHNpbXBsZSBtZW1vLg}, of $30$
characters, decodes to the $22$ bytes of the text ``This is a simple
memo.'', padded with zeros to $512$ bytes.

\emph{Labels and messages.} The \texttt{label} at an index names its
address, for example by the name of the recipient; a client that renders the
payment for inspection SHOULD display the label together with the address,
and a displayed label MUST be identifiable as distinct from the address.
The \texttt{message} at an index is descriptive text that a client may
display for that payment (ZIP 321, ``Query Keys''). Both values are
\texttt{qchar} strings, percent-decoded to octet strings; an octet outside
\texttt{qchar} reaches them only by percent encoding. Neither enters the
transaction (Definition~\ref{def:plan-payment}).

% compute/OUTPUT.md, zip321.py:
% [sec:zip321-params] Sapling output in a v5/v6 transaction: cv 32 + cmu 32
%   + epk 32 + C_enc 580 + C_out 80 + proof 192 = 948 bytes; floor(2,000,000
%   / 948) = 2109 (specification's size-limit argument)
% .wip/repin/scripts/wallet_zip218.py (zips 660b3f80):
% [sec:zip321-params] ZIP 218 per block: nActionsOrchard <= 330;
%   nActionsIronwood <= 330; nSpendsSapling + nOutputsSapling <= 300;
%   nJoinSplit <= 0; nActionsOrchard + nActionsIronwood + Sapling spends +
%   outputs + 2 nJoinSplit <= 330
% [sec:zip321-params] Sapling output >= 948 bytes, size bound 2109; budget
%   bound min(2109, 300) = 300
% [sec:zip321-params] Orchard-protocol Action >= 820 + 2272 + 64 = 3156
%   bytes, size bound 633 > 330, so the Ironwood bound is 330 - a_O - s - o
% Cited numbers: 330, 300, 0 and the 330-unit budget: ZIP 218, 'Shielded
%   pool action limits'; 820, 2272, 64: ZIP 229, 'Transaction Format'
\emph{Capacity.} A request carries at most $10{,}000$ payments
(\S\ref{sec:zip321-uri}), and the one transaction it asks for is bounded
further by consensus; the bound that binds is the least of the following.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{Size.} A block has at most $2{,}000{,}000$ bytes (protocol
  specification, \S``Block Header Encoding and Consensus''), and a Sapling
  output contributes at least $948$ bytes to a transaction, so a
  transaction has at most $\lfloor 2{,}000{,}000 / 948 \rfloor = 2109$
  Sapling outputs (protocol specification, \S``Balance and Binding
  Signature (Sapling)''). This is the bound that ZIP 321, ``URI
  Semantics'', states.
\item \emph{Shielded budget.} ZIP 218, part of NU7, requires of every
  block, with $S$ the number of Sapling spends plus outputs,
  $A_{\mathsf{O}}$ and $A_{\mathsf{I}}$ the numbers of Orchard-pool and
  Ironwood-pool Actions, and $n_{\mathsf{JS}}$ the number of JoinSplit
  descriptions, each summed over the block's transactions,
  \begin{gather*}
    S \le 300, \qquad A_{\mathsf{O}} \le 330, \qquad
    A_{\mathsf{I}} \le 330, \qquad n_{\mathsf{JS}} \le 0, \\
    A_{\mathsf{O}} + A_{\mathsf{I}} + S + 2\, n_{\mathsf{JS}} \le 330
  \end{gather*}
  (ZIP 218, ``Shielded pool action limits''); versions~$5$ and~$6$ carry
  no JoinSplit description in any case (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
  \S``The transaction format''). Each of these sums over a block is at
  least the term of any one of its transactions. A transaction that pays
  $n$ payments by Sapling outputs has $o \ge n$ Sapling outputs; with $s$
  Sapling spends and $a$ Actions of the two pools it is mined only if
  $s + o \le 300$ and $a + s + o \le 330$, hence only if
  \[
    n \le o \le \min(300,\, 330 - a) - s .
  \]
  The all-Sapling bound is therefore $\min(2109, 300) = 300$ outputs, fewer
  with spends or Actions, and the bound of~(i) never binds; one
  transaction carries at most $330$ units of the shared budget.
\item \emph{Orchard pool.} No payment output lies in the Orchard pool
  (Construction~\ref{def:route}, Proposition~\ref{prop:route-change}), so
  the Orchard-pool Actions of the transaction only consume the shared
  budget of~(ii).
\item \emph{Ironwood pool.} An Action creates one note, so a transaction
  that pays $n$ payments by Ironwood-pool outputs has $a_{\mathsf{I}} \ge
  n$ Ironwood-pool Actions; with $a_{\mathsf{O}}$ Orchard-pool Actions, $s$
  Sapling spends and $o$ Sapling outputs it is mined, by~(ii), only if
  \[
    n \le a_{\mathsf{I}} \le 330 - a_{\mathsf{O}} - s - o .
  \]
  An Action contributes at least $820 + 2272 + 64 = 3156$ bytes to a
  transaction: its description, its share of the proof and its spend
  authorisation signature (ZIP 229, ``Transaction Format''). The size limit
  alone admits $\lfloor 2{,}000{,}000 / 3156 \rfloor = 633$ Actions, so the
  shared budget binds, at $330$ payments by Ironwood-pool outputs.
\item \emph{Transparent outputs.} ZIP 218 does not apply its limits to the
  transparent components of transactions (same section), so payments by
  transparent outputs are bounded by the size limit alone.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{From request to plan.} Each payment $P_i = (a, v, m)$ of an accepted
request, its value fixed by the payer if absent, is a payment in the sense
of Definition~\ref{def:plan-payment} and becomes one output of the plan:
its pool is that of Construction~\ref{def:route}, a TEX address is paid by
Construction~\ref{def:tex-transfer}, and its memo, if any, is the memo
field of that output's note plaintext (Construction~\ref{def:zip321-memo}).
A TEX payment funded from shielded notes needs the two transactions of
Construction~\ref{def:tex-transfer}, so the MUST of ZIP 320, ``Design
considerations for Senders'', and not the SHOULD of ZIP 321 that asks for a
single transaction, fixes the shape of such a plan. From there construction
is that of \S\ref{sec:pipeline-plan} and Constructions~\ref{def:dummy-assembly}
and~\ref{def:authorise}: an Orchard-protocol output is sealed as in the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Encryption to the recipient'', a Sapling output as
in the protocol specification, \S``Sending Notes (Sapling)'', and the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures'', gives the
digest that every signature signs.

\end{document}
